# Transcription review — Theodore Roosevelt diaries, 1878–1883 (LOC microfilm)

Same conventions as the 1884 review. Records live in `transcription/volumes/<year>/<frame>_<L|R|S>.json`.

## 1878

335 pages with writing · 105 blocks flagged medium/low

- **429_0530_L** (1878-04-14) — low: Lines 4-5 heavily overlapped by show-through; readings uncertain.
- **429_0531_R** (1878-04-17) — low: Very heavy mirrored show-through overlaps lines 1-5; only fragments readable.
- **429_0531_R** (1878-04-17) — low: 
- **429_0539_R** (1878-05-03) — low: writing very blurred and overlaid by show-through; lower lines on page are mirrored offset and ignored
- **429_0543_R** (1878-05-09) — low: first line partly blotted/possibly struck; mixed with mirrored offset writing; cannot confirm it is a normal-reading TR line
- **429_0549_L** (1878-05-18) — low: continues from previous page (entry begun May 16); heavy show-through; a small word ("added"[?]) appears above line 4, possibly an interlineation or show-through
- **429_0550_L** (1878-05-20) — low: heavy show-through interleaved; several words uncertain
- **429_0568_R** (1878-06-24) — low: Heavy show-through interferes throughout; two lines in middle unreadable.
- **429_0570_L** (1878-06-27) — low: Last three lines blurred; reading of 'with' and word before 'Harry' uncertain.
- **429_0609_R** (1878-09-02) — low: Page very hard to read: heavy mirrored show-through interleaves the actual entry line by line, and several words appear struck or blotted. Many readings tentative.
- **429_0610_R** (1878-09-04) — low: Heavy mirrored show-through crosses nearly every line; name in line 1 and several words tentative.
- **429_0637_L** (1878-10-27) — low: Short line squeezed at foot of page; meaning unclear.

### 429_0479_R · JANUARY, TUESDAY 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0479/686,0,729,1157/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
New York.
Made about 20 calls.
A particularly
pleasant one on
Annie Murray;
she is a singular-
-ly neat girl, and
one whom I wish
I could see. Little
Fan[?] Smith is also
a great favourite
of mine—a pure,
religious, bright
girl, well read
and I think a
true friend. Edith
C. makes up my
trio of "Freundinnen".
```
> 'Fan' could also be read 'Fal'

### 429_0480_L · JANUARY, WEDNESDAY 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0480/0,0,696,1181/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The vacation has
now come to a
happy end, al-
-though it opened
so gloomily; but
I wish my
dearest Father were
better. Today he
told me I had
never caused him
a moments pain;
I should be less
than human if
I ever had, for he
is the best, wisest and
most loving of men,
the type of all that is
noble, brave, wise & good.
```

### 429_0480_R · JANUARY, THURSDAY 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0480/696,0,711,1181/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Cambridge.
I have returned
to my work today.
```

### 429_0481_L · JANUARY, FRIDAY 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0481/0,0,683,1157/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have naturally
been thinking a
good deal of
home, especially
of Father and of
my darling mother
and sweet sisters.
Elliott is a noble fellow,
wonderfully grown
up in every way.
I suppose, after him,
Jack Elliott is my
best friend, one who
would do anything
for me. But West[?] is
a very sweet fellow,
and I am very fond
of him. Alfred I
```
> 'anything' is underlined. 'West' reading uncertain (initial letter ambiguous). Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0481_R · JANUARY, SATURDAY 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0481/683,0,708,1157/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
like extremely; and
so I do Em, but
he grates on me
a great deal, and
is too selfish or ego-
-tistical, I scarcely
know which to
call it.
Of my college friends
I should say, at
present, that Hal
Minot, Will Blodgett,
Minot Weld and
Harry Chapin, were
likely to become
friends and very
possibly George
Pellew, Arthur Hooper
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0482/0,0,707,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*

```
and Charley Wash-
-burn. The other
boys are very good
as acquaintances;
Jack Tebbetts (and

little more.
```

*1878-01-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took up my
Sunday School
class as usual.
```

### 429_0482_R · JANUARY, MONDAY 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0482/707,0,716,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Studies now fairly
under way again.
I think I shall
do well in all
except French 4, and
very well in the
Natural History
courses.
I am boxing with
my "Tutor" five
times a week; I am
going to try hard
for the light
weight cups in
boxing and
wrestling.
```

### 429_0483_L · JANUARY, TUESDAY 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0483/0,0,690,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The Natural History
Professors are very
pleasant and
obliging.
```

```
-siz. Very pleasant
dinner.
```

### 429_0484_L · JANUARY, THURSDAY 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0484/0,0,694,1125/full/0/default.jpg

```
and remarkably

but very dirty.
```

### 429_0485_L · JANUARY, SATURDAY 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0485/0,0,680,1141/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-01-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dancing class in
the evening. Some
very sweet girls
in Boston—as the[?]
Carrie Goodwin,
Bessie Whitney
and the two
Miss Roaches. Also
Pauline Revere
Spent the night
with Arthur
Hooper. (Not com-
-ing back till
Monday).
```

```
evening; Miss

reading aloud.
```

```
Dancing class

```

### 429_0498_R · February, Tuesday 12. 1878.

*1878-02-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
He has just been
buried. I shall
never forget these
terrible three days;
the hideous suspense
of the ride on;
the dull, inert
sorrow, during
which I felt as
if I had been
stunned, or as if
part of my life
had been taken
away; and the
two moments
of sharp, bitter
agony, when
I kissed the
```
> Entry continues on Feb 13.

```
dear, dead face

He looked so

I feel[?] that if it
```

```
were not for the
certainty, that
as he himself has
so often said, "he
is not dead but
gone before," I
should almost
perish. With the
help of my
God I will
try to lead such
a life as he
would have
wished.
```

### 429_0500_L · February, Friday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0500/0,0,689,1197/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-02-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
He was the most
wise and loving
father that ever
lived; I owe
everything to him.
It is terrible to
think I have
never done any-
thing for him,
not even during
his sickness. I
never had an
unkind word
from him; though
I was always
promptly punish-
-ed if I did
wrong. For the
```
> 'punish-' shows an overwritten first letter. Entry continues on Feb 16.

### 429_0500_R · February, Saturday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0500/689,0,718,1197/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-02-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
last five years
he has scarcely
spoken to me
reprovingly. He
was so unselfish,
and was so
continually mak-
-ing others happy
that he was
always happy
himself; his 46
years of life were,
excepting the
last two months,
very happy ones.
```
> Continues from previous page (entry begun Feb 15), written under the Feb 16 heading.

### 429_0501_L · February, Sunday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0501/0,0,697,1186/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-02-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
He was as wise and
good in public as
in private life. He
was a great
personal worker
among the poor;
some of his old
"patients" whom
I visited have
been very touching
about him.
By the way,
it is very
pleasant to see
my old Mission
scholars again.
```
> 'By the way,' begins an indented new paragraph.

### 429_0501_R · February, Monday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0501/697,0,760,1186/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-02-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The family have
all been simply
wonderful during
his illness, espec-
ially Elliott and
Bamie. It was
no more than their
duty towards such
a man and such
a Father, but
they have nobly
done it
```

### 429_0503_L · February, Tuesday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0503/0,0,725,1215/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-02-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It seems impos-
-sible to realize
I shall never see
him again; he
is such a living
memory.
Truly "he was
eyes to the blind,
feet to the lame,
and a father to
the poor."
```
> 'Truly' begins an indented new paragraph.

### 429_0503_R · February, Wednesday 20. 1878.

*1878-02-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Every one has
been very kind.
I never shall
forget Uncle
Jimmie Gracie's
sweet sympathy
at the two times
when I most
needed it.
```

```
It is much worse
for Mother and
the girls than
for me; but it

for us all.
```

*1878-03-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had the first
meeting of the
glass ball club. I
broke 11 out of 25.
It seems brutal
to go about my
ordinary occupa-
-tions, but I must
keep employed.
I can not bear
to mix with
the fellows yet.
```

### 429_0510_R · March, Wednesday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0510/719,0,786,1182/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-03-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Every now & then
there are very
bitter moments;
if I had very
much time to
think I believe
I should almost
go crazy. But I
think I can
really and humbly
say "Thy will
be done".
```

### 429_0511_L · March, Thursday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0511/0,0,721,1189/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-03-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Looking back on
his life, it
seems as if mine
must be such
a weak useless
one in comparison.
I should like to
be a scientist; oh,
how I shall
miss his sweet,
sympathetic
advice!
```
> 'his' in line 2 is underlined. 'I should like to' begins a new paragraph after a gap.

```
The light weight
wrestling occurred

-member how

as if it was
years ago.
It is just one

```

```
ing over my

heart would
break; he shared
```

```
good but frightfully
```

```
stupid; Blennerhasset
```

### 429_0532_L · April, Thursday 18. 1878.

*1878-04-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have just come
across a lovely
hymn which
little Pussie has
copied for me;-

"Come unto me while
shadows darkly gather,
When the sad heart is
weary & distressed,
Seeking for comfort from
your heavenly father,
Come unto me and I
will give thee rest.

Ye who have mourned when
the spring flowers were taken
When the ripe fruit fell
richly to the ground.
```

```
In the afternoon I
took the night
boat for Boston.
Darling, beloved
Motherling came
down and saw me
off.
On the boat I
felt awfully home-
-sick; and, oh! so
heartsick for
Father.
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0558/0,0,755,1193/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
All my Bostonian
friends have been
very hospitable
and kind; and
by so doing have
added wonderfully
to the enjoyment
of my college
course. Jack
Tebbets[?] has just
asked me to
spend a month
with him at
Schooner Head and
Harry ^Shaw^ wishes me
to stay with him
next Sunday.
Our Club Table
```
> Stray mark after 'hospitable'. Entry continues on next page.

*1878-06-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
having temporarily
broken up I am
now at Memorial
Hall with
hap-hazard little
Gorham Peters.
```
> continues from previous page (June 3 entry)

*1878-06-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The weather has
~~there~~ become much
warmer and hard
to study in; but
my three remaining
examinations need
very little preparation.
```

### 429_0559_L · June, Wednesday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0559/0,0,752,1235/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Annual in Botany.
In evening left
Cambridge for
New York
```

### 429_0559_R · June, Thursday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0559/752,0,755,1235/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At Oyster Bay L. I.
Arrived about
mid-day.
Coming back
here has recalled
Father very vividly
and very sadly
to me. Every
nook and corner
about the place,
every piece of
furniture about
the house is
in some manner
connected with
him. It is impossible
to tell in words
how terribly I miss him.
```

### 429_0560_L · June, Friday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0560/0,0,759,1219/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the morning
in Hults[?] Woods;
shot Parula americana
& Coturniculus passerinus.
Had a splendid
ride on Lightfoot.
Annie Murray has
come to spend a
few days with
Conie.
Aunt Annie
& Uncle Jimmie
are at Oyster Bay.
O, Father, Father
how bitterly I
miss you, mourn you
and long for you!
```
> Woods name uncertain. Possible dot after 'bitterly'.

### 429_0560_R · June, Saturday 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0560/759,0,775,1219/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the morning
in sandpapering
and varnishing
my boat.
All the family
are wonderfully
lovely to me; but
I wish Mother
and Bamie would
not quarrel among
themselves
~~It is~~ There
comes a dull pain
at my heart
whenever I think
of the dear lost one.
```
> 'It is' appears struck through before 'There'.

### 429_0561_L · June, Sunday 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0561/0,0,779,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
/Four months ago/
All through the
sermon I was
thinking of Father.
I could see him
sitting in the
corner of the pew
as distinctly as
if he were alive,
in the same
dear old attitude,
with his funny
little "warlike curl",
and his beloved
face. Oh, I feel
so sad when I
think of the word 'never
```
> Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0561_R · June, Monday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0561/779,0,770,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Made a long
tramp in the
morning, rising
with the sun
and coming
back at nine.
Then built
my boat house
for the rest
of the day.
```

### 429_0562_L · June, Tuesday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0562/0,0,764,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rainy, but I
divided my day
pretty equally
between my
horse, gun and
row-boat.
I am afraid,
if I should stay
long at home I
should get spoiled
and conceited; the
family pet me
up so.
```
> Does not visibly continue the 'never' sentence from June 9.

### 429_0562_R · June, Wednesday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0562/764,0,760,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rode with Annie
Murray to Syosset
and down Laurel
Hollow; and took
her out in the
row boat. She is
a singularly
sweet and lady
like girl; and
very pretty and
clever. She
goes away
to-morrow
```

### 429_0563_L · June, Thursday 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0563/0,0,780,1214/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rowed over to
Pine Island where
I shot Dendroica
pina[?]. Had my
first swim.
This is the
first summer
that all the
Roosevelt boys
(except myself)
have been in
business.
```
> Species epithet reads like 'pina'; unclear.

### 429_0563_R · June, Friday 14. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0563/780,0,764,1214/full/0/default.jpg

```
~~Spent the day~~
~~in building~~
~~my boat house.~~
Fanny Smith
arrived ~~in the~~ ^yesterday^
afternoon, and
West came over
to tea. Poor fel-
-low! he is really
deeply in love
with her and
has been so for
three years.
I rowed her over
to Cooper's Bluff,
down which we
ran like chil-
-dren.
```

### 429_0564_L · June, Saturday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0564/0,0,798,1220/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Fanny, Conie, West
& myself went
on a picnic to
Yellow Banks.
In the afternoon
I took Fanny
^out for^ ~~on~~ a long ride;
we had a very
pleasant ride.
I like her
very much.
Aunt Annie and
Uncle Jimmie have
come to spend some
weeks with us.
```
> 'out for' written in left margin over/above a struck 'on' (uncertain).

### 429_0564_R · June, Sunday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0564/798,0,734,1220/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Church in the
morning.
In the afternoon
went back to
Harvard (via
New York) to
pass my two
remaining ex-
-aminations.
I took tea at
6 W-57th St. The
house was very
gloomy and
desolate, and
thinking of
Father made
me feel so sad
and lonely.
```
> '6' sits in left margin. 'via' may carry an accent.

### 429_0565_L · June, Monday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0565/0,0,777,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Semi-annual in
Botany.
O, I made a
miserable failure
in my crack
examination,
three weeks ago,
owing to being
forced to sit
up all the previous
night with the
asthma. It was
terribly hard
luck; thank
heavens I am
not of a nature
to take things
hardly.
```

### 429_0565_R · June, Tuesday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0565/777,0,769,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Annual in German
5.
Funnily enough
I was elected
into the A. D.
club — a totally
unexpected
social success.
So ends my
second year at
Harvard; and
a most pleasant,
and, I think, useful
year it has been.
I took the night
boat to New
York.
```
> 'ends' written over another word.

### 429_0566_L · June, Wednesday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0566/0,0,771,1189/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Oyster Bay L. I.
Came out here
in the afternoon;
in the morning
I visited poor
Cattie[?].
I owe everything
I have and[?] am
to Father; he
did everything
for me, and I
nothing for him.
I remember so well
how, years ago, when
I was a very weak,
asthmatic child,
he used to walk[?]
up and down
```
> Ink blot obscures word between 'have' and 'am'. Name 'Cattie' uncertain. Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0566_R · June, Thursday 20. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0566/771,0,765,1189/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
with me in his
arms [illegible] for hours
together, night
after night; and
oh how my
heart pains me
when I think
that I never was
able to do anything
for him during
his last illness!
```
> continues from previous page (June 19 entry); struck/blotted short word after 'arms'.

*1878-06-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Made hideous
scores with the rifle
in the morning;
rowed to Plain
Point, and had
a horseback ride.
```

### 429_0567_L · June, Friday 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0567/0,0,745,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Am leading the
most intensely happy
& healthy, out of doors
life. I spending
my time riding
on horseback,
making long
tramps through
woods and fields
after specimens,
or else on the
bay, rowing or
sailing — generally
in a half naked
condition and
with my gun
along. I could
not be happier,
```
> 'I spending' as read (possible missing/abbreviated word). Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0567_R · June, Saturday 22. 1878.

```
~~Saturday 22.~~
```

*1878-06-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
except at those
bitter ^moments^ when I
realize what I
have lost. Father
was himself so
invariably cheerful
that I feel it
would be wrong
for me to be gloomy,
and besides, fortunately
or unfortunately, I
am of a very
buoyant temper — being
a bit of an optimist.
Had a glorious
20-mile ride on
Lightfoot; canter-
-ing the whole time.
```

```
Saturday 22
```

```
In the morning
rowed to Plum
Point & back. Then
```

```
Rowed to Pine
Island where I
found a chicka-
-dee's nest.
Took Miss Nellie
Beekman out
riding in after-

My face is
beginning to
look like
an [illegible: 2 words]
[illegible: 2 words]
```

```
Rowed round
Centre Island, shoot=
-ing on the way, in
2 hours. there was
quite a sea running
and I shipped a
good deal of
water.
Had a long
ride; I am very
fond of galloping

Long Island roads,
and there are some
beautiful pines around

```

```
2 sandpipers
```

### 429_0569_R · June, Wednesday 26. 1878.

*1878-06-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rode in the morning
to Brookville &
Glencove. Walked
in Hults[?] Woods
with Chuffie. Drove
Pussie to Cold-
-spring to pick
waterlilies. Rowed
to the marsh.
Sailed in evening
with Chuffie, tak-
-ing our dinner
on board the
boat.
```
