```
New London.
Came on here
to see the race.
Am staying,
in company
with Minot
Weld and
Jackson with[?]
at [illegible] Harry
Chapins.
```
> Last three lines blurred; reading of 'with' and word before 'Harry' uncertain.

### 429_0570_R · June, Friday 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0570/780,0,777,1178/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Harvard won
easily.
Am perfectly
hoarse from
shouting.
In evening I
took night boat
for New York
```
> Small 'x' mark after 'evening'.

### 429_0571_L · June, Saturday 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0571/0,0,952,1205/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Oyster Bay.
Came out here
in the afternoon.
Bought a
Sharps rifle.
Harvard won
the base ball
championship.
```

### 429_0571_R · June, Sunday 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0571/952,0,624,1205/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-06-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a very good
sermon. I was
much struck with
one remark, that
Christianity gives
us, on this earth,
rest in trouble,
not from trouble.
Nothing but my
faith in the Lord
Jesus Christ could
have carried me
through this, my
terrible time of
trial and sorrow.
We passed the evening
largely in singing
hymns, as usual
```
> 'from' underlined. Line starts read from full frame.

### 429_0572_L · July, Monday 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0572/0,0,818,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Tried the new
rifle and made
67 out of 100; it
works very well,
but I am an
awful shot.
I am getting
to be deep ma-
-hogany colour
from the waist
up, thanks to
the rowing.
My horse went
a little lame
today.
The bathing is
perfectly delicious.
```
> 'am' underlined.

### 429_0572_R · July, Tuesday 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0572/818,0,768,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Tried the rifle; 71
out of 100.
Went in town
in the afternoon
to see off Annie
Murray, who sails
for Europe tomorrow.
Paid her a very
pleasant visit
in the evening
```
> Score '71' slightly uncertain.

### 429_0573_L · July, Wednesday 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0573/0,0,793,1229/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 7 am was down
on the Russia to
see Annie Murray,
whom I found
very sweet and
pretty and a little
wee bit out of
temper.
Took Aunt Susie
out to Oyster Bay
Elliott and I
in his 21 foot sail-
-boat the Daphné,
started out at 9.30^p.m^,
with a light but
steady southerly wind,
and cast anchor in
Rye Harbour at 1.30^a.m^
```
> Accent-like mark over final e of 'Daphne'. Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0573_R · July, Thursday 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0573/793,0,767,1229/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the morning
at Mrs. Bulkleys, where
we took dinner.
At 4.15^p.m^ started back,
and did not make
our buoy till 11.00^p.m^
Enjoyed the sail
most thoroughly.
It reminded me
of the ^many^ cruises
Chuff and I
have taken to-
-gether formerly.
```

### 429_0574_L · July, Friday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0574/0,0,790,1221/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a long and
very sweet but
sad talk with
mother over our
beloved lost one.
Uncle Jimmie
Grace stayed up
from the city, and
we went out
sailing and
rowing together
```

### 429_0574_R · July, Saturday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0574/790,0,786,1221/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed around
Centre Island. 8 miles
Started 8.[?]1; Plum
Pt 9.15; ~~Pine~~ Centre
Island neck 10.00;
returned 10.33.
The portage took
14 minutes, and
the tide was
so low I had to
make a circuit
of half a mile.
Shot the rifle
with Alfred in
the afternoon,
beating him by
54 to 18.
```
> Middle digit of start time overwritten (3 or 5).

### 429_0575_L · July, Sunday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0575/0,0,789,1247/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have been feeling
very heart sick
for Father; when
singing tonight,
we none of us
could sing "Safe
in the arms of
Jesus"; we sang
it together on
that terrible Sun-
-day. I read to-
-day at prayers
one of his favour-
-ite chapters — Is. LV.
I can never thank
God sufficiently for
having given me such
a lovely, affectionate family.
```

### 429_0575_R · July, Monday 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0575/789,0,789,1247/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Beat West and
Emlen in a
rifle match —
57 to 48 & 49.
My horse now
goes very well
and is almost
entirely free
from lameness.
```

### 429_0576_L · July, Tuesday 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0576/0,0,815,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Five months ago
It seems to me
that I have
aged very much
since that
bitter day; now
I must rely on
myself in diffi-
culties, while be-
fore I had al-
-ways carried
everything to him.
The vividness with
which I can
recall his words
and actions is
sometimes really
startling.
```
> "Five months ago" is framed by two heavy drawn black bars (mourning marks) at left and right.

### 429_0576_R · July, Wednesday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0576/815,0,763,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed around
Loyd's Neck, por-
-taging the isth-
-mus, in which
operation I got
stuck in the
mud flats and
had hideous
work getting
out. Rowing home
there was a
strong south-
westerly wind
and I got was
caught in a
bad thunder-
squall. (15 miles
2 plover (& 2 gulls).
```
> "got was" as written; "got" may be overwritten or uncancelled.

### 429_0577_L · July, Thursday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0577/0,0,796,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out sailing
with West and
Alfred, taking
our lunch along.
Divided the
remainder of
the time pretty
equally between
my horse and
row boat.
I often feel bad-
-ly that such a
wonderful man
as Father should
have had a
son of so little
worth as I
am.
```

### 429_0577_R · July, Friday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0577/796,0,790,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rained all
day, and so,
for the first
time this sum-
-mer I had
to stay in
doors.
Of my five inti-
-mate friends, Cuff[?]
and John Elliott
over-; West and
Emlen under-
-estimate me; while
Alfred takes me
for about what
I am worth.
```
> First name in line 9 uncertain (Cuff/Cluff).

### 429_0578_L · July, Saturday 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0578/0,0,837,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Ran a mile
through the
woods at
speed; shall
probably repeat
this every day.
```
> A diagonal stroke across the initial R appears to be show-through, not a cancellation.

*1878-07-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
(14th)
the world, not through
lack of perseverance and
good intentions, but
through sheer inability.
I realize more and more
every day that I am as
much inferior to Father
morally and mentally
as physically. But "Trust in
the Lord and do good!".
```
> Continues the July 14 entry from the facing page (Sunday 14), marked "(14th)" and set off by a drawn rule and bracket.

### 429_0578_R · July, Sunday 14. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0578/837,0,765,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Communion
Sunday.
Was rather struck,
in reading Pascal,
by the following:-
"Deux excès: Exclure
la raison, n'admettre
que la raison". (Apol-
-ogie de la religion,
VIII, VII). Materialism
and superstition are
equally repugnant
to me.
During service today
I could not help re-
flecting sadly on how
little use I am, or
ever shall be in
```
> Entry continues on the lower half of the facing (Saturday 13) page, marked "(14th)". Apostrophe in "n'admettre" and the roman numerals of the citation are small and somewhat uncertain.

### 429_0579_L · July, Monday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0579/0,0,845,1231/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed round Centre
Island; high tide
and dead calm,
but hotter than
Tophet. Started
10.45; Pine Neck
11.20; (portage takes
5 minutes); Plum
Point 11.50; returned
12.13.
Had a long
gallop with
Emlen beyond
and around
Wheatley
```

### 429_0580_L · July, Wednesday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0580/0,0,805,1190/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
West and I started
on horseback for
Lake Ronkonkoma.
The day was cool
and cloudy. Started
8.00 a.m; reached
Smithtown 10.45 a.m,
took lunch, and in
afternoon rode to
the lake where we
took supper, and
returned to sleep
at Smithtown. In
all about 35 miles,
and the horse as
fresh as when he
started.
```

### 429_0580_R · July, Thursday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0580/805,0,773,1190/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started in the
morning for Oyster
Bay, which we
reached in two
hours and a half.
Very hot.
```

### 429_0581_L · July, Friday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0581/0,0,832,1182/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rowed to Rye Beach
and back. Perfectly
calm but very hot.
Started at[?] 5.15 ^a.m.^; Rocky
Point 6.10; Oak Bluff
6.40; Rye Beach 8.15.
Started back 4.45
p.m; Centre Island
buoy[?] 6.15; home 7.15.
In all I rowed
rather over 25 miles.
Saw a stormy petrel.
```
> Word before 5.15 is overwritten/blotted; "a.m." written small above. "buoy" spelled uncertainly.

### 429_0583_L · July, Sunday 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0583/0,0,797,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Lord, I believe;
help thou mine
unbelief!
```

### 429_0583_R · July, Monday 22. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0583/797,0,741,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
~~Ma~~ Took a long
tramp through
the woods and
fields with my
gun.
```
> First letters ("Ma"[?]) are scribbled over/cancelled; reading of the cancelled letters uncertain.

*1878-07-22 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 woodcock 2 doves
```

### 429_0584_L · July, Thursday 25. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0584/0,0,834,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went in town;
called on poor
Cassin[?]. In evening
came out here to
the Dickeys; I like
them ever so much,
they are so sweet
and homelike.
Honest old Charles
Dickey is coming
to Harvard next
year, and I shall
do the best I
can for him.
```
> Name in line 3 uncertain.

### 429_0584_R · July, Friday 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0584/834,0,768,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Returned to Oyster
Bay.
```

### 429_0585_L · July, Saturday 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0585/0,0,806,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rigged a ~~lug~~ ^leg of mutton^
sail, on a seven
foot mast, with a
six foot boom, in
my little out rigger,
lapstreak skiff, and
went out sailing.
She beats very
badly, but is stiff
and sails pretty
fast off the wind.
```
> "lug" cancelled with a stroke; "leg of mutton" written small above at upper right.

### 429_0586_L · July, Monday 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0586/0,0,819,1231/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rowed to Loyds Neck,
shooting through the
marsh. At high tide
it is good fun to
paddle through the
lagoons and among
the winding channels,
with an occasional
shot at a heron or
snipe. In the afternoon
a strong southerly breeze
came up, so I rigged
my sail and went
out three or four miles
into the sound. Cuff[?]
has left for a three weeks
holiday
```
> Name "Cuff"[?] uncertain (same name as July 12). "holiday" squeezed small at line start.

*1878-07-29 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 sand snipe.
```

### 429_0586_R · July, Tuesday 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0586/819,0,759,1231/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rainy, but I
strolled through
the woods with
my gun during
the morning, and
had a splendid
ride in the af-
-ternoon .
```

*1878-07-30 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 dove 1 wild pigeon
```

### 429_0587_L · July, Wednesday 31. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0587/0,0,799,1216/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-07-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed round
Centre Island in
Alfred's boat in
1 h. 24 m.
```

### 429_0588_L · August, Friday 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0588/0,0,925,1205/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rowed round
Loyd's Neck. Started
10 a.m ; : Point
11.15 ; Huntington
Harbour Lighthouse
12.15 ; Neck 1. p.m;
home 1.45. Portage
took but four
minutes, tide
being dead high.
Southerly wind.
```
> A gap with a small colon-like mark stands before "Point" in line 3, as if a name were left blank.

### 429_0588_R · August, Saturday 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0588/925,0,627,1205/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Emlen and I sail-
-ed over ^across^ the sound,
intending to go to
Greenwich but struck
the wrong side of
Shippan Point
and got into
Darien; we had
a fresh, full sail
breeze.
My horse has
done wonderfully
this season; I have
not missed riding
him a single day
when at home; his
back and legs have
both stood beautifully.
```
> "across" written small above "over"; "over" is not visibly cancelled. Line starts recovered from full frame.

### 429_0589_L · August, Sunday 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0589/0,0,804,1231/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I like very much the
text over the door
of our Church:- "Be
ye doers of the word
and not hearers
only". I am no
believer in faith
without works.
Have been reading
the beautiful "Memorial
of Theodore Roosevelt".
With God's help I
shall try to lead
such a life as
Father would have
wished me to.
```

### 429_0589_R · August, Monday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0589/804,0,734,1231/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out woodcock-
shooting, tramping
about twenty
five miles through
awful places, for
the cock are
only now found
in very thick
cover. I shot
wretchedly, mis-
-sing over half
my birds. As the
day was pretty
hot both I and
the dog are
pretty well done
up.
```
> Small marks above "only" and "now" appear to be letter corrections.

*1878-08-05 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
7 woodcock.
```

### 429_0590_L · August, Tuesday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0590/0,0,802,1224/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
It being Edith C.'s
17th birthday, I sent
her a bonbonierre[?].
```
> Spelling of the last word uncertain.

### 429_0590_R · August, Wednesday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0590/802,0,753,1224/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dear old Jack
Elliott arrived
today, to stay
five or six weeks
```
> A small mark (quotation mark or tick) precedes "Dear".

### 429_0591_L · August, Thursday 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0591/0,0,818,1227/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Jack Elliott and
I went in my
boat to Loyds
Neck; the wind was
three quarters aft,
so we rigged the
~~sail~~ ^mast^, so[?] with sail,
oars and paddle
managed to make
pretty good time.
We shot through
the lagoons, took
lunch, had tough
work getting out of
the marsh, as the tide
was ^dead^ low, rowed home
against a head wind.
```
> "sail" cancelled with "mast" above; "dead" inserted above "low"; a blotted/overwritten letter precedes "rowed".

*1878-08-08 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 yellowlegs 2 heron
```

### 429_0591_R · August, Friday 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0591/818,0,778,1227/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Jack and I went
out sailing; the
breeze was almost
a reefer, but we
kept all our
canvass up, and
had a glorious
time.
Oyster Bay is the
perfection of a
place for fellows;
I often wonder if
any one[?] could
have a happier
time than I am
having.
Six months ago - it
seems so much longer.
```
> Words in line 13 partly obscured by show-through.

*1878-08-09 · marginalia · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
[underline]
```
> Heavy ink line drawn under the printed "Friday 9." in the heading.

### 429_0592_L · August, Saturday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0592/0,0,816,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The Harvard rank-list
arrived today; my
marks for last year
were French IV, 51;
Themes 69; Nat. Hist
3, 79; Hist. 87;
Nat. Hist 8, 89; Germ IV,
92; Germ V, 96. My
average is about 85,
10 per cent better than
last year. I am well
satisfied; & my dearest
Father would have
been so pleased.
Funnily enough
I am rather laid
up by an attack
of cholera morbus.
```

*1878-08-10 · marginalia · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
[underline]
```
> Heavy ink line drawn under the printed "Saturday" in the heading.

### 429_0592_R · August, Sunday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0592/816,0,740,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
For ~~th~~ almost the
first time this year
I did not go to
church, thanks to
the cholera morbus.
As usual when
I have nothing
to do (a rare event,
thank fortune) I
have been think-
-ing a great
deal of Father.
```

### 429_0593_L · August, Monday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0593/0,0,815,1214/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Jack & I rowed to Plum
Point, paddled up the
marsh on this side of
it, portaged (for 200 yds
over awful ground, with
clouds of mosquitoes)
into the great marsh
on the north side of
Centre Island, shot through
it, rowed to the neck,
lunched, shot through
the pine woods &
sailed home.
Edith C. has written
me a very sweet little
note of thanks for
the bonbonierre[?].
```
> Spelling of the last word uncertain.

*1878-08-12 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 sand snipe.
```

### 429_0593_R · August, Tuesday 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0593/815,0,725,1214/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Jack and I rowed
to Loyd's Neck, shoot-
-ing through the
marsh at flood
tide and coming
out with the ebb.
A strong south
wind arose so we
did not get home
till quite late in
the afternoon.
```

*1878-08-13 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 yellowlegs 1 sand snipe
```

### 429_0594_R · August, Thursday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0594/828,0,727,1215/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went out woodcock
shooting, tramping
from 8 a.m. till
5 p.m. (9 consecutive
hours) through awful
ground, without
food & rest; going of
course at a slow
pace. I tried my
new knickerbockers
gaiters and hunting
shoes, which worked
to a charm. The
cover was fearful, so I
missed two thirds of my
shots; but had glorious sport,
& funnily enough, feel
fresh as a lark. 6 woodcock
```
> Lines 4-8 are partly obscured by show-through; "9 consecutive" and "food & rest" are the least certain readings. "6 woodcock" tally written at end of last line.

### 429_0595_L · August, Friday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0595/0,0,825,1212/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Edith C. has come to
spend two weeks with
Corinne.
Emlen, Jack &
I sailed over
to ~~[illegible]~~ the Con-
-necticut shore, &
coming home in the
afternoon got caught
in a bad rain
storm.
```
> A word before "the Con-" is heavily cancelled and unreadable. "has" after "Edith C." appears overwritten.

### 429_0596_R · AUGUST, MONDAY 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0596/857,0,742,1238/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Edith, Corinne
& Jack, Emlen and
I went out sailing
and took our
lunch at Yellow-
banks.
Am teaching
Jack to spar;
had a little
match with Em
and rather used
him up. He
beat me rifle
shooting.
```
> Line 2 '&' written run into 'Jack'.

### 429_0597_L · AUGUST, TUESDAY 20. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0597/0,0,813,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rowed Edith out
towards Loyds
Neck.
Sparred[?] with
Jack and West;
while out riding
with the latter I
was made the
recepient of confi-
-dences respecting
his devotion to the
much worshipped Fanny.
Poor old boy! It is
really touching to
see how much in
love he is.
```
> 'recepient' as written.

### 429_0597_R · AUGUST, WEDNESDAY 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0597/813,0,737,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
~~Took~~ ^Drove^ Edith to
Coldspring to pick
waterlilies, spend-
-ing a lovely
morning with
her.
In the afternoon
Nell arrived on
the Waterwitch
with Jack Roos-
-evelt, from his
three weeks cruise.
```
> 'Took' struck and 'Drove' written above.

### 429_0598_L · AUGUST, THURSDAY 22. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0598/0,0,802,1224/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Elliott, Jack & I took
Mother, Edith & Corinne
out sailing; in the
evening we all,
together with Uncle
Jimmie & Aunt Annie
and Alfred & Emlen,
took tea at Wests.
Afterwards Edith & I
went up to the
summer house.
My ride today was
so long and hard
that I am afraid
it may have injured
my horse.
```

### 429_0599_L · AUGUST, SATURDAY 24. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0599/0,0,826,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The other day a dog
annoyed me very
much while on
horseback, and I
told the owner I
should shoot it; which
threat I fulfilled
while out riding
with West today,
rolling it over with
my revolver very
neatly as it ran
alongside the horse.
```

### 429_0599_R · AUGUST, SUNDAY 25. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0599/826,0,751,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I like our custom
of having family
prayers in the
morning and evening
of Sunday very
much.
```

### 429_0600_L · AUGUST, MONDAY 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0600/0,0,791,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Early in the morning,
which was clear & cool,
I had a magnificent
twenty mile gallop
with Emlen.
Towards midday Emlen,
West, Jack & I
started off on the
Addie for a
short yachting
cruise; Emlen leaves
us early tomorrow
morning. The
wind was light
and we made
New Haven about
10 p.m.
```

### 429_0600_R · AUGUST, TUESDAY 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0600/791,0,698,1223/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The wind was very
light and we
sailed till ~~6~~
8 p.m before
reaching New
London. We
divided our time
between swimming,
reading the New
Symposium, clam-
-bering about the
rigging and
playing whist —
with a good deal
of sheer doing
nothing. It is
very lazy but very
pleasant.
```
> Line 3 ends with a blotted/overwritten character (possibly '6'), rendered as struck; uncertain.

### 429_0601_L · AUGUST, FRIDAY 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0601/0,0,810,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Edith Carow[?] came to
spend two weeks with
Corinne.
Emlen, Jack &
I sailed over
to [illegible] the Con-
-necticut shore, &
coming home in the
afternoon got caught
in a bad rain
storm.
```
> Second word of line 1 partly blotted; place name in line 6 unclear.

### 429_0607_L · AUGUST, WEDNESDAY 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0607/0,0,838,1216/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We sailed to Green-
-port, the wind
being light and
puffy. Having
several hundred
cartridges aboard
we practise with
our revolvers at
everything that
comes up from
bottles or buoys to
sharks and porpoises
I never slept
or ate better in
my life.
```

### 429_0607_R · AUGUST, THURSDAY 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0607/838,0,721,1216/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
There was a fair
steady breeze and
we got under weigh
early in the morning
and all day long
we sailed in a
straight course
by the low
& sandy bluffs
of the Long
Island coast;
I passed a good
of deal of time in
the cross trees, whist
and discussions about
materialism and politics
occupying the remain-
-der.
```
> '&' at start of line 9 appears written over another character. 'of' at start of line 13 is as it appears (stray/misplaced word).

### 429_0608_L · AUGUST, FRIDAY 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0608/0,0,850,1249/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We reached Oyster
Bay early in the
morning (1[?] a.m) &
came ashore for
breakfast. We have
most heartily
enjoyed the cruise.
In afternoon
Jack, Alfred &
I went out
sailing.
```
> Hour in line 3 uncertain (1 or 7). Words on lines 1–3 partly overlaid by show-through.

### 429_0608_R · AUGUST, SATURDAY 31. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0608/850,0,720,1249/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-08-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Jack & I went
out after sea coots
but did not see
one, though; we
rowed nearly
20 miles.
George Minot has
come to spend a
few days with us.
```

### 429_0609_L · SEPTEMBER, SUNDAY 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0609/0,0,833,1216/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I had a long talk
with Uncle Jim
about my future
life; I have absolute-
-ly no idea what
I shall do when
I leave college.
Oh, Father, my
Father no words
can tell how I
shall miss your
counsel and ad-
-vice!
However for the next
two years my duty is
clear - to study well
and live like a brave
Christian gentleman
```
> Last words of the final lines run to the gutter edge; any final punctuation not visible.

### 429_0609_R · SEPTEMBER, MONDAY 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0609/833,0,706,1216/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
Elliott & Alfred stayed
up for the [illegible].
There was a
strong, steady wester-
-ly wind, [illegible]
sailed over to Rye
and [illegible] back & Alfred
[illegible] I [illegible]
Mother, Elliott, Jack
& George
Minot [illegible]
[illegible] had
a [illegible] pleasant
[illegible]
Bulkleys, where
[illegible] Major[?] Lee[?]
Kitty[?].
```
> Page very hard to read: heavy mirrored show-through interleaves the actual entry line by line, and several words appear struck or blotted. Many readings tentative.

### 429_0610_L · SEPTEMBER, TUESDAY 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0610/0,0,862,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Jack & I went out in
our skiff, intending
to pass the night out;
but it rained in
torrents, so towards even-
-ing we came home. We
lunched at Matinnicock Pt,
and on our way back, while
off Oak Bluffs were struck
by a heavy squall from
the North West, which
soon kicked up a very
big sea; so we hoisted our
sail and drove with
the wind on our quarter, at
a furious speed, to Rocky Pt;
staunch though the little boat
is, we shipped a good deal of
water
```
> Final word 'water' squeezed below the last ruled line at right.

### 429_0610_R · SEPTEMBER, WEDNESDAY 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0610/862,0,712,1222/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
Poor, dear Harry[?] Minot[?]:
George M. has just told
me that he has [illegible]-
-[illegible] lost his mind
and is in an insane
asylum. What a ter-
-rible thing it is to
have insanity in a
family. [illegible] I
feel for him; he is
as sweet and gentle
a fellow as I have ever
seen, and very[?] bright;
and a true friend to
me. [illegible] I sorrow for
his family.
Uncle Will[?] and Aunt
Susie have come to see[?] us.
```
> Heavy mirrored show-through crosses nearly every line; name in line 1 and several words tentative.

### 429_0611_L · SEPTEMBER, THURSDAY 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0611/0,0,631,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went in to New
York, where I pass
the night. Call[?] on
poor Cousin [illegible]; he is
dying by inches.
Cousin Sam Roose-
-velt is dead; it
is terrible for his
poor daughters. I
have been feeling
very sad about
dear old Hal
Minot.
In the evening Jack
Roosevelt, Emlen & I
had a very pleasant
dinner at the Union
League Club.
```
> Heavy show-through on this page; transcribed from the full frame because the half is cut off.

### 429_0611_R · SEPTEMBER, FRIDAY 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0611/631,0,924,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
West, Emlen & my-
-self took the 8.35[?]
train for Boston
en route for the
Maine woods
where we intend
to pass three
weeks. I can hard-
-ly realize (about
old Hal Minot;
but Father's death
has taken away much
of my capacity for
grieving.
```

### 429_0612_L · SEPTEMBER, SATURDAY 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0612/0,0,842,1237/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We travelled all
last night, reaching
Matawaumkeag at
10.30, and from
there took ~~the~~ a
buckboard and
reached here
(~~Highland~~ Island
Falls) at 8. p.m.
Whenever I think
about poor old
Hal Minot I feel
very sad; he was
such a loveable man.
Have a pretty bad
attack of asthma; I
miss riding my horse; he
has not been unwell a
day this summer.
```
> 'Highland' appears struck through; a caret mark stands before '8.' with nothing visible above it.

### 429_0612_R · SEPTEMBER, SUNDAY 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0612/842,0,712,1237/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning took
a walk & read the
bible, learning by heart
the XIX Psalm — a most
beautiful hymn of
praise. There is no church
here, but in the afternoon
I went to a Methodist
meeting in the school
house. Thinking about
poor Hal Minot has a-
-gain made me think
much and oh so
sadly of Father's death;
I try whenever I can
only to recall his
grand and beautiful
life. Oh, Father, Father!
```

*1878-09-08 · marginalia · top · ink · hand uncertain · medium*
```
```
> Heavy ink underline beneath printed 'SUNDAY' in the date heading (no words).

### 429_0613_L · SEPTEMBER, MONDAY 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0613/0,0,842,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day
hunting partridges
but did not even
see one
```

*1878-09-09 · marginalia · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
seven months ago.
```
> Written just below the printed heading, which is underlined in heavy ink beneath 'MONDAY 9.'

### 429_0613_R · SEPTEMBER, TUESDAY 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0613/842,0,707,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
West, Emlen, Will T., &
one guide (Sewell), &
Fox[?] in a batteau
started down stream to
a large lake ^(Mattawamkeag[?])^ at whose
foot we are camping
out; I preceeded them
in a canoe with
another guide (Dow)
& had good fun pad-
-dling, and tramp-
-ing through the
woods, but though
I had several shots
at pigeons & ducks
I missed them all.
```
> Line 2 'one' has a blotted or struck letter before it. Small insertion above 'lake' in tiny script. 'preceeded' as written.

*1878-09-10 · marginalia · top · ink · hand uncertain · medium*
```
```
> Dotted/dashed ink underline beneath printed 'TUESDAY 10.' in the heading (no words).

### 429_0614_L · SEPTEMBER, WEDNESDAY 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0614/0,0,832,1226/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the morning Emlen
and I paddled about
Mattawomkeag Lake in
the canoe; I had two
or three shots with the
rifle at a loon which
was over a hundred
yards off, and came
pretty close to him.
In the afternoon Em &
I tramped about ten
miles through the woods
after partridges; we saw
~~but~~ ^only^ one bird but enjoyed
the walk very much; he
and I are great companions
and he is a true friend.
1 partridge. (ruffed grouse).
```
> Word at start of line 14 appears struck/overwritten with 'only' above; struck word reading uncertain.

### 429_0614_R · SEPTEMBER, THURSDAY 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0614/832,0,694,1226/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rainy, so we stayed in
camp, reading, playing
whist and cleaning
our guns. We have
quite a varied diet
— duck, partridge, trout
pickerel &c.
In the afternoon Em
and I watched (from
behind some bushes)
the river, in hopes of
some ducks passing
by; but after sitting
about three hours in
the rain we returned
with nothing.
```

### 429_0615_L · SEPTEMBER, FRIDAY 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0615/0,0,845,1218/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained all day so
we stayed in camp
- reading, playing
euchre and whist
and practising with
our rifles. I again
waited a couple of
hours for ducks,
getting one long
and unsuccessful
shot. I killed
a bat and a
nighthawk.
```

### 429_0615_R · SEPTEMBER, SATURDAY 14. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0615/845,0,684,1218/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained till noon;
then it cleared and
I took a fifteen
mile tramp through
the woods with
Sewell, but got noth-
-ing. I had two shots
with the rifle at
a couple of foxes a-
-bout sixty yards
off across the river,
but missed both;
I don't think I ever
made as many con-
-secutive bad shots
as I have this week.
I am disgusted with
myself.
```

### 429_0616_L · September, Sunday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0616/0,0,856,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Read my bible in the
morning; in the
afternoon, for lack
of better occupation,
cleaned guns, labelled
specimens and
ma[?] bathed in the
race of the damn.
```
> 'ma' unclear

### 429_0616_R · September, Monday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0616/856,0,673,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There was frost last
night, and today I
took a thirty mile
tramp through the
woods with Sewell,
keeping near the
Mattaumkeag[?] river
most of the time.
Except half an hour for
lunch we were on the
go steadily from 8 a.m
till 7 p.m. I got but
four shots and saw
no game whatever
during the last 20 miles,
but enjoyed the walk greatly.
```

*1878-09-16 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 red rabbit 1 merganser
2 ruffed grouse
```

### 429_0617_L · September, Tuesday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0617/0,0,836,1238/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
West and I, with
Dow, took the
canoe and paddled
to the upper
end of the lake
and shot through
the slews and
bogs - with very
indifferent suc-
cess. We three
camped out in
a wigwam near
the foo[?] head of
the lake, taking
a very frugal
supper.
```
> 'foo' may be an uncorrected false start

*1878-09-17 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 ~~ruddy[?]~~ ^dipper^ duck, 1 wood
duck
```

### 429_0617_R · September, Wednesday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0617/836,0,705,1238/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The three of us got
up at four in the
morning, and
started after ducks
before daylight,
but saw nothing.
When we got back
to the old camp
I took Will Dow
and tramped
about ten miles
through the woods,
getting four shots.
I use[?] like moc-
casins to walk
through the woods.
with very much
```
> second word of 'I use[?] like' unclear; last line 'with very much' as written

*1878-09-18 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 merganser 3 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0618_L · September, Thursday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0618/0,0,846,1250/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked about fif-
teen miles through
the woods; about
three miles were
through a succession
of alder swamps
and cedar swamps,
where the walking
was about as
difficult as any
I have ever been
through. I killed
a spruce partridge
in a cedar swamp
and a birch part-
ridge on a hemlock
ridge.
```

*1878-09-19 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 canada grouse, 1 ruffed grouse
```

### 429_0618_R · September, Friday 20. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0618/846,0,703,1250/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took a ten mile
tramp with Sewell;
I saw nothing
in the woods
but after a
rather neat
stalk killed a
duck in a
small lake.
Was nicely sold
by the boys with
a dead duck
placed in a life
like attitude in a
pond. Have had
beautiful baths in the
dam[?]race every day.
```
> 'damrace' written with an extra stroke between dam and race

*1878-09-20 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 ruddy (?) duck.
```
> '(?)' appears to be TR's own

### 429_0619_L · September, Saturday 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0619/0,0,849,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Emlen and I
went out shoot-
ing in the
afternoon, but he
never fired of his
gun; I had two
shots, missing a
duck and killing
a partridge. The
leaves are now
beginning to turn
and the woods ^are^
perfectly beautiful.
I have enjoyed this
week very much; the
trip so far has been
a great success.
```

*1878-09-21 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0619_R · September, Sunday 22. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0619/849,0,700,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day
much as last
Sunday.
```

### 429_0620_L · September, Monday 23. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0620/0,0,866,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Broke camp and
started for Island
Falls in the batteau
- a clumsy looking
craft. Sewell paddled,
Dow and I rowed,
Thomson trolled, and
West and Emlen,
with rifle and
shot gun, watched
from the bow for
loons and ducks.
We made very good
time; I spent the
afternoon tramping
through the woods
with West.
```

*1878-09-23 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0620_R · September, Tuesday 24. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0620/866,0,711,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took a twenty five
mile tramp through
the woods with
Dow, shooting a
logcock and two
partridges. I have
had wonderfully
bad luck ~~in~~[?] ^as regards^
shooting, finding
very little game;
but nevertheless have
enjoyed the trip
greatly. There has
been absolutely no
disagreement between
any members of the
party.
```
> word under 'as regards' appears struck, possibly 'in'

*1878-09-24 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0621_L · September, Wednesday 25. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0621/0,0,845,1228/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day
driving around in
a buckboard with
Will Dow, getting
out whenever we
saw a partridge-
- which occurred
five times. I saw
a fox
```

*1878-09-25 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
5 ruffed grouse
```

### 429_0621_R · September, Thursday 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0621/845,0,701,1228/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove in a wagon
to Mattawomkeag[?]
where we took the
night train for
Boston; tomorrow the
other three fellows
go on to New York,
while I go out to
Harvard.
```

### 429_0622_L · September, Friday 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0622/0,0,842,1226/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
All the fellows
greeted me with
enthusiasm. To
my great sur-
prise I was offered
the Porcellian; of
course I could not
accept, being a
member of the
A.D - but I wish
I could. It is very
pleasant to get back
among all the
fellows again; but
I can't help feeling
a little bit home
sick.
```

### 429_0622_R · September, Saturday 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0622/842,0,708,1226/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I find that my
recitations, lectures
and labratory
work will occupy
about 20 hours a
week. I have a
recitation every
day, including
Saturday, at 9 a.m.
Harry Shaw and
Dick Saltonstall
both asked me to
spend tomorrow
with them. Our
club table is at
Mrs Wilsons, where the
food, service & atten-
dance are excellent.
```

### 429_0623_L · September, Sunday 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0623/0,0,862,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to Church
as usual; I shall
not take up my
sunday school till
next sunday or
the one after.
```

### 429_0623_R · September, Monday 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0623/862,0,688,1241/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-09-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have begun study-
ing regularly. I
joined the rifle
club; I am going
to practice pretty
regularly with
the rifle this
year.
```

### 429_0624_L · October, Tuesday 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0624/0,0,860,1248/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Was elected into
the Glee club.
```

### 429_0624_R · October, Wednesday 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0624/860,0,689,1248/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went up to the
first meeting of
the Δ.K.E; I get
rather tired of
seeing such a
drunken club as
a regular thing,
but once in a
while it is good
enough fun to
go up there - though
I never drink
anything up there.
I shall not begin
smoking till I
am twenty one;
as it is, I drink
very little.
```

### 429_0625_L · October, Thursday 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0625/0,0,848,1238/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Attended the
first meeting of
the Art Club, and
found it very
pleasant.
```

### 429_0625_R · October, Friday 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0625/848,0,689,1238/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Of my studies
this year I like
best the philosophy
and political econo-
my; my natural
history courses are
also very Interesting,
though less so than
last year; for the
others I do not
care so much.
```
> capital I in 'Interesting' as it appears

### 429_0626_L · October, Saturday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0626/0,0,858,1287/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the morning
at the rifle range
```

### 429_0626_R · October, Sunday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0626/858,0,670,1287/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Communion Sunday.
```

### 429_0627_L · October, Monday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0627/0,0,848,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am working
pretty hard now;
but as I have optional
recitations, thanks
to my high average
last year, I expect
to get about three
weeks holidays during
the course of the
winter.
```

### 429_0627_R · October, Tuesday 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0627/848,0,695,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started with my
boxing teacher
again.
```

### 429_0628_L · October, Wednesday 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0628/0,0,863,1253/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Last night a Porcellian
and an A.D. man, both
drunk, were discussing
me, and in the quarrel
the former let it leak
out that I preferred
his club. The A.D men
held a meeting and
voted to give me my
choice of the two
clubs - as I had never
been in the A.D rooms,
had not signed the
constitution, possessed
no voting powers and
in fact was not
a member. Of course
by this arrangement
```
> sentence breaks off at foot of page; not continued on next page (Oct. 10)

### 429_0628_R · October, Thursday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0628/863,0,679,1253/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I hate to hurt some
body's feelings, and
I have rarely felt
as badly as I have
during the last 24
hours; it is terribly
hard to know
what is the hon
orable thing to do.
```
> 'hate' underlined

### 429_0629_L · October, Friday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0629/0,0,841,1236/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Have finally decided
to go into the
Porcellian; my classmates
who are in it are
Minot Weld, Harry
Shaw, Dick Trimble,
Charley Morgan and
George Griswold.
The A.D men from our
class are Bob Bacon,
Billy Hooper, Billy
Blodgett, Charley Ware,
Harry Jackson and
Freddy Allen &
Johnny Samson.
```
> 'A.D' may be written with a delta-like A; final '&' uncertain

### 429_0629_R · October, Saturday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0629/841,0,703,1236/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Arthur Hooper has
left college; Brooks
and Peters are dropped
Charley Ware is
a capital fellow
and a thoroughly
good one too; I have
grown to like him
very much. The same
is true of swell old
Harry Shaw.
Harry Chapin is
also a perfect
trump.
```

### 429_0630_L · October, Sunday 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0630/0,0,840,1243/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It makes it very
homelike to have
old Charley Dickey
in the same house
with me. I think
I have been able to
do a good deal
for him.
```

### 429_0630_R · October, Monday 14. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0630/840,0,700,1243/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
One of my Natural
History courses this
year needs a great
deal of labratory[?]
work, which I like
but which takes
up an awful
amount of time.
```

### 429_0631_L · October, Tuesday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0631/0,0,855,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have been sparring
with several of the
fellows.
The A.D men have
behaved like
trumps.
```

### 429_0631_R · October, Wednesday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0631/855,0,671,1232/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Our table is composed
of Minot Weld, Harry
Chapin, Charley
Ware, Charley Washburn,
Ralph Ellis, Gorham
Peters & Dick
Saltonstall.
```

### 429_0632_L · October, Thursday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0632/0,0,853,1264/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a very pleasant
dinner at the
Hoopers; I was very
glad to see Miss
Jeannie again.
```
> 'pleasant' runs to page edge; 'Miss' written with long-s ligature

### 429_0632_R · October, Friday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0632/853,0,679,1264/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sparred with
Tucker Burr, using
him up.
In the afternoon
Dick Saltonstall
drove me in a
buggy over to his
house on Chestnut
Hill, where I am
now, enjoying
myself to the ut-
most. All his
family are just
too sweet to me
for anything, and
the whole house is
so homelike.
```

### 429_0633_L · October, Saturday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0633/0,0,867,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I spent the morning
walking through the
woods with Dick, his
sister Miss Rose, and
their cousin, Miss
Alice Lee - a very
sweet pretty girl.
About midday we
drove over to the
Whitneys where we
took dinner and tea.
We spent the evening
dancing and sing-
ing, driving back
about 11 oclock.
```
> 'Miss' written with long-s ligature

### 429_0633_R · October, Sunday 20. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0633/867,0,674,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Church in the
morning; in the
afternoon I went
chestnutting with
Miss Alice Lee.
```

### 429_0634_L · October, Monday 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0634/0,0,877,1273/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove back to
Cambridge in the
morning.
In the evening
went to the glee
club and heard
some really beau-
tiful singing
```

### 429_0634_R · October, Tuesday 22. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0634/877,0,680,1273/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Quite a little
town and gown
row apropos of
a Butler and
Kearney political
meeting. It was
good fun to
see the crow-
wads in.
```
> 'crow-wads' as written; reading of last words uncertain

### 429_0635_L · October, Wednesday 23. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0635/0,0,853,1295/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
I am very much
interested in the
Political Economy,
although I by no
means altogether
agree with Mill.
I am getting
rather disgusted
with Ferrier's Philosop[?]
```
> last word runs into the gutter/page edge; ending not visible

### 429_0635_R · October, Thursday 24. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0635/853,0,697,1295/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took a long tramp
with Jack[?] Tebbetts[?],
who has improved
wonderfully; I like
him very much
indeed.
Have just received
a letter telling me
that Cornelius has
distinguished him
-self by marrying
a french actress!
He is a disgrace
to the family -
the vulgar brute.
```
> name in line 2 uncertain

*1878-10-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
P.S. She turns out
to be a mere
courtezan! a harlot!
```
> 'mere', 'courtezan' and 'harlot' underlined; postscript added below entry

### 429_0636_L · October, Friday 25. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0636/0,0,843,1261/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took the evening
boat for New York.
I have not hither-
to been studying
as hard as I ought
to have—at least
in some subjects; but
as soon as I come
home from this
holiday I shall
set to with a
will.
```

### 429_0636_R · October, Saturday 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0636/843,0,677,1261/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Breakfasted with
West; poor old Doc[?]
is awfully cut up
about Cornel's[?] insane
marriage. Spent the
morning with
darling little
Aunt Annie,
lunched with
West and Alfred
and came out
to Oyster Bay in
afternoon. It is
just lovely to be
back with the
family again.
All look beautifully
except dear old Nell
```
> 'Doc' has a small raised mark after it (possibly a superscript letter). Last line runs off the page; sentence not visibly continued on next page.

### 429_0637_L · October, Sunday 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0637/0,0,841,1217/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
My twentieth birth-
day. Lovely though
it is to be spendin[?]
it with darling
Muffie, Bysie and
Pussie[?], I can
not help having
some very sad
thoughts about
my beloved Father.
Oh, Father, sometimes
I feel as though
I would give
half my life to
see you but
for a moment!
Oh, what loving memories
I have of you!
```
> 'Pussie' begins with a B-like overstroke; reading uncertain. 'spendin' runs to the page edge.

*1878-10-27 · other · bottom · ink · hand TR · low*
```
2 pay[?] Gundrill[?]
```
> Short line squeezed at foot of page; meaning unclear.

### 429_0637_R · October, Monday 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0637/841,0,693,1217/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning
rowed around
Centre Island. There
was a stiff Norther
blowing, and so heavy
a sea running in
the sound that the
curlers filled the
boat half full.
Sailed home from
Pine Island at a
tremendous speed.
In the afternoon
took a brisk
walk through the
woods with my
gun, but saw
nothing.
```

### 429_0638_L · October, Tuesday 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0638/0,0,849,1255/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out sailing
for ducks. The wind
was light and
baffling and we
saw but few birds
but had capital fun.
We got into a flock
of coots and I
knocked down two;
I killed a checker-
back loon after
a long chase;
and missed a
shot at an old
squaw; we saw some
grebes but could not
get near them.
```

*1878-10-29 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 loon 2 surf ducks.
```

### 429_0638_R · October, Wednesday 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0638/849,0,685,1255/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There was a south
easter blowing, with
continual heavy
rain squalls. I went
out sailing for
ducks but after
getting drenched
through returned
with nothing. I
missed a good shot
at a grebe.
```

### 429_0639_L · October, Thursday 31. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0639/0,0,865,1275/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-10-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Raining again! I have
had most awful
luck in weather
this time. Monday
so rough we couldn't
go out; Tuesday so
calm we couldn't
get anywhere, yesterday
and today raining.
```

### 429_0639_R · November, Friday 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0639/865,0,679,1275/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on to
Cambridge.
```

### 429_0640_L · November, Saturday 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0640/0,0,875,1236/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Was a steward
of the field meet-
ing of the
athletic association
which took place
today.
In evening was
initiated into the
Porcellian Club,
and was "higher"
with wine than I
ever have been
before—or will be
again. Still, I
could wind up my
watch. Wine always
makes ^me^ awfully
fighty.
```

### 429_0640_R · November, Sunday 3. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0640/875,0,669,1236/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rather under the
weather, owing to
last nights spree.
Took up my
sunday school
class again; the
scholars were
all delighted to
see me.
```

### 429_0641_L · November, Monday 4. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0641/0,0,872,1249/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Am going to give
an accurate abstract
of my doings this
week, as a sampl.
7.45 prayers 8.00 breakfa[?]
8.30—1.00 study. 1.00 lunch,
1.30—4.30 study. 4.30
—6.00 walk. 7.00 went
to dinner to University
Creco[?]. Came out towards
12.00 and lounged in
Club for a couple of
hours.
```
> 'breakfast' runs off the page edge; 'sampl.' as written; 'Creco' reading uncertain.

### 429_0641_R · November, Tuesday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0641/872,0,668,1249/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
7.45 Prayers. 8.00 breakfast.
8.30—12 Studied. To my
utter astonishment
Nell & Mother turned
up, on for the day.
I took Nell up to
the Club, and lunched
with Darling little
Muffie in town.
Studied 3.00—5.00.
5.00—6.00 boxed. Dinner
at 6.00. Played whist
till 9.00; studied till
11.00.
```

### 429_0642_L · November, Wednesday 6. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0642/0,0,778,1228/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
7.45 Prayers 8.00 Breakfast
8.30—4.00 studied.
4.00—6.00 walked with
Dick Saltonstall and
Jack Tebbetts. Spent
evening in Club
till nine; studied till
11.00.
```
> 'Tebbetts' reading slightly uncertain.

### 429_0642_R · November, Thursday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0642/778,0,693,1228/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Prayer 7.45. 8.00 Breakfast
9—5.30 studied. Dinner
at 6.00[?]. 8—10 studied
```

```
Friday
```

```
Prayers 7.45
Studied 9.00—5.30
In the evening
```

### 429_0644_L · November, Sunday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0644/0,0,770,1190/full/0/default.jpg

```
Saturday
```

*1878-11-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took breakfast at
the club at about
10.a.m.
Drove over to see
football match
with Harry Chapin.
In afternoon drove
over to Minot
Welds, where I shall
stay till Monday
morning.
```

### 429_0644_R · November, Monday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0644/770,0,671,1190/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-11 · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sunday
```
> Written above printed 'MONDAY 11.', which is struck through by hand (~~Monday 11.~~).

*1878-11-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Church in morning;
walked home with
Miss Wheelright. In
afternoon drove over
with Minot in his
tilbury to Dick
Saltonstalls, where
we took tea and
came home at 10
oclock by moon
light. Miss Alice
Lee was there, as
sweet and pretty
as ever; and I
enjoyed myself
most heartily.
```

### 429_0645_L · November, Tuesday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0645/0,0,814,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a sparring
match with
Billy Gaston.
```

### 429_0646_L · November, Saturday 16. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0646/0,0,796,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Political Economy
examination.
In afternoon
went out to
George Minots, where
I spend the night.
```
> 'to' preceded by a small stroke or blot.

### 429_0646_R · November, Sunday 17. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0646/796,0,656,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After dinner drove
back to Cambridge
in time for my
Sunday School.
```

### 429_0647_L · November, Monday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0647/0,0,794,1184/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have 3 exams. this
week; have been
studying like a
Trojan lately.
```

### 429_0648_R · November, Saturday 23. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0648/804,0,643,1214/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There was an
alarm of scarlet
fever among the
Richardson children
so I turned out
promptly and
for the present
am "chumming"
with Harry
Jackson, Bob
Bacon having
gone home with
a sore throat.
```

### 429_0649_L · November, Sunday 24. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0649/0,0,801,1225/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went in to Harry
Shaws for dinner,
and had a very
pleasant time.
```

### 429_0650_L · November, Tuesday 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0650/0,0,790,1206/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took dinner at
the Hoopers[?]; Miss
Jeannie Tuckerman
was there
```
> Ink heavy/smudged; family name in line 2 uncertain; 'there' blotted.

### 429_0650_R · November, Wednesday 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0650/790,0,685,1206/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After lunch drove
over with Dick
Saltonstall to his
place (at Chestnut
Hill) where I shall
spend Thanksgiving.
I like the whole
Saltonstall family
very much; I really
feel almost as if
I were at home
when I am over
there. Mr. Saltonstall
is one of the
best examples of a
true, simple hearted
"gentleman of the old
school" I have ever met.
```

### 429_0651_L · November, Thursday 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0651/0,0,780,1219/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-11-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day with
"Alice" and "Rose"—
dancing, walking,
playing lawn
tennis &c. I have
gotten very well
acquainted with
both of them; Rose
is a very good, pleasant
girl, and as for
pretty Alice Lee,
I think her one
of the sweetest, most
ladylike girls I have
ever met.
They all call me
by my first name
now
```

### 429_0651_R · December, Sunday 1. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0651/780,0,670,1219/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Breakfasted as usual
at the Porcellian;
these Sunday break-
-fasts are a great
institution, taking
place about 9.30.
The food is capital,
and it is pleasant
to be with the
fellows.
```

### 429_0652_L · December, Monday 2. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0652/0,0,783,1201/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Shifted my quarters
to Ware's & Washburnes
room, where I shall
probably remain
till Xmas. All the
fellows have been
very kind about
asking me to share
their rooms.
Teddy Bayliss[?] came
of age and gave
a Porcellian breakfast
in the club; it was
great fun.
```

### 429_0653_R · December, Thursday 5. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0653/797,0,665,1201/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sparred for over a
half an hour steadily
with Sharon; getting
the worst of it of
course; but I gave
him his work.
I have gotten pretty
high marks in my
hour examinations so
far.
I enjoy the Porcellian
more and more every
day; I like almost
all the fellows—es-
pecially Otho Williams
```

### 429_0654_R · December, Saturday 7. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0654/806,0,666,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-07 · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sunday 8
```
> Written above printed 'SATURDAY 7.', which is struck through by hand (~~Saturday 7.~~).

*1878-12-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Several 'honoraries'
were out at the
Porcellian breakfast.
After Sunday School
went in town and
had a very pleasant
dinner at Jack
Tebbets.
```

### 429_0655_L · December, Sunday 8. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0655/0,0,789,1201/full/0/default.jpg

```
```
