### 429_0655_R · December, Monday 9. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0655/789,0,683,1201/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
This being out of my
own room has broken
up my regular habits
a good deal, and cut
into my studying
and exercise. Kind
though the two Charlies
are I find it rather
difficult to get my
hours arranged to suit
me; and spend more
time in the club than
I ought to, playing
whist and billiards,
and at partridge
suppers.
```

### 429_0656_L · December, Tuesday 10. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0656/0,0,787,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We have started a
Finance Club which
bids fair to do very
well; it will be of
great benefit to us.
Bob Bacon & I are
preparing a piece on
municipal taxation.
```

### 429_0656_R · December, Wednesday 11. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0656/787,0,685,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It is strange how much
I am pressed at Harvard;
I have absolutely no
time to think, ex-
-cept about my studies.
Sometimes however, at
night especially, I get
thinking about my
loved Father, and
then I feel very
desolate and heart=
sore. It seems so
terrible to think
that he will not be
with~~x~~ us at Christmas.
It often does not seem
possible that he is
really dead. I can
```
> 'with' followed by a cancelled letter or two; entry continues on next page (Dec 12)

### 429_0657_L · December, Thursday 12. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0657/0,0,786,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
recall all his speeches
and acts so very vividly.
It is so sweet and yet so
very sad to remember
one of his last speeches
to me; when he said
that after all I was the
dearest of his children
to him. O, how little
worthy I am of such
a father; I feel such a
hopeless sense of inferiority
to him; I loved him so,
and yet I could do so
little for him! He was
such a very large part
~~in my [illegible]~~ in my
life, and yet I am
```
> continues from previous page (Dec 11 entry); struck-through words on second-to-last line partly unreadable; continues on next page

### 429_0657_R · December, Friday 13. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0657/786,0,686,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
now so pressed and driven
by work and pleasure,
that I often have not
even time to think of
him. But perhaps it is
better so, for when I do
think much, I feel such
a terrible, dull pain at
thinking that never in
this world shall I see
him again. But with
the help of my God I
shall ^try^ to lead such a
life as he would have
wished, and to do noth-
-ing I would have
been ashamed to confess
to him. I am very
```
> continues from previous page (Dec 11 entry); first six lines under a grey stain, readable but less certain; 'he' in 'as he would' is underlined; 'have' appears written over a letter; continues on next page

### 429_0658_R · December, Sunday 15. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0658/771,0,672,1201/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
This week everything
seems to conspire to re-
-mind me of Father. The
whole Sunday School went
to the funeral of one of
the children. When
the coffin came into
church, it recalled the~~x~~
day when I saw the
body of him I loved dearest
carried so, with such
vividness that it
almost unmanned
me. I was afraid
I should break down in
church. It has made
me feel very homesick
```
> cancelled stroke after 'the' at end of line 8; entry continues beyond this page

### 429_0659_L · December, Wednesday 18. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0659/0,0,764,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went up to the ΔK.E
Theatricals which were
really remarkably good -
very clever and with
hardly any smut[?]. Stayed
up there till about 12. It
was a pretty wet night
and I had capital
fun. Afterwards went
up to the club where
I stayed till about 2 a.m
Otho Williams and
I, being the two
worst billiard players,
^in the Club^ have great matches
together. I like him
very much.
```
> 'in the Club' written in left margin with a caret/mark '1' indicating insertion

### 429_0659_R · December, Thursday 19. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0659/764,0,688,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Italian Exam, in which
I did ^rather^ poorly.
Of my college
friends so far, perhaps I
am most intimate
with Minot Weld; almost
as much so with Harry
Chapin. But I am very
fond of Harry Shaw,
Dick Trimble, Jack
Tebbets, Dick Saltonstall
and also Charley
Washburn and
Charley Ware.
Have gotten
pretty good marks
so far, this year.
```

### 429_0660_L · December, Friday 20. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0660/0,0,773,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
~~Went on to N.Y.~~
```
> entire line struck through; same words entered under Dec 21 on facing page

### 429_0660_R · December, Saturday 21. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0660/773,0,670,1211/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on to N.Y.
It is ever so
pleasant to get
home again; I
enjoy college to the
full, and yet I
am always only
too delighted to
get home. Truly
these are the golden
years of my life.
I can not ima-
gine a fellow having
a happier time
than I have had
since I have been
in College.
```

### 429_0661_L · December, Sunday 22. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0661/0,0,780,1220/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Although the family
are all so cheerful,
yet we can none
of us help thinking
a good deal of our
dear lost one; but
now the sweet is
overcoming the
bitter in our mem-
ories of him.
I wonder if I shall
ever be very miserable!
In spite of the great
sorrow - the greatest
which could have
befallen me - I have
yet been very happy
these last six months.
```

### 429_0661_R · December, Monday 23. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0661/780,0,692,1220/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to see my
old Mission scholars.
Poor Cassin[?] is dead.
All the Roosevelts are
getting engaged.
John is crazy with
delight; Em confessed
to me that he is really
deeply in love with
sweet Miss Parish,
Doc of course is
abjectly devoted
to Fanny; ~~Em~~ Al is
carrying on a
lethargic love affair
with Miss Tuckerman.
```
> name on line 3 uncertain (Cassin/Cassius?); struck word before 'Al' looks like 'Em'

### 429_0662_L · December, Tuesday 24. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0662/0,0,770,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Jack Elliott came
on from Philadel-
-phia - just the
same dear old
absentminded fellow
as ever. It is a
relief to find some
one not in love.
Am bothered a
little by what
Doc calls an
"ephemeral fever".
```
> followed by a line of scribbled flourishes/dashes below the text

### 429_0662_R · December, Wednesday 25. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0662/770,0,682,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Xmas. Had
rather a melan-
choly day. It
does seem so
very terrible not
to have Father
with us. He was
the perfect life
of all our
enjoyments.
Received beau-
-tiful presents -
chiefly books
```
> 'life' on line 8 read as written; could conceivably be another word

### 429_0663_L · December, Thursday 26. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0663/0,0,745,1184/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Confined to the
house by the fever.
As regards my
prospects in after-
-life it seems to
look more & more
as if I was going
to be a Naturalist.
If so I shall have
to study three
years abroad - whi[?] it
makes me perfectly
blue to think of
it.
```
> word after dash on line 11 unclear (possibly a false start 'whi' or 'which')

### 429_0663_R · December, Friday 27. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0663/745,0,679,1184/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Still in the house.
Emlen has been
very touching
about his love
for Grace Parish.
Dear old fellow!
I wish him
every success,
with all my
heart.
Old Nell seems
to be doing
very well.
What a wonder-
ful set of rela-
-tions I have got -
cousins and all,
especially my own family.
```

### 429_0664_L · December, Saturday 28. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0664/0,0,760,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out to Oyster
Bay with Jack
Elliott. Took a
long tramp through
the woods and
fields. It is cold,
although without
snow, and there
are redpolls and
crossbills in the
woods; and I
shot an Ipswich
sparrow.
Passed the evening
talking philosophy
and playing
double dummy
whist.
```

### 429_0664_R · December, Sunday 29. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0664/760,0,683,1230/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to Church. In
afternoon took a
long tramp with
Jack. He is the
best companion
on a trip of this
kind I know.
I always enjoy
these winter excursions
to Oyster Bay so
much. The shooting
though very poor
is great fun, and
after the cold
and fatigue, of the
day, one appreciates
so a warm supper and
rousing wood fire.
Snowed in the night
```
> last line written smaller below the final ruled line, possibly added later

### 429_0665_L · December, Monday 30. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0665/0,0,769,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Snowy & windy. Jack & I
went out in the rowboat,
spending ½ an hour breaking
our way through the shore ice.
Sailed to Plum Pt, rowed to Pine
Island, where we lunched, & as
West Harbour was frozen solid,
put out again. There was
a heavy surf running & we were
swamped twice before we
succeeded in launching our
boat through the rollers, getting
soaked through & through with
the freezing water. Had the devils
own work ^rowing^ getting to Rocky Pt;
then hoisted our sail & went
like the wind. Plenty of ducks, but
water was too rough, & they too shy,
to come near them. Tired[?] & Rather[?]
```
> 'rowing' written above 'getting' (getting not clearly struck); last line squeezed below ruling, final words uncertain

### 429_0665_R · December, Tuesday 31. 1878.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0665/769,0,693,1192/full/0/default.jpg

*1878-12-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 7.30 started through the
woods, with Rempson[?] the
gunner. As there was ^4 inches^ snow
on the ground we took
no dog. We tramped
about 25 miles across
country, not having a
mouthful to eat and
not sitting down till
we reached home at
7.30 - having walked
12 hours. So I am
tired out, but have never
had a more glorious
day; the weather was
beautiful (clear, cold & still) the
sport splendid & I shot very well.
6 quail 8 rabbit 1 squirrel
```
> gunner's name uncertain

## 1879

### 429_0674_R · Wednesday, January 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0674/887,0,930,1486/full/0/default.jpg

* · inscription · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Theodore Roosevelt
```
> Signature written above the printed ornament/heading.

*1879-01-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Oyster Bay, Long Island.
The family came out
of town yesterday, with
Uncle Jimmie &
Aunt Annie, to spend
New Years with us.
We had a very jolly
time. They left in
the afternoon, when
Jack ^Elliott^ & I took a
brisk tramp through
the snow covered
woods with our guns.
```
> "Elliott" inserted above, between "Jack" and "&"; "Jack" not visibly struck.

### 429_0675_L · Thursday, January 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0675/0,0,852,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
There was quite a
heavy snow storm
going on, but I
went out hunting;
but finally the
snow completely
covered the rabbit
tracks so I gave
up.
In afternoon
went in town. (N.Y[?]
```
> Heavy show-through; last line ending "(N.Y" uncertain.

*1879-01-02 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 rabbits
```

### 429_0675_R · Friday, January 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0675/852,0,927,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
For some unexplained
reason Tom Ludlow
has asked me to
be usher at his
wedding; so has
Jack Roosevelt.
Have been made
the confidant
of West, Em
& Al in their
love affairs.
Old Nell had
two of his friends
up to dine with
me; I liked one,
Russell, very much.
```

### 429_0676_L · Saturday, January 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0676/0,0,862,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It makes me
perfectly blue to
think of leaving
the family; and
yet I have a
royal time at
Cambridge.
Called on pretty
Annie Murray.
What an old
trump Nell is!
```

### 429_0676_R · Sunday, January 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0676/862,0,931,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Hal Chapin took
tea with us. Had
such a sweet talk
with Muffie.
I wonder if ever
a man had
two better sisters
than I have!
In evening went
on to Harvard.
```

### 429_0677_L · Monday, January 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0677/0,0,864,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Arrived early in
morning; have
began studying
with a will.
```

### 429_0678_S · Wednesday, January 8, 1879., Thursday, January 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0678/0,0,1794,1580/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Examination in
Natural History in
which I am afraid
I did poorly. (Too true)
Am studying
hard"; and am
also working on
an article for the
Finance Club
(Municipal Taxation)
and on a catalogue
of the Birds of
Oyster Bay.
```
> "(Too true)" in smaller writing, probably added later. Mark after "hard" looks like a superscript double stroke/ditto; rendered as ".

### 429_0679_S · Friday, January 10, 1879., Saturday, January 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0679/0,0,1799,1583/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 10.30 a.m Harry Shaws
cutter drew up before the Porcel-
lian, and we drove over to
the Saltonstalls, where
we found Dick and
Minot Weld; also Alice
and Rose. The sleighing
was splendid; we
could not have had
a better day - cold,
clear and calm. We
then drove in town,
as I wished to see
Mr Saltonstall about
Dick's coming into the
Porc. Spent the evening
playing whist and billiards
with Otho Williams, Van Ren-
-saeler and Charley Morgan.
```
> Entry on right page (Jan 11).

### 429_0680_L · Sunday, January 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0680/0,0,855,1519/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started my Sunday
School class again.
```

### 429_0681_S · Tuesday, January 14, 1879., Wednesday, January 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0681/0,0,1756,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on by night
boat to New York
to Ludlows wedding.
```

*1879-01-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Arrived at N.Y. Went
to a rehearsal of
the wedding. One
of my old flames,
pretty Helen White,
is a bridesmaid.
```

### 429_0682_L · Thursday, January 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0682/0,0,858,1543/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Bad weather, but
Grace church was
crowded. I ushered
on the middle aisle
with Livingstone,
Rutherford, and
Cruger.
Took the night
train for Harvard;
driving down to it
in the cutter was
upset.
```
> Punctuation after "weather" a small faint mark, read as comma.

### 429_0683_L · Saturday, January 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0683/0,0,853,1536/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Late last night was aroused by
a deputation of about half the Porc.
who came down to tell me I was
elected 5th man of the first nine
of the Pudding. I was dressed
nolens volens, taken up to the
club and all around, and did
not get to bed till 6. a.m.
The others of the 1st nine are Bob Bacon,
Dick Trimble, Billy Hooper, Charley Ware
(myself) Harry Jackson, Harry Shaw, Charley
Morgan & Jeff Miller.
In the evening we had a
Porc. Dinner given by Harry Shaw
and Minot Weld; it was a great
success and many graduates
were out. Late in the evening some
of the men, getting awfully drunk,
raised particular Cain,
```
> Lines listing the first nine written smaller; entry may continue on following page.

### 429_0684_L · Monday, January 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0684/0,0,855,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Began running for the
Pudding.
```

### 429_0685_L · Friday, January 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0685/0,0,867,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Was initiated into the
Pudding.
```

### 429_0685_R · Saturday, January 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0685/867,0,937,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 hour examination
in the morning.
In afternoon went
out to Chestnut
Hill to pass Sunday.
I spent most of the
evening with Mr.
Saltonstall, looking
over his old family
papers, comparing
our Egyptian and
Syrian experiences
and talking over
things in general.
Tom Lee came in and
we had a grand theol-
-ogical discussion.
```

### 429_0686_L · Sunday, January 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0686/0,0,870,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to church in
morning and had a
very interesting sermon.
In afternoon we
coasted on double run-
-ners - a party of
about twenty five of us
in all. I never had
better fun. The more I
see of Rose Saltonstall
and Alice Lee, the
more I like them; es-
-pecially pretty Alice.
The whole family call
me "Thee" now.
Alice came in to take
tea and spend the
evening.
```

### 429_0687_R · Friday, January 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0687/869,0,921,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-01-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to Chestnut Hill
to coast on "toborgans",
or "crust runners". Besides
Alice and Rose, Lulu
Lane, Nana Rotch and
Bessie Whitney were out
there. The fellows were
Dick S., Minot W.; Jack
Tebbets, Ell Whitney
Tom Lee and myself.
We had capital
fun. Alice, Miss
Whitney, Dick & I
coasted on the same
torbogan.
```

### 429_0688_L · Saturday, February 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0688/0,0,895,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out to Worcester
to spend Sunday
with Charley Washburn.
Took tea at the
Tiffanys; met a pretty
Miss Cross. Had a
capital sleighride behind
Charleys trotter.
```

### 429_0688_R · Sunday, February 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0688/895,0,890,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Passed a very pleasant
Sunday; of the same
kind that I always
pass at home.
```
> Stray marks after "Sunday" (blot or show-through) read as semicolon.

### 429_0689_L · Monday, February 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0689/0,0,876,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Began grinding
like a trump.
```

### 429_0690_R · Thursday, February 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0690/887,0,929,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Semiannuals began
```
> Small mark before "Semiannuals" (possibly an opening quote or stray stroke) not transcribed.

### 429_0691_L · Sunday, February 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0691/0,0,873,1506/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
One year ago today the
man, who was not only
my father, but was my
dearest loved friend,
died. O God give me
strength to live as he
would have wished
me! It has been very
sad to write to the
family. Fortunately
I have been studying
so hard - and on Sun-
days almost as hard
with my S. S. class re-
that I have not had
much time to think;
but there are very sad
moments, especially in the
evening and at night.
```
> 're-' at line end appears to be a false start or hyphenation; transcribed as written

### 429_0692_R · Sunday, February 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0692/871,0,933,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have been working
hard all the week
```

### 429_0693_L · Friday, February 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0693/0,0,871,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Semi annuals ended
today. I have been
working hard since
Christmas, and shall
now take a rest.
```
> 'Christmas' has an ink blot over the final letter(s)

### 429_0693_R · Saturday, February 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0693/871,0,923,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Our great, midyear
Porc. supper came off
today. Besides the ~~28~~ ^18^
immediates about 40
honoraries attended. All
the older men had
known Father either
personally or by repu-
tation, and I had
very pleasant talks
with them. How I
wish I could ever
do something to keep
up his name!
George Upham came
into the club - the other
Sophmores are Sam
Hammond Chas[?] Dick Sprague
& Billy Peters.
```
> '28' appears struck/overwritten with '18' written above. 'Chas' followed by an apostrophe-like mark; reading uncertain. 'Sophmores' as written.

### 429_0694_L · Thursday, February 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0694/0,0,881,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening started
by 7. p.m train for
Island Falls, Maine,
where I intend to
spend a couple of weeks.
```

### 429_0694_R · Friday, February 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0694/881,0,895,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-02-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Mattawomkeag
about 11 a.m, where
I found Will Seavall
waiting for me in a
sleigh. From there ~~it~~
it is 36 miles to
Island Falls, where
I am now, and it
was nearly ten
when we arrived here.
It was bitterly cold,
but still, and we were
well wrapped up, so
I enjoyed the ride
very much. The road
was hardly broken
out, in places.
```
> 'it' at end of line 5 appears struck through. 'Seavall' as written.

### 429_0695_L · Saturday, March 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0695/0,0,856,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Tried snowshoing for the
first time. Went about
six miles, making the
round of Bill Dows
"lucivee" traps, which
however contained nothing.
The woods looked simply
perfect, all the evergreens
being loaded with
snow; I have never seen
a ~~prettier~~ ^grander^ sight than these
northern forests in winter.
I got along very well, except in
a cedar swamp. We saw many
tracks of lucivees, foxes, otters
and rabbits; and found a
porcupine up in a hemlock. I
brought him down quite neatly
with the rifle.
```
> 'prettier' struck with 'grander' written above. 'snowshoing' and 'Dows' as written.

### 429_0695_R · Sunday, March 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0695/856,0,948,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Read my bible in the
morning. In the af-
ternoon took a tramp
through the woods; I
can not get used to the
extreme beauty of the
snow covered pine and
spruce forests. The cold
does not bother me atall
in the woods, but if
there is any wind
it is very disagreeable in
the open. I get along
very well on snow shoes
but the swamps are very
tough work - although per-
haps not so bad as in
summer. There is very little
life in the woods.
```
> Heavy show-through from reverse interferes; 'atall' appears run together.

### 429_0696_L · Monday, March 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0696/0,0,909,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went off on a tramp through
the woods with Will Dow. Walked
up to Dyer Brook, then a couple
of miles along it, and then
struck out in the woods. The best
walking was on the hardwood
grounds; it was fearfully hard
work in some of the thick, swampy
spruce and cedar thickets, but not
as bad as in summer. I saw an
arctic woodpecker by a stream and
some Hudsonian[?] chickadees in a
spruce wood, and ~~some~~ ^two^ whiskey jacks
came round us at lunch time; we
saw two or three hare tracks, and once
the round footprints of a canada lynx,
and found where a spruce partridge had
passed the night: but in an 8 hour
tramp, these were the only traces of life.
```
> 'Hudsonian' heavily inked, possibly written over another word. 'some' struck with 'two' above. Initial 't' of 'The best' may be lower-case.

### 429_0696_R · Tuesday, March 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0696/909,0,914,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the day tramping
through the woods with
Will Seawall. We saw a
good many tracks of
lucivees, foxes, rabbits, otters,
minks, weasels, squirrels, porcupines, &c;
but the only living creatures
were a couple of chickadees &
a woodpecker. Finally we
found the ~~tra~~footprints of a
partridge on some hard-
wood ground, and following
them up roused the bird
from under a fallen birch
top, and I shot it. The
snowshoing is poor, the snow
being wet & heavy, and the
weather warmish.
```
> 'tra' overwritten by 'footprints'. 'Seawall' as written here.

*1879-03-04 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 ruffed grouse.
```
> game tally written below entry, same form as tally on 10 March

### 429_0697_L · Wednesday, March 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0697/0,0,887,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day snowshoing
through the woods as
usual - and with my
usual ill success as
regards game. ~~But~~
Otherwise I am enjoying
myself to the utmost,
but the asthma keeps me
up a good deal at
night. Lunched at a
logging camp. Finally
I saw a partridge in the
top of a spruce and
killed him with the
rifle - also a porcupine.
By trapping we have caught
a lucivee and a fox; but have
seen nothing but the tracks of game
other than the 2 partridges ( Ruffed grouse
```
> 'But' struck through. Final line runs to page edge with an opening bracket and no closing.

### 429_0697_R · Thursday, March 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0697/887,0,907,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Seawall, Dow and I
started with a pung
and a shaggy, lean horse
for a logging camp 30
miles distant, reaching
it after dusk. When we
started the thermometer was
10° below zero, but I was too
well wrapped up to heed
the cold. The roads were
frightful; none but a
backwoods horse could travel
them. I saw several flocks
of snow buntings, a red
crossbill and a small
party of grosbeaks which
were very tame and
confiding. I also saw the tracks of
a mouse - probably hesperomys.
```

### 429_0698_L · Friday, March 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0698/0,0,907,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dow and I started out early
in the morning, although there
was a heavy snow storm going
on, and tramped through the
woods till the afternoon without
seeing anything, when we
roused a caribou in a
dense, low spruce wood. I
caught a glimpse of him
as he vanished not thirty
yards distant, but had
no chance to fire. We
followed his tracks till
dusk, when we went into
camp. The night was very
cold, and we had but
little food and no blankets,
and so were pretty uncomfortable
inspite of our huge fire.
```
> 'uncomfortable' runs off the page edge.

### 429_0698_R · Saturday, March 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0698/907,0,916,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Before sunrise we again started
on the caribou's trail, the thermometer
being below zero. He led a
most tortuous course through
cedar swamps, hardwood lands,
hemlock ridges[?], &c; and
sometimes would go in a straight
course across the great cranberry
bogs. Generally he trotted, coming
down not only on his hoofs but on his
whole lower legs, ~~but~~ ^the snow^, being
over three feet deep, but in
bad ground he made great jumps
like a huge rabbit. The snow
shoing was very bad and after
following him till midday without
finding where he had stopped to browse
or rest[?], we gave the chase up, and reached
the logging camp at sunset.
```
> 'but' struck with 'the snow' written above. 'thermometer' runs off page edge.

### 429_0699_L · Sunday, March 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0699/0,0,909,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I was pretty well fagged
out by my two days
chase after the caribou
and have been lying
back and resting all today.
I have not been bothered
atall by the asthma since I
came into camp. I like these
lumbermen very much,
and get on capitally with
them - great, rough, hospit-
-able fellows. I am great
friends with one in especial,
Charley Brown. It has rained
a little and then frozen,
improving the snowshoing
greatly, and makings the forests
look beautifully, each tree covered
with glittering icicles.
```
> 'makings' as written.

### 429_0699_R · Monday, March 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0699/909,0,924,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Clear and cold. Seawall and I
while hunting through the woods
found a small deer yard. We
approached it from different sides,
and I saw the buck first, getting
a shot at its head, but I tripped
up at the moment and missed
it. It then broke out of the
yard and ran; after following
at full speed about a mile
I got another shot and killed
it. ^with the rifle.^ We then dragged it to camp.
I spent the afternoon in shooting
rabbits, also with the rifle, stealing
cautiously through the woods. In
the evening we had a royal venison
supper - a pleasant change from the
ordinary routine of pork & beans.
```

*1879-03-10 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 buck 3 white rabbit.
```

### 429_0700_L · Tuesday, March 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0700/0,0,860,1503/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started back for Island Falls. It
was warmish, sleeting and
raining, and the roads were
frightful. Several times when
the horse floundered off the track
into the deep snow-drifts we
had to unharness him and
drag the sleigh ourselves for a
hundred yards or so. It was
very fatiguing work, as we walked
all the time, and got wet through
and cold and hungry. The
road was chiefly through the woods,
with every now and then clearings,
with the shaggy, ^black^ cattle and sheep
crowding around the barns - the
pictures of bleakness. Heard some snow
^buntings^ birds singing beautifully. Reached
Island Falls in time for tea.
```
> 'buntings' written above 'birds'; 'birds' is heavily inked and may be struck or overwritten.

### 429_0700_R · Wednesday, March 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0700/860,0,992,1503/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning went the round
(7 miles) of the lucivee traps, which
contained nothing, but we saw a
coon cross the road ahead of us,
and following his tracks half a
mile I shot him from a dead
tree. About 10 a.m we started
through the woods for Matta-
womkeag Lake, at the foot
of which we camped
out for the night. The
snowshoing, for the first
time since I have been
up here, was capital.
On the way down I
picked up a couple
of spruce partridges in a
swamp.
```

*1879-03-12 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 raccoon 2 canada grouse.
```

### 429_0701_L · Thursday, March 13, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0701/0,0,914,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We broke camp pretty early
and reached Island
Falls in time for dinner.
The day was clear, calm
and cool, but not
cold, and I enjoyed
the walk extremely. We
came home the entire
distance over the lake
and river, only needing
our snow shoes about
half the time. We saw no
living thing, but amused
ourselves by practising
at long ranges (300-500
yards) with the rifle,
and all ^three^ of us made
~~some~~ very good shots with
the "old reliable" (Sharp's business[?] - .45[?]
```
> 'some' struck; 'three' inserted above. Last line runs past the ruled frame into the margin; closing reading uncertain.

### 429_0701_R · Friday, March 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0701/914,0,929,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the entire morn-
ing hunting partridges,
without seeing one, and
making the round of
the lucivee traps, which
contained ^nothing^. However I have
had good success this
trip, and I have
never passed a
pleasanter two weeks
The skins of the fox,
lucivee, coon and
buck make quite a
set of trophies; and
we have shot enough
partridges and rabbits
to eat - not to men-
tion the venison. I have
collected a good many specimens
```
> Entry ends at the foot of the page mid-sentence; no continuation on the following page (15 March).

### 429_0702_L · Saturday, March 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0702/0,0,844,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started for Mattawomkeag
in a sleigh at 5. a.m.
It was a very pleasant
day. Took the night
train for Boston. I am
now going to study
all the time till
Easter, doing double work
to make up for my
holiday.
```

### 429_0703_R · Saturday, March 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0703/939,0,884,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sparred for the light
weight cup; won
against my first
man but was
beaten by Hanks,
the champion.
I have been
studying extremely
hard the past week
and have succeeded
in making up
pretty much all I
lost while I was
in Maine.
```
> 'against' begins with an overwritten/blotted letter.

### 429_0704_R · Wednesday, March 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0704/996,0,856,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon
I went out to Chest-
-nut Hill, where I
dined and spent
the night. ~~the~~ at the
Saltonstalls. They were
so cordial and so
glad to see me that
I felt exactly as if
I were at home. I
passed the evening at
the Lees, with pretty Alice.
I am going to send
her & Rose the fox and
lynx skins, made into
rugs.
```
> A short word after 'night.' is struck/blotted (reads like 'the'). 'her' followed by an ink blot before '&'.

### 429_0705_L · Thursday, March 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0705/0,0,904,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Δ.K.E theatricals. They
were very good; it was
rather a "wet" night.
```

### 429_0705_R · Friday, March 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0705/904,0,929,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Pudding Theatricals. They
were excellent - I have
never seen better. the
singing afterwards, both
in the room and in the
yard, was great fun.
```

### 429_0706_L · Saturday, March 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0706/0,0,898,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There was a Porc. supper
but I cut the first
part of it to go in to
Jack Tebbetts: to meet
Alice Lee, Rose Saltonstall,
the Rotches, Miss Whitney
& Miss Lane. I enjoyed
myself very much indeed.
I came out about 11,
in time for the cream
of the supper; there were
some most absurd
scenes; Otho Williams en-
-gaged me to act as
his second in a duel
with Charley Sprague
- both being decidedly
"mellow".
```

### 429_0706_R · Sunday, March 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0706/898,0,896,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a very unusually
interesting time at
Sunday School, showing
the boys a lot of
eastern photographs.
Called on Jeannie
Hooper & Mrs Tuder[?]
in the evening.
```
> Surname after 'Mrs' reads 'Tuder'; uncertain.

### 429_0707_L · Monday, March 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0707/0,0,907,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-03-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening drove over
to Minot Welds to see
some private theatricals;
Miss Bacon and Miss
Frothingham both acted,
and very well to. I sat
by pretty Alice Lee[?] before
and during the perform-
-ance, and spent most
of the remainder of the
evening with Nana
Rotch; so it is needless
to say I enjoyed
myself very much.
Got back about one oclock
and stayed in the Porc,
singing and talking,
till three.
```
> 'Lee' cramped and faint between 'Alice' and 'before'.

### 429_0707_R · Tuesday, April 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0707/907,0,907,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Otho Williams, Jim[?] Wright,
Charley Sprague and
myself had a very
pleasant game supper
in the Porc.
```

### 429_0708_L · Wednesday, April 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0708/0,0,860,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I gave a little lunch-
party in my rooms.
Mrs Saltonstall matron-
ized; the girls were Alice,
Rose, Nana & Emily Rotch,
Bessie Whitney & Lulu Lane;
the fellows Minot, Dick ^Saltonstall^, Jack
Tebbetts, Harry Shaw, Gardy
Lane & Ell. Whitney. We
took them over the Porcel-
-lian and Pudding, and
passed a lovely day. I
gave Alice the lynx skin, made
into a rug; and Rose the fox.
Alice is making me a pair of
slippers. En passant, Nana R.
is very sweet & pretty. Took the
night boat for New York with
Minot & Dick who are going
to spend the vacation week with me.
```
> 'Saltonstall' inserted above between 'Dick' and 'Jack'. Last line written below the ruled frame.

### 429_0708_R · Thursday, April 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0708/860,0,958,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the day showing
Minot & Dick the
sights; both get along
beautifully with the
family, and the latter
is very amusing.
In the evening I[?] we
went to Aunt Annies;
Margy Tuckerman[?],
Fanny Smith & Edith
Carow were there, look-
-ing very prettily.
Edith is just the same
sweet little flirt as
ever
```
> 'I we' appears as an overwritten 'I'/'we'; surname after 'Margy' uncertain.

### 429_0709_L · Friday, April 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0709/0,0,909,1499/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Miss Christine & Miss Lucy
Kean lunched with us; I
like them very much.
Darling little Muffie
has gotten sick, and it
makes me feel miserable.
```

### 429_0709_R · Saturday, April 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0709/909,0,909,1499/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Nell gave us a dinner at
the Jockey Club, with
Grove Porter, The[?] Iselin,
Harold & Smith Hadden.
I drove out in Harold
Haddon's dog cart, the
others coming in drags.
We enjoyed ourselves
extremely, I like Hadden
very much.
```
> Word before 'Iselin' looks like 'The' (possibly an abbreviated first name). Surname spelled both 'Hadden' and 'Haddon' as written.

### 429_0710_L · Sunday, April 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0710/0,0,910,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Poor old Em has been
rejected by Grace Parish!
I had a long talk with
him. It is awfully hard
on him. Muffie is still
not atall well.
```

### 429_0710_R · Monday, April 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0710/910,0,894,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Emlen gave us a lunch
in the Down Town
Club - a great success.
We dined at West's,
to go to the theatre.
```
> 'West's' could also read 'Wests'.

### 429_0711_L · Tuesday, April 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0711/0,0,973,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went to the dog show.
Lizzie Moran[?] lunched
with us. Took Annie
Murray to the rehearsal
of the Fancy Dress Ball.
Took the night train
for Boston. Both boys
have enjoyed the
holiday intensely and
so have I. Motherling
is almost well again
```
> first lines written lighter than the rest

### 429_0711_R · Wednesday, April 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0711/973,0,848,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started work again
```

### 429_0712_R · Friday, April 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0712/993,0,861,1494/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Called on Miss. Nana
Rotch and spent
the afternoon there
- teaching her the
five step waltz.
```

### 429_0713_L · Friday, April 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0713/0,0,867,1503/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have had 2 examinations
this week & so have
been studying hard.
My horse has arrived;
I determined to keep
him in Cambridge
this Spring. I keep
him at Pikes stable.
Went over to
Chestnut Hill in the
afternoon, where I
intend to pass Saturday
& Sunday. We dined
at the Lees.
```

### 429_0713_R · Saturday, April 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0713/867,0,962,1503/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Snowed heavily
all day long! But
in spite of the weather
I took a long walk
with pretty Alice.
I spent most of
the remainder of the
day in teaching
the girls the five
step and a new dance,
the knickerbocker. In
the evening we played
whist and read ghost
stories.
```

### 429_0714_L · Sunday, April 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0714/0,0,972,1514/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
After Church ~~I~~ ^we^ went
to the Francises[?]. In the
afternoon Codman[?] &[?]
Rose, & Alice & myself
went out walking.
```
> "we" written above an "I" that appears struck/overwritten. Rest of page is heavy mirrored show-through of the April 18-19 entries.

### 429_0714_R · Monday, April 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0714/972,0,862,1514/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came back to Cambridge
with much regret
after my two days rest.
In the afternoon
took my horse out
for the first time; he
went beautifully, but
of course is full of
the Old Harry which
adds greatly to the
enjoyment.
```

### 429_0715_L · Tuesday, April 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0715/0,0,971,1504/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took a ride on my
horse; he ^it^ will be
the greatest pleasure
to me this spring,
as I shall ride
him every day the
weather is even
decent. It was the
one thing wanting
for perfect enjoyment.
In the afternoon
I went in to the
Jeffries' to look over
their collections & dined
there. Played whist
in the evening.
```
> "it" written above "he"; no clear strike-through on "he". "perfect" underlined.

### 429_0715_R · Wednesday, April 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0715/971,0,863,1504/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Now that the beautiful
spring weather has fairly
set in, I get up in
time to study an hour
or two before breakfast,
and so have my
afternoons and
evenings pretty much
to myself - for I
have done my hard
studying for this year.
One reason I enjoy
riding here so much is
that there are so many
places, - Milton, Jamaica
Plains, Forrest Hill, Chestnut
Hill &c - where I can ride over
& stay to dinner or lunch. Besides
the rides through all the country
round Boston are beautiful.
```
> last line written below the ruled frame

### 429_0716_L · Thursday, April 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0716/0,0,890,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
I rode over early in the
morning to Chestnut
Hill to spend the day.
After lunch I went
out driving with
Rose, Alice and
Endicott. I had
to ride back to
Cambridge immediately
after dinner, to go
to a "conversazione" at
Laughlins, where I met
one of the Miss Long-
-fellows - a bright,
clever girl.
```
> Heavy mirrored show-through overlaps the writing; image dark.

### 429_0716_R · Friday, April 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0716/890,0,929,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-04-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
I rode up back of
Watertown, pretty fast;
I am trying to sweat
the horses winter coat
off him. In the after-
noon I went in town
with Harry Chapin &
dined at Youngs Hotel;
afterwards we joined
Minot Weld and Dick
Saltonstall at the theatre.
I went out with
Minot to pass the night
at Jamaica Plains.
```
> Dark image with heavy show-through; "sweat" and "Youngs Hotel" fairly clear.

### 429_0717_L · Wednesday, May 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0717/0,0,876,1493/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
My horse is a little
lame from yesterday's
exploits; so I could
not go to the Salton-
-stalls to dinner.
Spent the evening
in the Δ K E, drowning
my sorrows; I have
been an awful fool
to ride the horse so
hard.
```

### 429_0718_R · Saturday, May 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0718/940,0,882,1513/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I went in the morning
to see the class races,
which were exceedingly
good; I sat with the
Lees. In the afternoon
I went out to
pass Sunday at
Milton with the
Whitneys, whom I
like very much - espe-
cially jolly Ella[?] and
pretty Miss. Bessie.
We spent the evening
singing, dancing &c.
```

### 429_0719_L · Sunday, May 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0719/0,0,985,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning we drove
over to Lake Ponkapoag[?]
through the Blue Hills;
the scenery around there
is very beautiful; and
the country is now
simply ~~beautiful~~ perfect.
In the afternoon I
took a long walk
through the woods
with Miss Bessie.
```

### 429_0719_R · Monday, May 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0719/985,0,830,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Early in the morning
came back to Cambridge.
I took tea with the
Lanes[?], and had a
very pleasant evening;
both of the girls are
very sweet and Miss
Lulu[?] very pretty.
```

### 429_0720_L · Tuesday, May 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0720/0,0,991,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After dinner Dick S. and
I drove over in a buggy
to Chestnut Hill where
I passed a very pleas-
ant evening; I brought
the girls out some gold
and silver charms.
I am afraid
I have lamed my
horse hopelessly; it is
very tough luck; but
at least I did have
royal sport for the
three weeks and a half
I had him.
```

### 429_0720_R · Wednesday, May 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0720/991,0,844,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I dined at the Lees,
in the evening taking
a walk with Alice.
Our photographs are
a great success. I
walked back to
Cambridge with John
Tebbetts[?].
```

### 429_0721_L · Thursday, May 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0721/0,0,1002,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon acted as
judge at the Harvard
Athletic Ass. In the evening
called on the Lanes where
I found Alice and Rose;
we spent most of the
evening in dancing.
```

### 429_0721_R · Friday, May 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0721/1002,0,823,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Glee Club concert in the
evening. I sat by Miss
Bacon. Afterwards there
was dancing in
Memorial Hall and I
had some lovely
waltzes with Alice,
Rose, Luly[?] Lane &
Miss Francis.
```

### 429_0722_L · Saturday, May 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0722/0,0,981,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the afternoon Van
Ransaeler[?] & I called
on the Lanes, taking
Miss Luly & Miss Kitty (&
Mrs. Lane) round to see
Van's R[?] room & mine.
We then played lawn
tennis; and stayed to
tea and spent the
evening, dancing till I was nearly ^dead^.
My horse is hopelessly
lame and I am going
to send him back to
New York. It is fright-
fully tough luck; but
I deserved it. I miss
having him to ride
horribly.
```
> "till I was nearly" squeezed in small at line end; "dead" written above at the margin.

*1879-05-24 · entry · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I have never passed a
pleasanter evening.
```
> Written at foot of page, partly below the ruled frame, in a different (smaller, slanting) style; probably an addition to the evening account.

### 429_0722_R · Sunday, May 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0722/981,0,834,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon I
drove over in a buggy
to Chestnut Hill, and
went out walking with
the girls
```

### 429_0723_R · Tuesday, May 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0723/997,0,828,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I drove over in a buggy
to Chestnut Hill, where I
spent the afternoon with
the two girls; I then
drove over to the Whit-
-neys where I took tea
and spent a very
pleasant evening with
pretty "Bessie" (we are very
intimate now). It was
pretty dark driving home
```
> "very" (second) underlined.

### 429_0724_R · Thursday, May 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0724/893,0,932,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I walked over to Chestnut
Hill, spending the afternoon
with Rose & Alice, and
dining at the Saltonstalls.
I walked back in the
evening.
```

### 429_0725_L · Friday, May 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0725/0,0,877,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening I went
in town with Harry Shaw[?]
and Dick Saltonstall to
a theatre circus party
given by Charley Codman.
I took Alice there and
from it, sitting by her;
and the whole thing
was a great success.
```

### 429_0725_R · Saturday, May 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0725/877,0,948,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-05-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I drove over to the Bacons
to take tea and had
a very jolly evening.
George Minot was there.
I like both Miss Nellie
and Miss Julia very
much. After tea we
went to the Frothinghams.
```

### 429_0726_R · Monday, June 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0726/1009,0,835,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
My first annual examination.
Looking over my diary for
the last two weeks it
would seem as if I had
not studied much; but
really I have, getting
up every morning at
six, and studying till
eight; and from half past
eight to half past one;
and usually ^(about half the time)^ from two
till five. So I get in
from seven to ten
hours a day. During most
of the year I ~~can~~[?] rarely
work eight hours a day,
^generally not much more than six.^
My horse has improved
wonderfully, and I exercise
him a little every day.
```
> "(about half the time)" written small above line; "generally not much more than six." written small between lines below "work eight hours a day". Word before "rarely" blotted out, probably "can".

### 429_0727_R · Wednesday, June 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0727/1011,0,833,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dick S. and I drove over
to the Whitneys, where we
took tea and passed a
very jolly evening.
```

### 429_0728_L · Saturday, June 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0728/0,0,875,1505/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I passed my third and hardest
examination in the morning
and so took a holiday in
the afternoon. My horse is
now perfectly well, and so I
rode over to Chestnut Hill;
he was in wild spirits, and
cut up so that I had hard
work to prevent him
spraining his leg again.
It makes me feel like a new
man to have the horse well
again; I had a most glorious
ride. I spent the afternoon
walking with Alice; dined at
the Lees; and spent the
evening dancing, talking
&c, riding back at
10 oclock.
```
> "dined" appears written over another word.

### 429_0728_R · Sunday, June 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0728/875,0,940,1505/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Van Rensselaer[?] and I
dined ^took tea^ at the Lanes
and spent the evening
there, very pleasantly.
```
> "took tea" written small above "dined"; no clear strike-through on "dined".

### 429_0729_R · Tuesday, June 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0729/1014,0,830,1505/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rode over to Chestnut
Hill and spent the
afternoon at the
Saltonstalls.
```

### 429_0730_L · Friday, June 13, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0730/0,0,1020,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Passed my last exam-
-ination.
In the afternoon I
rode over to the
Saltonstalls, and spent
the afternoon walking
with Rose. I took
dinner there; after-
wards went out
walking with Alice.
```

### 429_0730_R · Saturday, June 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0730/1020,0,815,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Breakfasted with the
Porc. at 5.30[?], and my
horse was before the
door and I rode over
to the Bacons where I
lunched, spending the
day very pleasantly
with Miss Nellie &
Miss Julia. I rode
back to Cambridge
at about 6. p.m.; at
7.30 we had a Porc.
dinner. Charley Morgan
got very drunk, and, not
having been sober for a
week, nearly had the D. Ts; I
slept in his room; & walking home
next morning in a dress suit
nearly walked on a proctor
```
> Upper half overlaid by heavy mirrored show-through; time in line 2 uncertain. Entry runs to the bottom of the page and may continue on the next page.

### 429_0731_L · Sunday, June 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0731/0,0,889,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Van Renselaer & I
drove over to take tea
at the Saltonstalls. We
went out walking with
Rose and Alice in the
afternoon, and got
drenched in a rain
storm; driving home
it was pitch dark,
and I came within
an ace of smashing up
against a wagon.
```

### 429_0731_R · Monday, June 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0731/889,0,946,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am leading a life of
most luxurious ease at
present; breakfast in
the Club about 10; my
horse is then before the
door; and I ride
~~[illegible]~~ off - generally
lunching at some
friends house. It is
very pleasant - but
I do not suppose it
would be healthy to
continue it too long.
When I do not stay
out to tea or dinner,
I generally have some kind
of a spree - a "champagne
supper" - up in the
club.
```
> Line 7 begins with a short struck-through word.

### 429_0732_L · Tuesday, June 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0732/0,0,895,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
It was Rose's birthday;
I rode over to Chestnut
Hill where I lunched
and spent the day,
bringing over two
fans for Rose &
Alice. En passant;
those two young
ladies have cost me
over $150 in fans.
In the evening we[?]
went to the strawberry
night of the D.K.[?]
```

### 429_0732_R · Wednesday, June 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0732/895,0,940,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon I
rode over to call on
the Guilds ^at Forest Hill^; I then
rode to Chestnut Hill
where I dined at the
Lees, afterwards taking
Alice to a strawberry
festival at the Lowells.
In the evening I went
to the Pudding Strawberry
Night
```

### 429_0733_L · Thursday, June 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0733/0,0,994,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I rode over to Milton
and spent the day at
the Whitneys. I had
great fun teasing pretty
Bessie about having called
me a flirt; it appears
that ~~I~~ she and Nana
Rotch had compared notes
and come to the conclusion
I was a "gay deceiver"!
In the evening went to
the Δ.K.E strawberry night.
I got into a row with a
mucker and knocked him
down; cutting my knuckles
pretty badly against his
teeth.
```

### 429_0733_R · Friday, June 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0733/994,0,812,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Class day; I think I have never
passed such a pleasant day.
I was one of the Junior Class Ushers;
from 11 till 12.30 I ushered in Saunders[?];
then lunched in the Club; then till
4.30 I stayed at Van Renselaers[?] spread
in the new gymnasium, dancing with
twenty different girls from Boston &
New York - especially Maggie Wolf, Helen
White, Cornelia Bayliss[?], Susie[?] Wheelright,
Pauline Revere, ^Rose L.[?]^ &c. I then ~~usher[?]~~ ^ushered at the^ to
tree; went to the Pudding spread where I
was with Nana Rotch; I then took
Alice to the tea at Morgans, and afterwards
to the Lanes; at 8.00 she and I went to the
yard, and sat in a window in Hollis till
10. looking at the ^brilliantly lighted^ yard and listening to the Glee Club.
I never saw her looking more[?] in perfection.
We then went and danced at Memorial, and
at 11.30 she went home, and I spent
a couple of hours in the Club.
```
> Interlinear insertions above lines 11 and 17; last line written below the ruled frame.

### 429_0734_L · Saturday, June 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0734/0,0,996,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I rode over to see the
Lowells where I passed
a very pleasant time.
In the evening there
was a Porc. Supper.
```

### 429_0734_R · Sunday, June 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0734/996,0,829,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the afternoon Harry
Shaw drove me over to
the Saltonstalls, where we
took tea. Any number of
fellows came in to call
during the afternoon. In the
evening Ellerton[?] W. & Rose,
Harry & Rosy L. and
Alice and myself went
out walking.
```

### 429_0735_L · Monday, June 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0735/0,0,1020,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Breakfasted, as usual, in the
Club, with Harry Shaw. I
rode over and spent the
day at the Lees, walking
with Alice, dancing &c,
returning late in the
evening. My horse is a[?]
looking and feeling
beautifully, and is in
wild spirits. I shall send
him home at the end of the
week.
```

### 429_0735_R · Tuesday, June 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0735/1020,0,841,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning I rode over
to call on Susie Wheelright
in Jamaica Plain. I then
rode to the Saltonstalls, where
I shall stay till Thursday.
I spent the afternoon talking
with Rose, teaching Phil how
to shoot and playing with
Betty[?]. In the evening I
called on the Lowells.
Mr & Mrs Saltonstall are
just too sweet for anything.
They have added so much
to my happiness this year
that I scarcely know how
to thank them. So have
the Lees. I shall be even more
sorry to leave Chestnut Hill
than to leave Harvard.
```

### 429_0736_L · Wednesday, June 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0736/0,0,994,1537/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Early in the morning I rode over
to Cambridge to usher for
Commencement Day. I lunched
at the Lanes; they have
been very kind to me this
year. The four girls - Alice, Rose,
Lila[?] and Miss[?] Kitty[?] [illegible]
[illegible] [illegible] at lunch[?]. The
afternoon I spent in the
club and at the ball
match with Yale, which
we won. I rode back
to the Saltonstalls in
time for tea.
```
> Lines 7-8 (names at lunch) heavily overlaid by show-through and not securely readable.

### 429_0736_R · Thursday, June 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0736/994,0,837,1537/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After breakfast I bid goodbye,
with the most heartfelt
regret, to Chestnut Hill, and
rode over to Cambridge. In
the afternoon I came out
to the Whitneys, where I
shall spend a couple of
days. Like my other Boston
friends they have been
exceedingly kind to me.
The two Miss Lanes and
Nana Rotch were out there
too, and we had an
extremely jolly time.
```

### 429_0737_L · Friday, June 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0737/0,0,1013,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
At 11.10 we started in a special
car to see the "Great Harvard-
Yale Race" which Harvard
won easily. The party consisted
of Alice, Rose & Rosie Lee,
Bessie W. Nana R, Miss Sullo[?]
the two Miss Lanes, Van R.
Jack T., Harry Guild, Dick
R, John Wheel.[?] Ell W. &c.
I have hardly ever
enjoyed myself more.
We did not get home
till 2 a.m., and I
have absolutely no voice
left; thanks to the shouting,
We sang ^during^ most of the return
journey. I spent a large
proportion of my time with
Alice, who[?] never looked prettier
```
> Entry appears to continue past the bottom frame line; last line written on the frame rule.

### 429_0737_R · Saturday, June 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0737/1013,0,831,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Bade adieu to the Whitneys,
and came on to New York.
So ends my Junior
Year; and I can not
possibly conceive of
any fellow having a
pleasanter time than
I have had. I doubt
if I ever shall enjoy
myself so much
again. I have done
well in my studies
and I have had a
most royally good
time with the Club,
my horse, and above
all the sweet, pretty
girls at Chestnut
Hill &c.
```

### 429_0738_L · Sunday, June 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0738/0,0,947,1505/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out to Oyster
Bay; Russell, Haddon,
Betts, Maud Elliott
& the two Miss Kanes
are passing Sunday
with us. I was
perfectly delighted
to see all the
family again;
Muffie & the two
darling girls
greeted me so lovingly.
I spent the whole
day talking with
them all. All the
"Roosevelt boys"
are out here.
Aunt Lucy & Maud
Elliott are staying with us.
```

### 429_0738_R · Monday, June 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0738/947,0,878,1505/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-06-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Re-commenced the old
Oyster Bay life; spent the
day putting my rowboat
in order, building the boat
house, taking a pull to
Plum Pt; sailing with
Uncle Jimmie (who, with
Aunt Annie, is spending
the summer here) &c &c.
I lead just the same
active, out of door life
that I always enjoy
so much - with my
horse, gun and row boat.
The swimming is capital.
Fing[?] & Virginia R. are
passing the summer with
West. Em, poor fellow, is still
very much cut up.
```

### 429_0739_L · Thursday, July 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0739/0,0,958,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I have been getting myself
into condition with the
row boat; rowing to Lloyds
neck &c. Today I tried
my horse jumping; he
went fairly, but at one
fence we got a regular
cropper, the horse rolling
right over on me. In the
evening Em, West, Al &
myself sailed down to
Matinnicock Pt, where we
met Will who was
sailing up with the two
Haddons, Doug. Robinson
and Percy King - all
good fellows. Pretty
"Little Jane T." has
arrived.
```

### 429_0739_R · Friday, July 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0739/958,0,870,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We all spent the day
together, sailing,
playing lawn tennis,
jumping our horses &c.
```

### 429_0740_L · Saturday, July 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0740/0,0,971,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning took a long
ride with Emlen[?], teaching
our horses how to jump; then
rowed round Hog Island
with West. Spent the after-
noon with the whole party,
playing lawn tennis &c.
In the evening we all (about
20 fellows & girls, matrons &c)
went out for a moon-
light sail; coming home,
we danced, sang, told
stories &c. I am leading
the most delightful life
a fellow well could.
```

### 429_0740_R · Sunday, July 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0740/971,0,902,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Communion Sunday. I
like our simple Presby-
terian form for this
service so much; it always
makes me think - and I
generally do not do very
much thinking on serious
subjects.
```

### 429_0741_L · Monday, July 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0741/0,0,915,1523/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went up to ~~spen~~[?] spend
the night at the
Dodges (I shall return
tomorrow night). I like
and respect both Earl
and Cleave very much.
Went out driving, visit-
ing our old ~~[illegible]~~ places at
Mosholu and Riverdale.
Saw the two Pyne boys.
```
> Line 1 and line 8 each contain a short struck or blotted word.

### 429_0742_L · Wednesday, July 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0742/0,0,908,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed round Loyds
neck ^(16 miles)^. I am now
getting into beautiful
condition, and am deep
brown from the waist
up. In the afternoons
after Will has come
up from the city I
~~see~~[?] play lawn tennis,
wrestle, and swim with
him. I spend the
whole time out in the
open air; and at night
am always tired enough
to sleep like a top. Natu-
rally I am in magnifi-
cent health and spirits.
```

### 429_0742_R · Thursday, July 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0742/908,0,929,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alfred stayed up for the
day; we rowed around
Centre Island, played lawn
tennis, rode &c. I am now
training my horse to go
in harness. Gerty, Fing[?]
& Virginia Roosevelt are
staying with West; they
are very sweet girls; I
spent this evening over
there, dancing most of
the time.
```
> Second name in line 6 written same way as 'Fing[?]' on 0738_R.

### 429_0743_L · Friday, July 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0743/0,0,1003,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Emlen stayed up for a
holiday, spending the day
with me. We rowed round
Centre Island, and led
the usual active, out of
doors life; with at the
same ^time^ a delightful sense
of ease and absence of
care - that constitutes the
chief charm of Oyster Bay
~~fr~~[?] to me.
```

### 429_0743_R · Saturday, July 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0743/1003,0,854,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Elliott stayed up for a
holiday; so I spent the
day with him, sailing,
playing lawn tennis &c.
Am teaching ^my^ the horse to
trot, as I think of put-
ting him in a tilbury
next winter.
```
> 'my' written above 'the', apparently as a correction; 'the' not clearly struck.

### 429_0744_R · Monday, July 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0744/982,0,854,1514/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rowed across the sound
to Sheffield Island
light and back, a
distance of 30 miles.
Going over there was no
wind and the tide was
with me, so I took but
2 3/4 hours; coming back
I took 4 1/2 hours, as
there was a heavy head
wind and I shipped
a good deal of water.
In the evening we
danced, played charades
&c - there being several
fellows and girls staying
in the house.
```

### 429_0745_R · Friday, July 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0745/1012,0,816,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Alfred, West and I started
off for a weeks cruise on
the Meteor - a 24[?] foot
jib & mainsail open boat.
Our provisions consisted of
hard tack, canned
meats & coffee.
We started in the
afternoon at 3.30 with
a light wind on our
quarter, which failed
us at 8.30, when we
anchored off the mouth
of the Saugatuck river.
This trip we have sailors
hammocks, which we sling
in the back[?] pit[?] - a great
improvement on our former way
of sleeping on the boards
```
> Heavy show-through overlaps the writing. Entry continues onto next page.

### 429_0746_L · Saturday, July 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0746/0,0,955,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Weighed anchor at 5.30.
The winds were not
very heavy - and dead
ahead. During the course of
the day I saw a good
many terns and gulls
and a ~~few~~[?] coots - also
a stormy petrel. In the
afternoon the wind died
away altogether, and we
came to anchor off the
Thimble Islands. About
nine in the evening
however it breezed up so
from the southward, that
we had to run under the
lee of one of the islands
for shelter.
```
> Heavy show-through interleaved with writing.

### 429_0746_R · Sunday, July 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0746/955,0,873,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Under way 5.30. I don't
like sailing on Sunday
but we are short of water
& must reach New London.
The wind was about
north east, so that we had
to beat the whole time;
it grew heavier and heavier,
kicking up a big sea,
so that off Bartletts
reef we had our hands
full to keep the boat
going ahead and right
side up; we took a great
deal of water aboard.
Reached New London
4 p.m.
```

### 429_0747_L · Monday, July 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0747/0,0,909,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the morning we
sailed round to the
Chapins house where we
took dinner; in the
afternoon started back,
against a strong
S. W. wind; at sunset
we put into Saybrook
harbour. Saw a shark
(5[?] or [illegible]) and a good
many porpoises - all
serving as targets
for our revolvers.
```
> Line 10 obscured by an ink blot and show-through.

### 429_0747_R · Tuesday, July 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0747/909,0,926,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 5. a.m. we broke out
of Saybrook; we had a
great deal of difficulty
getting out over the
shoals at the mouth of
the harbour, and through
the tide rips, as there was
a strong south west wind
and a big sea. It
rained all the morning;
at noon the breeze
died away leaving a
heavy sea running.
At 7.30 we came
to anchor under the
lee of Charles Island
```

### 429_0748_L · Wednesday, July 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0748/0,0,995,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Under way 5.15.; the
winds were light and
baffling, and it was
a dead calm from 9
till 6.; so, though we
worked well at the
sweeps, we did not
reach Oyster Bay till
11.15.
Al & Doc are not
only good friends but
extremely pleasant
companions. The trip
has been a great success.
```

### 429_0749_R · Saturday, July 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0749/1018,0,839,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am leading the most
healthful, pleasant, out of
doors life - driving, riding
rowing, sailing, swimming,
walking & shooting, ~~&~~
all day long. Two days
out of three one of the
boys - Will, Em, Al or
West - stays up with
me. I wish Johnny Elliott
were here. By Jove, these
five are friends worth
having.
In the evening we
read, play whist, or dance.
A pretty little Miss Hale,
a Philadelphian, is staying
with Aunt Lizzie; I take her
out rowing quite often
```
> Entry may continue on following page.

### 429_0750_R · Wednesday, July 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0750/966,0,849,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-07-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Nellie stayed up from
town, and so I spent the
day with him; we rowed
round Loyds - ~~[illegible]~~ 15 miles,
and virtually racing the
whole way. As athletes we
are about equal; he rows
best; I run best; he
can beat me sailing
or swimming; I can beat
him wrestling or boxing;
I am best with the
rifle, he with the shot
gun, &c, &c, &c.
```

### 429_0751_R · Friday, August 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0751/977,0,829,1515/full/0/default.jpg
