*1879-08-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out woodcock
shooting with young
Pendleton Cruger. We
tramped pretty steadily
from 7[?] a.m till 7
p.m, with pretty good
success. He is a pretty
good shot — I am
not. It was very
hot, and the covert
was frightful, making
it very hard walking;
but we were rather
cooled by the nature
of the ground, which
necessitated our keeping
wet from the waist down,
in the mud holes, swamps &c.
7 woodcock.
```

### 429_0752_L · Monday, August 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0752/0,0,972,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went out woodcock
shooting with Cruger.
We first shot through
Platts[?] swamp, getting half
a dozen; then went to
Townsend's, where we killed
two brace; picked up a
couple in the Beekman's;
and then went to
several little swamps
and wet, boggy
places, near Locust
Valley; picking up two
or three more birds. I
really shot very well
today — rather an
unusual incident with
me
10 woodcock.
```

### 429_0752_R · Tuesday, August 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0752/972,0,856,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
One of the prettiest girls
in Oyster Bay, and
one of the most charming
is Miss Emily Swan.
I have had several
very pleasant rows
and rides with her.
```

### 429_0753_R · Thursday, August 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0753/966,0,862,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Cruger and I went out
in the skiff after[?]
snipe; rowing along
the beaches, paddling
and poling through
the broad, shallow
lagoons, and narrow,
winding creeks of
the marshes, and using
the little lug sail
when ever the wind
favoured us. We had to
make several portages.
We came out of Loyds[?] neck
with the tide, which runs
very rapidly out of the creek[?],
so that we needed to do
nothing but steer & shoot.
8 snipe 1 heron
```

### 429_0754_R · Monday, August 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0754/967,0,870,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I row almost every
day — either to Loyds
neck or round Centre
Island. If any of
the boys stay up I
generally play lawn
tennis with them.
I often take Motherling
out walking, or ~~one~~
Bysie or Pussie out
rowing. I am teaching
pretty Miss Emily
Swan to play
lawn tennis.
```

### 429_0755_L · Thursday, August 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0755/0,0,979,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went woodcock shooting
with Cruger. The
birds were moulting,
however, and were very
much scattered; moreover
we shot badly; and
so, though we tramped
about all day, our
bag was small
```

*1879-08-14 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 woodcock.
```

### 429_0756_L · Saturday, August 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0756/0,0,970,1556/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
My marks for last
year arrived; my
average is over 87%,
which is higher than
either of my first two
years. I am more than
satisfied with it. In
Zoölogy and Political
Economy I lead every
body.
In the afternoon,
after Nell has arrived,
I always play lawn
tennis with him — after
which we generally get
a swim. My horse be-
-gins to go quite well
in Harness. I shall keep a
dogcart & sleigh next winter[?]
```
> last word runs to edge of page

### 429_0756_R · Sunday, August 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0756/970,0,848,1556/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Every Sunday
we have morning and
evening prayers; I wish
we could have them
every day.
Motherling is just
too sweet and pretty
for anything; and
I doubt if there
ever were two more
^lovely^ ~~noble~~ and unselfish
girls than dear,
noble Bysie, and
darling little
Pussie. These three, and
my best friend, old brother
Nelly give me as happy
a home as a man could
possibly have.
```
> 'lovely' written in left margin over/beside struck 'noble'

### 429_0757_L · Monday, August 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0757/0,0,982,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Tomorrow I leave for the
Maine woods; shall stop a
day or two at the Salton-
-stalls on the way. I have
had a lovely summer so far;
and am brown as a nut,
and in capital training.
The family, the cousins &
everybody are just too sweet
to me for anything.
I am thinking pretty seriously
as to what I shall do when
I leave College; I shall probably
either pursue a scientific course,
or else study law, prepar
-atory to going into public
life.
```

### 429_0757_R · Tuesday, August 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0757/982,0,843,1534/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went in to New York.
I wonder if there
could be two better
guardians than Uncle
Jim Roosevelt and
Uncle Jimmie Gracie!
I can't imagine it.
I dined at the
University Club with
Robinson, Haddon,
Stratton, Sedgwick
and Kingsford. We
had a very jolly
dinner; they are
all capital fellows.
```

### 429_0758_L · Wednesday, August 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0758/0,0,913,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went on to Boston;
and came out here
to Chestnut Hill, where
dear old Dick gave
me the most hearty
and enthusiastic greeting.
Dined with Mrs. Lee.
Then we drove over to the
Whitneys, who were just
as sweet and cordial as
they could be; Bessie
looked very pretty. Rose,
Rosy and Alice were
staying there. Tomorrow
is Ell's[?] birthday, and we
are going on a picnic, with
a whole crowd of fellows and
girls — the Guilds[?], Bacons, Sharps[?],
Greens, Sears, Coolidges, Codmans &c.
```

### 429_0758_R · Thursday, August 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0758/913,0,924,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
At nine oclock ^a.m.^ Dick
and I drove over to
the Whitneys, and did
not return till
after midnight. I
had a lovely day,
sailing, walking, driving,
dancing, playing lawn
tennis &c. I really
heartily enjoyed seeing
dear, honest Rose,
pretty little Rosy Lee,
sweet Alice, and [illegible]
pretty Bessie, again —
especially Rose and Alice.
The Whitneys are exceeding
-ly hospitable and
cordial.
```
> word after 'and' obliterated by ink blot

### 429_0759_L · Friday, August 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0759/0,0,903,1556/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove over to Cambridge
in the morning. Went
in town to see the
girls — Rose, Rosy and
Alice — off to the Glades[?].
They wanted me very
much to go down to
stay a few days with
them; and they were
all so cordial, and
Alice was so bewitching
-ly pretty that it was
frightfully hard to
refuse; I took the
night train for Matta-
-wamkeag[?] however,
as I agreed to meet
Emlen. Dined at the
Union Club with Frank Lee.
```
> ink blot over start of 'frightfully'

### 429_0759_R · Saturday, August 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0759/903,0,915,1556/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Reached Mattawamkeag[?]
at 10 a.m. and
drove over to Island
Falls with Dave
Sewall. Emlen and
Mr. Cutler are here,
and I shall make a
short trip with
them to Katahdin
```

### 429_0760_L · Sunday, August 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0760/0,0,905,1536/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the day chiefly in
preparations. I have 2
complete changes of
clothes, & plenty of
handkerchiefs & woolen
socks. I dress in a flannel
shirt, a light, strong
duck trousers & heavy
underflannels; carry a
heavy jacket & a blanket;
and have my necessaries
in a small bag. I have
taken both rifle &
shot gun.
```

### 429_0760_R · Monday, August 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0760/905,0,903,1536/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Paddled with Emlen
down to our old
camping ground at
the foot of the
Lake and back
— about 25 miles.
Hunted ^for^ partridges,
but found none.
Picked up a duck
in the thoroughfare.
Em & Arthur are
capital fellows for
a trip of this
kind — unselfish,
good natured, and
not minding a
little fatigue &
hardship.
```

*1879-08-25 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 wood duck.
```

### 429_0761_L · Tuesday, August 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0761/0,0,895,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Emlen, Arthur Cutler,
Will Dow, Will Sewall[?]
& I started for Mt.
Katahdin. Drove 23
miles; and then carried
our packs about 10,
when we went into
camp. I carried about
45 lbs, including my
gun & cartridges.
Crossing a stream I
lost one of my
shoes ~~crossing a stream~~;
fortunately I had
brought a pair of
moccasins tied to
my pack.
```

### 429_0761_R · Wednesday, August 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0761/895,0,913,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Walked up to the
head of Katahdin ~~Lake~~
Lake where we camped.
I get along very well
with my pack. Killed
4 ducks in a logan,
after a rather neat
stalk; also picked up
a couple of partridges.
We caught a few trout.
Black flies are pretty
^bad^ ~~frightfully~~; but they do not
bother us at night; ~~and~~
It is very pleasant & like
old time[?] in the evenings,
round the roaring logs
of the camp fire.
```
> struck word at end of line 13 read as 'and', uncertain

*1879-08-27 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2[?] ruffed grouse 1 wood duck 3 black duck
```

### 429_0762_L · Thursday, August 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0762/0,0,932,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
After lunch we
started for
Katahdin; (before I
had tramped about 5
miles after partridges). We
caught about ^100^ ~~[illegible]~~ trout
at Sandy brook; then
got lost, and after
tramping through
frightful ground till
after dark camped out
by a small water
hole; wet, tired and
hungry — but happy.
There are plenty of ^fresh^ tracks
of both bear & caribou,
but we saw nothing living,
except the usual woodpeckers,
chickadees, jays, &c &c
```
> '100' written above an overwritten/struck number

### 429_0762_R · Friday, August 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0762/932,0,896,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started before daybreak,
walking straight through
the woods, & then up Katahdin;
it was very difficult walking, &
both Emlen & Arthur gave out
before reaching the ~~top~~ ^summit^,
the view from which was beauti-
-ful. I find I can ^endure fatigue^ ~~tire out~~[?]
& hardship pretty nearly as well
as these lumbermen. Coming
back we followed a spotted
trail which sometimes set
at fault even the two
skilled backwoodsmen. Reached
our camp at Katahdin Lake about
dark, having caught about
60[?] trout. It is raining & we
are all soaked through —
but in excellent health & spirits.
```

### 429_0763_L · Saturday, August 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0763/0,0,926,1507/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the morning
walked half way
round the lake
but saw nothing;
there are ^very few^ ~~few~~ partridges
and ~~not a[?]~~ few
ducks round here;
larger game is
not scarce, but
almost impossible
to get at. In the
afternoon walked
some distance down
beside Sandy Brook;
coming home killed
a duck in Moose
Pond. Trout of small
size are very plentiful.
```

*1879-08-30 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 black duck
```

### 429_0763_R · Sunday, August 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0763/926,0,892,1507/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-08-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Loafed about camp,
cleaning guns, mending
clothes, ~~cleaning~~ bathing
in the lake &c. You get
pretty dirty in camp.
Black flies have been
very numerous this trip,
and have been a great
annoyance to the others;
funnily enough they
do not bother me very
much. There are plenty
of fish round here; game is
very scarce; but I
am enjoying myself
exceedingly
```

### 429_0764_L · Monday, September 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0764/0,0,942,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Was up before sunrise,
and took a trip to[?]
round some barrens and
bogs; crippled a duck
in a logan, but it
crawled off among the
rushes. In the afternoon
we shouldered our
packs, broke camp &
started for Island Falls.
After crossing the
Wissaticook[?] went into
camp for the night.
Am in beautiful condition;
& find I can walk,
wrestle or shoot with
most of these lumbermen.
```

### 429_0764_R · Tuesday, September 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0764/942,0,873,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started in good
season, walking out
to the East Branch of
the Penobscot, which we
crossed, & then drove to
Island Falls. I have
enjoyed the trip ex-
-ceedingly; the boys
are most pleasant
companions.
```

### 429_0765_L · Wednesday, September 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0765/0,0,935,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the afternoon
driving about in a
buckboard looking
for partridges; but
saw none.
```

### 429_0765_R · Thursday, September 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0765/935,0,893,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day pre-
-paring for my Mun-
-sungun trip; I shall
go in a canoe, ~~with~~
alone with Seawall.
For provisions I
take tea, pork and
hardtack ^& some flour^; we have
a shelter tent, two
blankets & some cooking
utensils; & one complete
change of underclothing
each. I take 50
cartridges for the rifle
and 100 for the shot-
gun. I shall only
use moccasins
```

### 429_0766_L · Friday, September 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0766/0,0,928,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started at 5 a.m in a
rough wagon to drive to the
Oxbow of the Aroostook ^River^ —
46 miles distant. We
reached it 6 p.m.; and
are staying in a regular
backwoods house — fare
and ~~accom~~ sleeping ac-
-comadations being both
primative to a
degree. I killed a spruce
partridge and a red tailed
hawk, from the wagon.
The rout all day long
was through a sparsely
settled, thickly wooded
country & for about 3 miles
through a dreary waste of burnt
land.
```

*1879-09-05 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 Canada Grouse.
```

### 429_0766_R · Saturday, September 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0766/928,0,880,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started in fair[?] season
in a pirogue or dugout.
It stands rough usage
better than a ~~canoe~~ ^birch^ canoe.
We went about 20 miles
up the Aroostook, pad-
-dling sometimes, but
poleing most of the
way. The scenery is
very beautiful and
wild; I saw no trace
of man — but also no
trace of game. Trout
are plenty however.
~~[illegible]~~ Pitched camp before
dark, to cook the bread, trout
& partridge. Black flies, mos-
-quitoes & midges pretty
plentiful; I don't mind
them much.
```
> last line written below the ruled frame

### 429_0767_L · Sunday, September 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0767/0,0,926,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We started as usual, as
there was no use ~~to~~[?] of
laying up; but I
compromised by not
shooting or fishing.
We poled up the Aroostook
till lunch time, when
we were near the
mouth of the Mun-
-sungun; up this we
had to wade, dragging
our boats — the water
now up to ^our^ ankles, now
to our hips. It was
heavy work; moreover
it was raining heavily, and
towards dusk we pitched
camp, drenched through
& tired out. Midges bad.
```

### 429_0767_R · Monday, September 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0767/926,0,889,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained hard all day. We
started early; for several hours
it was rapid, shoal water,
through which we waded, drag-
-ging the heavy dugout over
the rocks and shallows; then we
got into deeper, dead water,
but this was nearly as bad
owing to the beaver dams
and log jams, which we had
to cut through or pull round.
There were some falls we had to
get up, taking every thing out of
the boat; then we poled up through
more dead water; then paddled
through the lower Munsungun Lake,
& half way up the middle one, where we
camped. Tired out, & wet through, hungry
& cold — but am having a lovely time.
But no trace of game.
```
> last line written below the ruled frame

### 429_0768_L · Tuesday, September 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0768/0,0,922,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained all day; but we
paddled up the middle
lake and then walked
(through most frightful
ground) to the head of the
upper one. On the way I
saw a few partridges; an
old moose track, a recent bear
track; and a few old signs
of deer and caribou. On
the lake are a few loons
and fish ducks. As game
is so scarce I shall go right
back to Island Falls, instead
of staying up here, as I
had intended. Am wet
through, as usual, and ^rather^ tired;
but, although the work is very
hard, I am enjoying the
trip greatly.  3 ruffed grouse.
```
> tally '3 ruffed grouse.' on same last line, below ruled frame

### 429_0768_R · Wednesday, September 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0768/922,0,896,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rose before daybreak &
started before sunrise, down
~~Pad[?]~~ the lake. Paddled
through the lake and
deadwater, running the
falls; then waded down
through the Munsungun
quick water, lunching
where we camped Sun-
-day night; then we
poled, (making fine time
down the swift water
and only occasionally
having to get out and
wade) until, early in the
afternoon we reached our
Saturday camp where
we are now. I am very fond
of the evenings round the
camp fire, beneath the [illegible] tent[?]
```
> last line written below the ruled frame

### 429_0769_L · Thursday, September 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0769/0,0,919,1538/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started in fair season,
paddling down stream
in great style; on the
way I shot a wood
duck. At the Oxbow
we disembarked and
walked about 15
miles (~~most[?]~~ ^half^ of the
way across country)
to a rough backwoods
house, where we are now.
Fare pretty rough, but
plenty of good milk, and
we have shot our own
meat — as, besides the
duck, I killed a rabbit
and ^a^ partridge on the way.
```

*1879-09-11 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 red rabbit
1 wood duck 1 ruffed grouse
```

### 429_0769_R · Friday, September 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0769/919,0,902,1538/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Walked off through the
woods to a pond
in a cranberry
bog, and back; in all
rather over 20 miles.
Bill shot a duck
in the pond and
picked[?] up 2 partridges
and a hare on
the way. The walking
was pretty good.
```
> lines 8-9 in fainter ink

*1879-09-12 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 black duck 2 ruffed grouse
```

### 429_0770_L · Saturday, September 13, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0770/0,0,938,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked down to
Island Falls, through
the woods — about 25
miles. The walking
was pretty ^good^, the
scenery beautiful
— for besides the
forests, grand enough
in themselves, we
passed several water-
-falls in deep ravines —
and I shot several
partridges. Wore one
of my moccasins almost
out, which made me
rather footsore.
```

*1879-09-13 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
5 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0770_R · Sunday, September 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0770/938,0,880,1517/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to "church", read
my bible, and spent
the rest of the
day shaving, bathing
and getting things
ready for a new
expedition. I always like
these backwoods meeting
houses; and I don't know
a better or more intelli-
-gent race of men than
these shrewd, plucky,
honest Yankees — all
of them hunters, lum-
-bermen or small
farmers.
```

### 429_0771_L · Monday, September 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0771/0,0,938,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained all day,
so did not start.
```

### 429_0771_R · Tuesday, September 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0771/938,0,873,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
started with Bill, in
a rough wagon with a
rougher horse; the roads
were rougher still; and
the cabin we are now
staying in, after going
about 30 miles, is
roughest of all.
```

### 429_0772_L · Wednesday, September 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0772/0,0,935,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked about 25
miles on through the
woods, to a clearing
kept by a man named
Littlefield - where we
were very well treated.
Shot one spruce partridge
in a low spruce
thicket, and another
in a tamarack
swamp.
```

*1879-09-17 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 spruce Grouse.
```

### 429_0772_R · Thursday, September 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0772/935,0,876,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked back through the
woods to where we had
left our horse, killing
4 partridges; then drove
to Island[?] Falls, shooting
a pigeon from the
wagon. As usual, it
rained; the weather
has been awful, for
the last two weeks;
but I am enjoying
myself exceedingly,
~~and~~ am in superb
health, and as tough
as a pine knot.
```
> 'Island' written in an unusual form

*1879-09-18 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
4 Birch Grouse 1 Pigeon
```

### 429_0773_L · Friday, September 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0773/0,0,950,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked down through
the woods to the
foot of Mattawam-
keag lake where we
are now.
```

### 429_0773_R · Saturday, September 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0773/950,0,887,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
walked about 20 miles
through the woods; killed
2 partridges on a hemlock
ridge or "horseback", and
afterwards found a
flock of 8[?] on an
"interval", of which I
killed 7. In the after-
noon we practiced
with our riffles; I shot
very well, but not as
well as Dow and
Seawell.
```

*1879-09-20 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
9 ruffed grouse.
```

### 429_0774_L · Sunday, September 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0774/0,0,925,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Paddled up to Island
Falls, to stay over night.
Packed up for good, as
I start tomorrow. A
porcupine blundered into
camp, and was ignomin-
iously expelled with
the tin dipper. What
glorious fun I am
having this year! No
fellow ever had a better
time than I have.
And my life has such
absurd contrasts. At one time
I live in the height of
luxury; and then for a
month will undergo really
severe toil and hardship
-and I enjoy both extremes
almost equally.
```
> last line written below the printed frame

### 429_0774_R · Monday, September 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0774/925,0,873,1515/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Seawall, Dow & I started
in a birch down the
Mattawamkeag. We paddled
down stream about 10
miles below the lake,
landing several times
to look for partridges. Dow
and I, by making a
circuit through the woods,
ambushed a flock of
fish ducks, of which ~~I~~ we
killed several; I had
several very long shots
- at loons, ^& cranes^ with the
rifle, but missed them.
I shall be ^very^ sorry to end
this trip; and yet how
I shall enjoy getting back
to Harvard.
```
> first word partly obscured by blot; reading 'Seawall' from later pages

*1879-09-22 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 Merganser.
```

### 429_0775_L · Tuesday, September 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0775/0,0,937,1471/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started tolerably late,
paddling down to
below Wytopitlock[?], and
then camping. We had
great fun with ducks
in the stream and
pigeons along the
banks, Dow killing
several; but for some
reason or other I
shot badly, missing
most of my birds.
However I redeemed this
by making some good
shots with the rifle
at Hawks and owls.
Shot a muskrat; both it & the
fishducks were very ^good^ eating!
```

*1879-09-23 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 merganser 1 Dipper duck 1 pigeon
```

### 429_0775_R · Wednesday, September 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0775/937,0,864,1471/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Paddled down through the
dead water, about 12
miles, to Kingman,
where I took the
train for Boston.
I was really sorry
to leave both Seawall
and Dow; I have
had capital fun this
trip, and have passed
as pleasant a
month as a fellow
could. Am feeling
as strong as a
bull.
By Jove, it sometimes
seems as if I were having
too happy a time to have
it last. I enjoy every
moment I live, almost.
```
> final punctuation unclear (period or colon); last lines run to bottom edge

### 429_0776_L · Thursday, September 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0776/0,0,932,1490/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It is ever so pleasant
to get back again
among all the
fellows, who greeted
me with uproarious
cordiality. I am
keeping my horse
and dogcart at
Pikes Stable; I
have really got
a very stylish
turnout. I intend
to have lovely fun
this winter.
```

### 429_0776_R · Friday, September 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0776/932,0,869,1490/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Dick over to
Chestnut Hill; they
were all so heartily
glad to see me that
I felt as if I had
come home.
```

### 429_0777_L · Saturday, September 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0777/0,0,932,1507/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove Harry Chapin
over to a lawn
tennis party at
Minot Welds; Rose,
Rosy, Alice & the two
Miss Lanes, Misses
Bacon &c[?] were there.
In the evening went
out to Chestnut
Hill; to pass Sunday
with the Saltonstalls.
My cart is the
greatest success of the
season.
I brought Phil a gun,
Cotty some soldiers, a gold
pin for Rose and a
silver one for Mamie.
```
> show-through heavy over first lines; '&c' uncertain; 'Minot Welds' read with help of 1 Oct entry

### 429_0777_R · Sunday, September 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0777/932,0,876,1507/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am having a
lovely time. Dear
Mr and Mrs Saltonstall
are just too sweet for
anything, and the
girls are as lovely
as ever,
In the afternoon
took Rose out walking.
```

### 429_0778_L · Monday, September 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0778/0,0,931,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
From 8.30 till 10 I study
Italian. 10-11; Italian
Recitation. 11-2 study
Political Economy. 2-3, Pol.
Econ. recitation. 6 hours work.
I am going to give an
abstract of my studies
each day this week —
not counting theses &c.
I am perfectly delighted
with the horse and
cart; it is as swell
a turnout as I know.
```

### 429_0778_R · Tuesday, September 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0778/931,0,880,1500/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-09-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
9[?]-1 Steady[?] work at my
zoology course in the
Museum. 2-5 Fieldwork
in Geology. 8-9 study
Political economy. 8 hours.
Called on the Lanes in
the evening; they both
are just as sweet as
they can be, and Miss
Lulu[?] is too pretty
for anything.
```
> first figure (8 or 9) and second word unclear; film dark

### 429_0779_L · Wednesday, October 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0779/0,0,944,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8.30-10 Study Italian. 10-11
Recite Italian. 11-12 Study
~~Polecon~~ Pol. Econ. 12-1
Geology &[?] Lecture; 2-3
Pol. Econ recitation
6 hours work.
~~Almost[?]~~ Every afternoon I go
out in the cart, gener-
ally taking some
fellow with me, unless
I go visiting.
Of all the fellows here,
Dick Saltonstall is my
most intimate friend,
(Harry Shaw coming next?)
then Minot Weld and Harry
Chapin & John Tebbets[?] &c. Old
Dick I place on a par
with the Roosevelts.
```
> '&' before Lecture may be a superscript insertion

### 429_0779_R · Thursday, October 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0779/944,0,867,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
^8½^
9-1. Zoology Course.
1.30-3.00. Study Pol. Econ
6 hours.
Dick and I drove over
in my cart to the
Whitneys, where we passed
a very pleasant evening.
The drive home was
delightful, there being a
most beautiful full
moon.
```
> '8½' written small above the '9', apparently a correction of the start time

### 429_0780_L · Friday, October 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0780/0,0,933,1538/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8.30-10.00, Study Italian.
10-11, Recite Italian. 11-12
study Pol. Econ. 12-1.
Geology Lecture. 2-3 Pol.
Econ. Recitation. 6 hours work.
Drove over to the
Saltonstalls where I took
dinner; pretty Rosy Lee
was there, also Mary
Tuckerman and a most
charming girl, Harriet
Lawrence.
```
> mark after '12-1' unclear

### 429_0780_R · Saturday, October 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0780/933,0,878,1538/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
9-11 Zoology. 11-1 Review
Geology. 4 hours work. In
all I work 36 hours a
week - generally a little
more.
Took Minot out for a
long drive in my "cart.
I shall enjoy this "cart"
more than I have
anything yet.
In the evening we
had a great spree up
in the Club; - so I did
not get to bed till
awfully late.
```

### 429_0781_L · Sunday, October 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0781/0,0,923,1529/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon walked
in town with Harry
Chapin to call on the
Hoopers.
Recommenced our usual
Sunday morning Porcellian
breakfasts.
```

### 429_0782_R · Wednesday, October 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0782/928,0,883,1519/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I go out in my cart
every afternoon; the
drives round here are
beautiful, and my
horse really trots very
fast.
Am training for
running; go about
a mile every day
at a fair rate —
say 5.15
```

### 429_0783_L · Thursday, October 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0783/0,0,936,1524/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove over with
Dick, to call at
Chestnut Hill, but
only stayed a very
short time, as I
had to get back
to go to the theatre
with Harry Shaw.
```
> heavy mirrored show-through interleaved with the lines

### 429_0783_R · Friday, October 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0783/936,0,894,1524/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I have my hands alto
gether too full of
society work, being
Librarian of the Porcellian,
Secretary of the Pudding,
Treasurer of the O.K.,
Vice-President of the Nat.
Hist Soc. & President of
the A.D.Φ; Editor of the Advocate.
The evenings I
generally spend in the
Club, playing whist
or billiards, often ending
off with a little
supper.
```
> '&' before 'President' slightly unclear; Greek letter phi in 'A.D.Φ'

### 429_0784_L · Saturday, October 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0784/0,0,942,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Had a great Porc. supper;
Dick S. and Jack Tebbets
came into the club. The
former slept in my room
- to the great detriment
thereof, Dick being a
most obstreperous man
when sprung!
```
> show-through heavy; 'Tebbets' and 'man' less certain

### 429_0784_R · Sunday, October 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0784/942,0,894,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Dick being decidedly under
the weather from last nights
spree, I drove him over to
Chestnut Hill, after break-
fasting in the Porc. about
11 oclock. I dined at
the Saltonstalls, and went
out walking with Rosy
Lee; and ^with^ Rose afterwards.
```
> writing smudged/blotted in places; 'decidedly' and 'dined' read with some uncertainty

### 429_0785_L · Monday, October 13, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0785/0,0,943,1529/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Jack Tebbetts
over to call on the
Miss Bacons. The
horse really trots
very well and the
cart rides as easily
as a cradle.
```
> heavy show-through

### 429_0785_R · Thursday, October 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0785/943,0,878,1529/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dick and I took the
two Miss Lanes to
call on the Chinese
Professor and had a
most amusing time.
```

### 429_0786_L · Friday, October 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0786/0,0,962,1520/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Minot Weld home,
calling on George Minot
and stopping at the
Saltonstalls for a
minute to congratulate
Mamie on her birthday.
```

### 429_0786_R · Saturday, October 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0786/962,0,865,1520/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the morning play-
ing Tennis with the
Miss Lanes. In the after-
noon I drove Harry Guild
over in my cart to his
house, to spend Sunday.
I like the Guilds very
much.
```

### 429_0787_L · Sunday, October 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0787/0,0,964,1526/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked home from Church
with the Miss Bacons;
in the afternoon drove
over to the Whitneys.
```

### 429_0787_R · Monday, October 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0787/964,0,854,1526/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The drive home from
the Guilds was just
perfection; it was a
divine morning and
the little horse went
like Sancho[?].
In the afternoon drove
Van R. over to the
Saltonstalls; we went out
walking with the two
girls.
```
> last word of line 6 unclear: 'Sancho' or 'Sanche'

### 429_0788_R · Wednesday, October 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0788/968,0,859,1555/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Harry Shaw and I gave
an opera party to Mr
and Mrs. Saltonstall,
Rose and Alice. I
drove him over to the
Saltonstalls to dinner.
We did not get out,
after the Opera, till
towards 12; the horse
was very restive at
starting, and finally
reared and fell,
pitching Harry and me
out of the cart. Nothing
was hurt however.
```

### 429_0789_L · Thursday, October 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0789/0,0,944,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Charley Morgan out
driving.
The evenings I
generally spend playing
whist in the club;
unless we make up
a theatre party. As
I am rather apt
to end up with a
little supper, I find I
dont get to bed very
early.
```
> 'dont' may have an apostrophe obscured by the flourish

### 429_0789_R · Friday, October 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0789/944,0,864,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Minot Weld over
to his house; the horse
goes well except that
he plunges and rears
when starting, and he
sometimes shys.
Took the night boat
for New York.
```

### 429_0790_L · Saturday, October 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0790/0,0,932,1520/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Breakfasted and lunched
at Aunt Lizzie's; they are
just too sweet to me
for anything.
Went out to Oyster
Bay in the afternoon.
Aunt Annie is staying
with us. It is perfectly
lovely to see all the
family again; and they
are too lovely to me
for anything.
```

### 429_0790_R · Sunday, October 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0790/932,0,879,1520/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Passed the usual dear
old Oyster Bay Sunday;
Family Prayers, late
breakfast, Church,
taking a long walk in
the afternoon, and after
late tea spending most of
the evening in singing
hymns.
```
> 'late' at start of line 7 begins outside the left printed rule

### 429_0791_L · Monday, October 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0791/0,0,938,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
My 21st birthday. Uncle
Jim, Aunt Lizzie, Aunt
Annie, Doc, Em & Al came
out to stay with us. In the
evening a good many girls
-including pretty Emily Swan-
came to tea, and we had great
fun dancing, playing charades
&c &c. My presents were very
valuable, and all are just
too lovely to me for anything.
I have had so much happiness in
my life so far that I feel,
no matter what sorrows come,
the joys will have overbalanced
them. And yet on this day I can
not help thinking with sadness about
Father, my best and most loved and
revered friend; may God help me to live
as he would have wished.
```
> last line written below the ruled frame

### 429_0791_R · Tuesday, October 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0791/938,0,880,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day playing
tennis, walking and rowing.
```

### 429_0792_L · Wednesday, October 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0792/0,0,940,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Early in the morning
started out for a 12 hour
tramp with dog and gun.
I had pretty good luck
in the morning with
rabbit and woodcock: in
the afternoon I found a
large covey of quail in a
thick wood & I followed them
till after moonrise, but the
birds were wild & the covert
- a dense cat-briar jungle which
scratched my hands and
arms ~~into a~~ ^till my^ shirt became soaked
with blood - was thick, and I
shot badly, so I only killed
two.
1 gray squirrel 2 gray rabbit
3 woodcock 2 quail.
```
> line 14: 'into a' appears struck with 'till my' written above; reading of correction uncertain

### 429_0792_R · Thursday, October 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0792/940,0,861,1509/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out sailing in the sound
for ducks. There were but
few birds, and these were very
shy; the wind, moreover, blew
so that with the jib bobbed
and three reefs in the mainsail
it was all I could do to
keep the clumsy old fishboat
going; so I did not get a
shot. In the bay I found
a stray loon who succumbed
after a great deal of powder
had been wasted.
1 loon.
```
> '1 loon.' tally at foot of page

### 429_0793_L · Friday, October 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0793/0,0,964,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-10-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took an old gunner, "Ramson",
and drove off to an old
farm house about eight miles
off; spent six or seven[?] hours
walking through the woods
and stubble fields. the
quail were plenty; but
scattered easily and had to
be whistled together.
In the evening went to a
very pleasant "All-Hallow-Een"
party at the Swans, where we
had great fun, going through
the canonical rites and
dancing besides.
3 rabbit 3 woodcock 9 quail.
```
> line 4: 'six or seven' uncertain, second word appears written 'even'; tally at foot of page

### 429_0793_R · Saturday, November 1, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0793/964,0,842,1525/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
[illegible] Went out shooting,
spending the whole day
tramping about through the
woods and fields. Game was
plenty but I shot badly.
This week it has been superb,
clear, cool autumn weather; I
have enjoyed the shooting very
much; and the evenings too,
spent in the cosy parlor, by
the bright wood fire, reading,
or playing whist with darling
mother and the two sweet girls.
Dear old Nell came up this
evening & how I wish we could
be more together.
2 squirrel 3 rabbit 5 quail.
```
> first word unclear (looks like 'Uken'/'Then'); tally at foot of page

### 429_0794_L · Sunday, November 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0794/0,0,962,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon I left
Oyster Bay with much
reluctance and came in to
New York to spend the
night at Uncle Jim's
In the evening went to
call on the Lees, who are
staying here; I can do
nothing for them myself,
but the Roosevelts have
come up to time nobly,
and they have spreads on
hand for every evening of
next week.
```
> 'Lees' and 'spreads' read with reasonable certainty

### 429_0794_R · Monday, November 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0794/962,0,852,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on to Boston.
```

### 429_0795_L · Tuesday, November 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0795/0,0,956,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started studying with a
will; by the end of this
week I shall make up
all I have lost.
```

### 429_0795_R · Wednesday, November 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0795/956,0,864,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Harry Shaw over to
the Lymans; afterwards
dined at the Saltonstalls.
```

### 429_0796_L · Saturday, November 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0796/0,0,988,1536/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove over to the Salton-
stalls in the morning,
and thence over to
the Whitneys, where I
took dinner and had
an exceedingly pleasant
time; Miss. Bessie I like
more and more. I then
drove over to Harry
Minots where I am
going to spend the
night. Passed the evening
playing whist and
billiards with the
Minot, Whitney and
Guild boys.
```

### 429_0796_R · Sunday, November 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0796/988,0,892,1536/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove back to Cambridge
in time for my Sunday
School. Passed the evening
very pleasantly with
pretty Lulu Lane.
```

### 429_0797_L · Monday, November 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0797/0,0,961,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove over in the after-
-noon to the Lees where
I dined and spent a
very pleasant evening,
not returning till after
eleven.
The horse and cart
go beautifully.
```

### 429_0798_L · Wednesday, November 12, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0798/0,0,937,1558/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove Minot Weld over
to the Saltonstalls; we
went out walking with
Rosy and Rose.
I spend most of
my spare time in
Cambridge in the club;
and I also go a good
deal to the A.D.C.[?], where
the fellows are very pleasant
and intellectual - a rare
thing with college boys.
I have been off on a ~~good~~ ^great^
many sprees this year, and have
been studying well and keeping
late hours; thanks to the
magnificent condition I am in
after the summer I seem to stand
it pretty well.
```
> 'A.D.C.' final letter unclear; 'good' struck with 'great' written above

### 429_0799_L · Friday, November 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0799/0,0,948,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went on to New York
to see our football team
play Princeton. Jack
Tebbets and Harry Chapin
are going to stay with
me till Monday when
we will all return
with Bamie & Conie
who are going to spend
a week at Chestnut
Hill.
```
> 'Bamie & Conie' read with moderate confidence; first word 'Went' over-inked

### 429_0799_R · Saturday, November 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0799/948,0,883,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Our team was beaten.
I called on Grace Potter,
who is just as sweet as
ever and dear, ^pretty^ little
Fanny Smith dined with
us. Afterwards we went
to the theatre, then took
a supper at Delmonicos
and stayed up till
all hours in the Harvard
Club where a lot of
fellows were singing &c.
```

### 429_0800_L · Sunday, November 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0800/0,0,1011,1496/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the usual dear old
Sunday. the family are
delighted with Jack.
Lunched with Aunt
Annie. Had a most
delightful call on Edith
Carow; she is the most
cultivated, best read girl
I know.
```

### 429_0800_R · Monday, November 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0800/1011,0,884,1496/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Came on to Boston
with the girls. Class
meeting at night.
```
> faint writing; heavy show-through on rest of page

### 429_0801_L · Tuesday, November 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0801/0,0,991,1521/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rainy; so took a buggy with
Dick and drove over to the
Lees to dinner; a good many
fellows came in afterwards
and we had the jolliest
kind of a time, dancing
and talking. It seems very
natural to have the girls
at Chestnut Hill. Just
before starting back Dick
took a glass of brandy which
rather set him up so that
he insisted on galloping the
horse, which of course ran away
4 or 5[?] times; I nearly died
laughing.
```
> '4 or 5' partly in left margin, uncertain

### 429_0801_R · Wednesday, November 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0801/991,0,892,1521/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Jack Tebbetts
over to the Lees where
we took dinner and
spent the evening as
usual, dancing and
playing games.
```

### 429_0802_L · Thursday, November 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0802/0,0,964,1480/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Snowing heavily; Dick &
I drove over to the
Saltonstalls for dinner.
```

### 429_0802_R · Friday, November 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0802/964,0,881,1480/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dined at the Laurences, passing
a very pleasant evening.
```

### 429_0803_L · Saturday, November 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0803/0,0,996,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Gave a lunch party of 34 persons
up in the Porcellian to Bamie & Corinne.
It was the greatest success imaginable
```

*1879-11-22 · table · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
left side | right side
Mrs. Saltonstall | Mrs Lee
Mr. Lane | Mr. Saltonstall
Minot Weld | Bamie Roosevelt
Anna Rotch[?] | Otto[?] Williams
Harcourt Amory | Harriet Lawrence
Rosy Lee | George Lee[?]
Jim[?] Porter | Jenny Hooper
Rose Saltonstall | Jack Tebbetts ^Fanny Dana[?]^
[illegible] Trimble[?] | Dick Saltonstall
Kitty Lane | Bessie Whitney
Charley Morgan | George Griswold
Julia[?] Bacon | Nellie Bacon
Edmund Bayliss[?] | Harry Shaw
Lulu Lane | Corinne Roosevelt
Van Rensselaer | Emmons Blaine[?]
Alice Lee | Bessie Hooper.
```
> Seating diagram: a drawn rectangle (the table) with 'Mr Whitney' at the head and 'Roosevelt' at the foot; side lists in rows/columns. Several surnames cramped; 'Fanny Dana[?]' squeezed in at right end of the 'Jack Tebbetts' line.

*1879-11-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Everything went off to perfection; the dinner was
capital, the flowers very pretty and we had
great fun with the toasts and speeches,
Mr Whitney and Mr. Saltonstall were especially
funny; all the girls looked extremely
pretty, the wine was good, and the fellows
all gentlemen.
In the evening drove over to the
Minots to a little party.
```

### 429_0803_R · Sunday, November 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0803/996,0,878,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Breakfasted in the Club; drove over
to Chestnut Hill, and came back
to teach Sunday School.
The two girls go back to-
morrow. It has been very pleasant
to have them on here, and they have
enjoyed themselves greatly.
```

### 429_0804_L · Wednesday, November 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0804/0,0,953,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove over in the cart to
take lunch at the Lees;
afterwards drove pretty
Rosy to a tennis
party at the Laurences.
Had a lovely drive after-
wards with her.
I then took up my
abode at the Saltonstalls
where I intend to stay
till Sunday. I am now
most thoroughly and happily
at home here, and I really
love every member of the
family. We played whist
in the evening.
```
> 'love' (line 14) is underlined in the original

### 429_0804_R · Thursday, November 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0804/953,0,896,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I took dear, honest, funny
Rose out for a drive in
the dog cart; then I went
skating with Phil; at
four oclock I went in
to the Lees to the
thanksgiving dinner.
Afterwards we had great
fun, playing games,
dancing & acting
charades.
```

### 429_0805_L · Friday, November 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0805/0,0,984,1553/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I took a long ride in the
cart with Alice; got caught
in the rain and had to
borrow an umbrella from
a strange house. In the
afternoon went out for
a long walk with Alice
and Rose, who were just
as funny as they could
be, making me buy
candy &c.
In the evening I stayed
up till all hours with
dear old Mr Saltonstall, talking
poetry and theology; while
sweet Mrs. S at first dozed
in a chair and then went
to bed.
```

### 429_0805_R · Saturday, November 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0805/984,0,892,1553/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rainy; so I spent the morning
in the house reading, dancing
with Mamie & Hattie[?] and
telling stories to Phil &
Cotty. After lunch went
out walking with Ell[?]
Whitney, Rose and Alice.
Drove over to call on Miss.
Julia Bacon. Spent
the evening at the Lees,
dancing and playing
whist.
```

### 429_0806_L · Sunday, November 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0806/0,0,956,1497/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-11-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After Church I left the
Hill with the deepest
regret that the lovely five
days were over, and drove
back to Cambridge. After
Sunday School I went in
to the Whitneys, where I
took tea and spent a
most pleasant evening.
```

### 429_0807_L · Tuesday, December 2, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0807/0,0,958,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went to a party at the
Wares[?]; it was Alice's and
Rose's coming out party
and I was much amused
at seeing all the buds.
I am going out into
society this winter.
```
> surname on line 2 uncertain

### 429_0807_R · Wednesday, December 3, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0807/958,0,897,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a little supper up in
the Porc.
```

### 429_0808_L · Thursday, December 4, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0808/0,0,990,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Jack Tebbetts and
Minot Weld over to the
latter's house, where we
stay to night. After dinner
went to a party at the
Grays, which was very
pleasant.
```

### 429_0808_R · Friday, December 5, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0808/990,0,892,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove back to Cambridge
in the morning; in the
afternoon drove over to the
Lees to dinner. Afterwards
drove Frank Lee into
Boston.
```

### 429_0809_L · Saturday, December 6, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0809/0,0,1008,1476/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning drove over
to Minot Welds where I
spent most of the day.
Dined in Boston at
Harry Shaws, afterwards
going to a commerce
party at his cousins; where
I passed a very pleasant
evening with a pretty
Miss Silsbee.
I enjoy my horse and
cart more and more
every day. I am afraid
going out will tire me
somewhat, as I get up
every morning at 7.15 no-
matter how late I have been
up - except Saturday and
Sunday.
```

### 429_0809_R · Sunday, December 7, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0809/1008,0,861,1476/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
My Sunday School is
getting along swimmingly.
I took tea with the
Miss. Lanes and passed a
very pleasant evening.
```

### 429_0810_L · Monday, December 8, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0810/0,0,974,1519/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After, as usual, playing
whist in the Club (Jack,
Dick, Minot and I do
so every night) we
all went off on a
spree, and had great fun.
```

### 429_0810_R · Tuesday, December 9, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0810/974,0,874,1519/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dined with Emmons Blaine
at Harry Shaws; later
went to a great party
at the Amory's.
```

### 429_0811_L · Wednesday, December 10, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0811/0,0,953,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dined at the Lanes.
They have been very sweet
to me, and I like them
both very much, especially
pretty Miss. Lulu.
```

### 429_0811_R · Thursday, December 11, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0811/953,0,887,1512/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went in to the first
Harvard assembly; I
had lovely fun, dancing
with Rosy Lee, who
looked as pretty as a
picture. When we came
out to Harvard we
had a little "symposium".
```

### 429_0812_R · Saturday, December 13, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0812/968,0,894,1527/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Ran 3 miles (slowly, in 21m) in
the morning. After lunch
drove Jack and Dick over
in the cart to Chestnut
Hill. Came back to
Cambridge and went in
town with Harry Chapin,
dining at Youngs and
going to the theatre after-
wards.
```

### 429_0813_L · Sunday, December 14, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0813/0,0,961,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Breakfast in the Porc as
usual. After Sunday School
I drove Harry Chapin
over to Minots[?]; where we
took[?] tea. Howard Townsend
is passing Sunday with
him.
```

### 429_0813_R · Monday, December 15, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0813/961,0,894,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In afternoon drove Jack T.
into Boston, where we called
on Mrs. Shaw & Mrs. Whitney.
In the evening went to
the first Papanti Hall
party and had a lovely
time, dancing with
pretty Nana Rotch[?].
Van Rennsaeler is
engaged to Luly[?] Lane!
I am very glad of it;
They are both perfectly
radiant. He is a very
good fellow, and she an
exceedingly sweet girl.
```

### 429_0814_L · Tuesday, December 16, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0814/0,0,990,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dined at the Amorys; after
wards went to see a
very funny play at the
Gaiety, with Harry Chapin
and John Tebbetts[?].
```

### 429_0814_R · Wednesday, December 17, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0814/990,0,887,1530/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Tom Lee over to
the Saltonstalls. Pudding
Theatricals in the evening;
very good.
```

### 429_0815_L · Thursday, December 18, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0815/0,0,985,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took Dick out driving; called[?]
on the Lanes and Rotches.
In the evening we had a
"champagne supper" or rather
dinner, and afterwards went
up to the Δ.K.E[?] theatricals
which were the best I have
ever seen; then finished the
evening with a punch in
the Club. Had an awfully
jolly evening.
```
> 'called' runs off right edge (only 'calle' visible); society initials read as Greek-style 'Δ.K.E', uncertain.

### 429_0815_R · Friday, December 19, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0815/985,0,876,1544/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening took the
night boat for New York,
with Harry Chapin
```

### 429_0816_L · Saturday, December 20, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0816/0,0,1017,1550/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It is perfectly delightful
to see all the family
again.
```

### 429_0816_R · Sunday, December 21, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0816/1017,0,852,1550/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day, after
Church, with the Roosevelts;
Doc spends the night
with us.
```

### 429_0817_L · Monday, December 22, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0817/0,0,1004,1489/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening Ike
Iselin gave me a
dinner; there were present
(besides Nell) Harold Hadden,
Doug Robinson, Bob
Sedgwick, Sydney Stratton
and Archie Russell
—all very good fellows,
We had capital fun.
```

### 429_0817_R · Tuesday, December 23, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0817/1004,0,864,1489/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Snowed hard; sleighing excellent.
In afternoon Nell and
I drove up in a cutter
to Jerome Park, where
we had a very cosy
little dinner, coming
home by moonlight
towards ten; then I
went to the University
Club, to a supper given
by the Harvard Glee Club.
```

### 429_0818_L · Wednesday, December 24, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0818/0,0,997,1565/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day calling on
various friends—among
them some very pretty
girls, as Annie Murray,
Emily Post, the two Miss
Kanes[?], the two Miss Potters,
Edith Carow, Fanny Smith,
Nellie Phelps and Lizzie
Moran.
```

### 429_0818_R · Thursday, December 25, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0818/997,0,856,1565/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Xmas. Lovely time.
Received beautiful
presents. Nell and I
over[?] superintended the
Newsboys dinner—
poor little wretches.
The usual Xmas
Family dinner was
as pleasant as it
could be—every
Roosevelt present.
```

### 429_0819_L · Friday, December 26, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0819/0,0,995,1489/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took lunch with Edith.
In the evening Alice, Rosy[?]
Rose[?], Dick, Harry Shaw
& Ell Whitney arrived
to spend a week with
us.
```

### 429_0819_R · Saturday, December 27, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0819/995,0,863,1489/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Showed the boys
around town in the
day. In the afternoon
went to a little
party at Aunt
Annies.
```

### 429_0820_L · Sunday, December 28, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0820/0,0,973,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the afternoon at
the Newsboys Lodging
house.
It is perfectly
lovely, having the
dear, sweet Chestnut
Hillers with us;—
and so natural.
```

### 429_0820_R · Monday, December 29, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0820/973,0,851,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Jack Kinsford[?], Ell
Whitney & myself showed
the three Chestnut Hill
girls about town in the
morning; we all lunched
at Ulert[?] and Hillys[?];
In the evening Aunt
Lizzie gave us a theatre
party, and we had an
uproariously jolly time
```

### 429_0821_L · Tuesday, December 30, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0821/0,0,984,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
^we all^
Went to an entertainment
at the music hall in the
afternoon; in the evening
went to a very pleasant
dinner at Mrs. Haddons;
Sat between her and Rosy.
```
> 'we all' inserted above line 1.

### 429_0821_R · Wednesday, December 31, 1879.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0821/984,0,896,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1879-12-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove Rose round the
park in a buggy; took
Alice out walking[?]; Alfred
gave the three men &
myself a lunch in the
down town club; in the
evening we had a small
party, and danced the
old year out and
drank the new year
in.
```
> 'walking' has a correction/overwritten letters above its start.

## 1880

340 pages with writing · 77 blocks flagged medium/low

- **429_0937_R** (1880-07-211880) — low: first two lines heavily smeared; family names uncertain
- **429_0952_L** (1880-08-19) — low: game tally; final short word after 'chicken' unreadable.
- **429_0966_R** (1880-09-17) — low: game tally; small cramped interlined words above '3 ducks' partly illegible
- **429_1020_R** (other) — low: Small figures in an unheaded space between the '76 and '77 columns on the Blue, Green, Night, Bittern and Least Bittern rows, apparently struck or overwritten (crossed strokes). Not included in row totals, which balance without them. Also a stray mark on the Sandsnipe row between '76 and '77.
- **429_1024_L** (endpaper) — low: Hasty ink notes on an unruled endpaper. Top line: a struck word, then a word read only as '[illegible]' (possibly beginning 'E'), then '60,40'; below it a further struck word under an ink blot. In 'Shot ... in Aug' a word is struck and a small '3'[?] stands above 'in'. 'famous[?] bill' first word uncertain. Small figures '223' and '22' written beside 'Law School' and 'Presents'. Brace total 341,40 (written vertically) = 15,40+126,00+100,00+100,00; 660,40 = 319,00 + 341,40. Large ink blots.
