### 429_0940_L · JULY, MONDAY 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0940/0,0,1006,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-261880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dick and I took
up our abode at the
Welds, where Alice and
Rose are staying.
```

### 429_0940_R · JULY, TUESDAY 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0940/1006,0,957,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-271880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the morning
playing tennis; the
afternoon and evening
with my darling in
the woods and on
the Rocky shores.
```

### 429_0941_L · JULY, WEDNESDAY 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0941/0,0,1006,1583/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-281880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon I
took darling little Queen
out driving in a buggy.
Went to a ~~gr~~ very
pleasant party in the
even.
```

### 429_0941_R · JULY, THURSDAY 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0941/1006,0,954,1583/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-291880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Alice's nineteenth birthday!
Just to think of it!
I can even now hardly
realize my wonderful
good fortune in having
won her; I can never
repay her love, but I
shall do my best to, by
unfailing tenderness and
care. How I love her! She
seems like a star of
heaven, she is so far above
other girls; my pearl,
my pure flower. When
I hold her in my arms
there is nothing on
earth left to wish
for; and how infinitely
blessed is my lot. If
ever a man has been
blessed by a Merciful
Providence, I am he.
Oh, my darling, my own
best loved little Queen!
```
> 'ever' blotted; punctuation after 'it' read as '!'/'?' uncertain

### 429_0942_L · JULY, FRIDAY 30. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0942/0,0,1018,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-301880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I was pretty sick today;
but it's worth while being
sick to find out what
perfect trumps my friends
are. All the rest of "Big Six"
(Sammy, Chip, Jack, Dick
and Minot) took the best
care of me. Above all, my
own best beloved darling,
my purest queen, my
sweetest Alice, acted the part of
an ideal little ~~life~~ wife; she
was so sweet and tender
and loving and thoughtful;
gave up all of her amusements
to take care of me; and
was such a bright, bewitching
little sunbeam that her
very presence made me feel
well. What a treasure I have won!
Was there ever a man as fortu-
nate as I? I can hardly believe it.
How I shall try to be worthy of her,
and make her happy!
```

### 429_0942_R · JULY, SATURDAY 31. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0942/1018,0,966,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-07-311880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rapidly recovering;
Alice took first prize
in the ladies' tennis
tournament.
```

### 429_0943_L · AUGUST, SUNDAY 1. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0943/0,0,1005,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-11880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
There was a very
good sermon in the
morning.
```
> ink smeared

### 429_0943_R · AUGUST, MONDAY 2. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0943/1005,0,979,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-21880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The Welds are just
too sweet to us for any-
thing. In the evening,
if we can't go out on
the rocks, Alice and I
generally retire to the
kitchen by ourselves.
```

### 429_0944_L · AUGUST, TUESDAY 3. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0944/0,0,1018,1572/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-31880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning I
walked up Newport
Mountain.
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0944/1018,0,976,1572/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-41880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Raining; but still I
contrived to be most of
the day with my
darling. Hop in the
evening.
```
> faint ink

### 429_0945_L · AUGUST, THURSDAY 5. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0945/0,0,1024,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-51880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Darling Queenie
over to dine at Schooner
Head.
```

### 429_0945_R · AUGUST, FRIDAY 6. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0945/1024,0,981,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-61880 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Shaw, Chapin, Saltonstall,
Rose, Alice and myself
all left; Alice and I
reached Chestnut Hill
late at night.
```
> faint ink

### 429_0946_L · August, Saturday 7. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0946/0,0,1038,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It is delightful to be
back at Chestnut Hill;
I am extremely fond
of all the Lees, and
love to be with them.
I spend all day long
with my own sweet
-est darling.
```

### 429_0946_R · August, Sunday 8. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0946/1038,0,990,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Darling took me out
driving. Oh, how hap-
py I am!
```

### 429_0947_L · August, Monday 9. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0947/0,0,1030,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I spend almost all
the time with my
darling; how bitterly
I hate to have to
leave her for six
weeks day after
tomorrow.
```

### 429_0947_R · August, Tuesday 10. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0947/1030,0,985,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
As usual I spent the
whole day with my
darling; she is so
bright and bewitching
and yet so tender.
I read aloud to her
(from Hawthorne or
Thackeray) a good deal;
and we are both very
fond of poetry[?] Long-
fellow, and parts of
Shelly and swinbrane[?].
```
> 'poetry' may be written 'potry'; last word appears to read 'swinbrane' (lowercase s); 'Shelly' as written.

### 429_0948_L · August, Wednesday 11. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0948/0,0,1066,1600/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent a lovely day with
my sweetest Queen, and
at last, with a heavy
heart in spite of the
shortness of the proposed
separation, bade her fare-
well for ~~six~~ ^seven^ weeks; and
took the night train
for New York.
```
> 'six' struck through with 'seven' written above it (reading of both small words medium confidence); 'separation' has an overwritten letter.

### 429_0948_R · August, Thursday 12. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0948/1066,0,959,1600/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Spent the day shopping;
came out to L. O. B.
in the evening; where
were darling Mother,
Aunt Susie and Uncle
Hill, and the two
Lockes.
```
> 'L. O. B.' and 'Lockes' read with medium confidence.

### 429_0949_L · August, Friday 13. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0949/0,0,1039,1572/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Played tennis with
Frank; paddled with
Emlen in his canoe.
```

### 429_0949_R · August, Saturday 14. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0949/1039,0,957,1572/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day packing
for my western trip,
and walking in
the woods and driving
mother in the light
wagon.
```

### 429_0950_L · August, Sunday 15. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0950/0,0,1021,1576/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Elliott arrived while we
were at church. I am
frightfully homesick
all the time for
Alice; I miss her more
than I can tell; she
is never absent from
my thoughts.
```
> 'never' underlined.

### 429_0950_R · August, Monday 16. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0950/1021,0,956,1576/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Nell and I took
the night train
for Chicago.
```
> final word has an extra stroke after 'Chicago' (possibly 'Chicagoe').

### 429_0951_L · August, Tuesday 17. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0951/0,0,1025,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Travelled all day through
the wooded hills of
Pensylvania and the
rolling prairies of
Ohio. It is great
fun to be off
with old Nell; he
and I can do about
anything together; we
never lose our
temper under diffi-
culties and always
accomplish what we
set about.
```
> 'Pensylvania' appears spelled with one n; 'prairies' letters partly unclear.

### 429_0951_R · August, Wednesday 18. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0951/1025,0,967,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Chicago in
morning. Thanks to a
great free-mason
meeting could hardly
get rooms. Boiling
hot; don't know
where to go; in a
general stew.
Later in the evening
got hold of a man
named Wilcox, and
will go to his farm
till Sept.
```

### 429_0952_L · August, Thursday 19. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0952/0,0,1026,1570/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went to Wilcox's
farm, at Huntly,
60 miles from Chicago.
In the afternoon Nell
and I took a pre-
liminary tramp
through the great
rolling fields after
prarie chickens; we
found a small flock
late in the evening;
he missed his bird,
I killed mine. It is
great fun to try
the open plains
shooting, to which I
am entirely unaccus-
-tomed — among
such vast, almost
level fields, with so
few trees. The farm
house fare is pretty rough.
```
> 'prarie' as it appears; 'Huntly' medium confidence.

*1880-08-19 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · low*
```
1 prairie[?] chicken.  [illegible]
```
> game tally; final short word after 'chicken' unreadable.

### 429_0952_R · August, Friday 20. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0952/1026,0,969,1570/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning took a
four hours tramp after
chickens; each of us got
several shots; Nell killed
2 brace, I but one bird.
```

*1880-08-20 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 Grouse
Elliott
4 grouse
```
> '1 Grouse' at bottom left; 'Elliott' above '4 grouse' at bottom right.

### 429_0953_L · August, Saturday 21. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0953/0,0,1027,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
E. & I started off in a
wagon, and shot all day.
When we came to a good
looking place we would tie
the horses to a fence, and
spend an hour or two
hunting through the
meadow, stubble fields
and pastures for chicken,
or by the slews[?] and
ponds for ducks, snipe
and plover. The roads are
excellent and almost
level and the country beau-
-tiful; we made a fair
bag; and I have rarely
passed a pleasanter
day.
```

*1880-08-21 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 grouse 2 doves 4 plover 6 snipe
Elliot shot 4 grouse 1 duck. 3 plover
Both of us also shot hawks, gophers
&c.
```

### 429_0953_R · August, Sunday 22. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0953/1027,0,961,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Both fare and accomo-
-dations here are of
the roughest; pork &
potatoes being the
invariable meal, except
for what we shoot.
But I like the
men a great deal;
and Nell and I are
the greatest compan-
ions. Elliott is a
good shot; I am not;
but I am improving
steadily.
In spite of enjoy-
ing my hunting so
much, I miss Alice
dreadfully; I would
give up everything
just to see her
sweet face again.
In the evening we got all
hands (male & female) together
& sang hymns.
```
> 'accomo-dations' as written.

### 429_0954_L · August, Monday 23. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0954/0,0,1024,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on an all
days hunting trip
in the wagon; I never
shot worse, while
Elliott hit about
everything he fired at.
```

*1880-08-23 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 grouse 1 rabbit 2 plover 6 ^sand^ snipe
Elliott 9 grouse 6[?] ^true[?]^ snipe 2 plover
```
> interlined words written above 'snipe' in each line.

### 429_0954_R · August, Tuesday 24. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0954/1024,0,973,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Before the sun was up
we started off, tramp-
ing in sullen silence
through the wet
prarie grass; but we
found few birds and
~~shot~~ very badly.
```
> first word of last line is heavily struck; reading 'shot' uncertain.

*1880-08-24 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 dove 2 plover 2 sandsnipe
E 2 grouse.
```

### 429_0955_L · August, Wednesday 25. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0955/0,0,1009,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Rained all day, so
stayed in the house.
~~O~~ Two of the men, a
texan desperado and a
jovial, stalwart irish boy
got fighting drunk; I
expected a row but it
did not occur. The other two
men (a canny scot and
a goodnatured german) were
on the side of peace. Any-
how, Nell and I are trav-
elling on our muscle, and
don't give a hang for
any man.
```
> a false-start letter before 'Two' appears cancelled; 'stayed' overwritten.

### 429_0955_R · August, Thursday 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0955/1009,0,964,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day shooting
but with very poor
luck.
Alice is always
the first thing I think
about in the morning,
and the last at night,
and I doubt if she
is ever absent five
minutes from my thoughts,
— or even from my
dreams.
```

*1880-08-26 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 Bittern 2 sandsnipe
E. 1 grouse.
```

### 429_0956_L · August, Friday 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0956/0,0,1058,1595/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We tramped through the
morning after game, but
saw nothing. The country
is shot out, and we
shall leave it tomorrow.
```

### 429_0956_R · August, Saturday 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0956/1058,0,971,1595/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Came into Chicago;
stayed at Sherman House,
where the accommodations
seemed regal compared
to what we have had
at the farm house
```
> 'Sherman' medium confidence; 'accommodations' has an overwritten letter; no final stop visible.

### 429_0957_L · August, Sunday 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0957/0,0,1053,1568/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning went
to Methodist Church; I
liked the earnest though
rather startling preacher
and the congregation;
of the latter the large
majority were men - generally
they are women.
```
> 'large majority' underlined.

### 429_0957_R · August, Monday 30. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0957/1053,0,972,1568/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Waiting for Wilcox
with whom we start
out West; so have
been "seeing" the city.
```

### 429_0958_L · August, Tuesday 31. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0958/0,0,1055,1569/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-08-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Still waiting for Wilcox
Drove round Chicago, read,
played whist, went to
theatre, took a long walk,
a turkish bath &c; so
both passed the time
very pleasantly. Dear old
Nell and I always
enjoy ourselves when
together; but I keep getting
more and more homesick
for Alice; I think of her
all the time, and nothing
can compensate me for
being away from her.
```
> 'both' has an overwritten letter.

### 429_0958_R · September, Wednesday 1. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0958/1055,0,969,1569/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At last Wilcox made his
appearance and we
shall leave in a day
or two.
```

### 429_0959_R · September, Friday 3. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0959/1035,0,973,1586/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Finally we started; Nell,
myself, an engineer and
an ex confederate soldier.
```
> 'and' and 'ex' partly overwritten.

### 429_0960_L · September, Saturday 4. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0960/0,0,1029,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Reached Caroll at 4 o'c in
the morning; started as
soon as possible in a two
horse wagon out on the
beautiful, rolling, fenceless
prarie, and did not
reach home till nine
o'c p.m. There were more
birds than we have before
seen, and I shot better
than I yet have, though
I missed several easy
shots. Troubled both by
colic and asthma.
```
> 'Caroll' medium confidence.

*1880-09-04 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
6 grouse 2 quail
E 6 grouse
```

### 429_0960_R · September, Sunday 5. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0960/1029,0,976,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After breakfast we again
left in the wagon
and drove about 25
miles south where we
put up for the night
at a farm house, where
of course the accomodation
are pretty rough. The
country is broken up
into numerous rolling
hills of slight elevation,
absolutely treeless and
sparsely scattered over
with settlers houses. Birds
are not very plentiful
and are wild; but I
shot pretty well, fully
as well as or better than
Elliott
```

*1880-09-05 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
6 grouse 2 quail
8 grouse
```

### 429_0961_L · September, Monday 6. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0961/0,0,1018,1539/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Early in the morning
started back over the rolling
prarie; our horses work beautifully
and go any where, except through
the slews, or where the sluggish
streams flow in their deep
narrow beds with steep banks.
Three of our setters work well;
the other two are simple
nuisances. Not only the
hunting itself is great
fun, but I enjoy greatly
riding across country in
the wagon. We did not
get in till after dark,
one horse dead lame, and
having nearly upset in
a mud hole. I shot well;
rather better than Elliott.
```
> 'any where' written as two words.

*1880-09-06 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8 grouse 1 quail 1 bittern
E. 6 grouse
```

### 429_0961_R · September, Tuesday 7. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0961/1018,0,949,1539/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
After breakfast started
in the wagon for
Wall Lake; passing
through much flatter
and less settled prairie
than yesterday; we stopped
before lunch to kill some
chickens in a patch of
long grass; after lunch
I stalked and killed
some snipe by a bayou,
getting up to my hips in
mud wading to recover
them, and getting bit-
-ten by a snake when I
came out. Then we hunted
through the stubble fields
till dusk and put up
for the night at a remarkably
neat, comfortable little
farm house. Shot fairly; about
as well as Elliott
```

*1880-09-07 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8 grouse 8 yellowlegs 1 rail.
E 6 grouse
```

### 429_0962_L · September, Wednesday 8. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0962/0,0,1027,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Early in the morning I
shot round the shores
of the lake after yellowlegs
and saw a most
beautiful sunrise over the
misty waters. There was
a large flock of pelicans
on the lake; and thous-
ands of yellowheaded black-
-birds along shore. After
breakfast we went off in
the wagon after chickens;
I shot wretchedly while
Elliott did splendidly.
Am having a lovely
time, but am a good
deal bothered by the
asthma. Am in splendid
walking trim, and can
tramp all day easily.
```

*1880-09-08 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
4 grouse 6 yellowlegs 1 grebe
E. 14 grouse 2 ducks.
```

### 429_0962_R · September, Thursday 9. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0962/1027,0,951,1563/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning we
started off after chickens
getting a couple of dozen
before dinner; it is glorious
autumn weather, cool and
clear and driving around
in the wagon over the
prairie is great fun. In
the afternoon Nell and
I paddled round the
lake in an old flat
-bottom after ducks and
snipe; he shot well and
I didn't. Finally my
second and last gun also
broke! which knocks me
up for the present. We
have had very hard luck
this trip; a constant succession
of unavoidable accidents.
```

*1880-09-09 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
6 grouse 21 yellowlegs &c
E 8 grouse 3 ducks 1 grebe
```

### 429_0963_L · September, Friday 10. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0963/0,0,1027,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
~~I~~ Drove back to Caroll
in the wagon; on the way
we nearly upset over a
big rock, both the driver
and I being sent flying
out of the wagon on
our heads, but we were
very little hurt. Took the
night train for Chicago.
```
> a cancelled false-start letter (blotted) precedes 'Drove'.

### 429_0963_R · September, Saturday 11. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0963/1027,0,960,1554/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Chicago safely.
```

### 429_0964_L · September, Sunday 12. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0964/0,0,1020,1548/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took communion at
the Methodist Church;
the services were simple
and impressive.
Much though I have
enjoyed the trip, it has
not made up to me
for being away from
my darling; nothing
ever could. But it has
done my health a
world of good.
```

### 429_0964_R · September, Monday 13. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0964/1020,0,960,1548/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Left in the night
train for St. Pauls.
Both Elliott and I
had to buy new
guns; and we parted
with the other two
men, whom we dislike.
```
> last word may be abbreviated 'dislik.'

### 429_0965_L · September, Tuesday 14. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0965/0,0,1039,1574/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Left St Pauls in the
evening, with two new
guns and a stub-
tailed old pointer for
Moorhead, Minnesota, on
the Dacotah boundary,
to see old Jack Elliott
```

### 429_0965_R · September, Wednesday 15. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0965/1039,0,939,1574/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Moorhead in the
morning. In the afternoon
Nell and I took a buggy
and drove off to some huge
stubble fields, which we hunted
through with great success till
dark, or rather till old stub-
tail found and attacked a
cursed skunk. It was great
fun to be off entirely alone
and among a new kind
of grouse, too. When we went
back to the miserable old
hotel we were overjoyed
to find dear old Jack,
who is entirely unchanged,
except that he is more
of a man and really
good looking. We stayed
up till all hours talking
things over.
```

*1880-09-15 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 sharptail grouse 2 golden plover
E 5 " " 3 pinnated grouse.
```
> ditto marks under 'sharptail grouse'.

### 429_0966_L · September, Thursday 16. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0966/0,0,1063,1574/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day with old
Jack, whom we have
not seen for bew[?] nearly
two years; it is lovely
to see the old man again.
I am now feeling
in superb health and
strength.
```
> 'bew[?]' appears to be a false start before 'nearly'

### 429_0966_R · September, Friday 17. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0966/1063,0,944,1574/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Nell and I started out
in a wagon and pair
driven by a solemn old
german, driving north
beyond the buffalo river.
We hunted grouse in
the ~~[illegible]~~ stubble fields
and ducks in the
slews; the grouse were wild,
and we only saw the
"white bellies" — no
chickens. I shot well,
better than Elliott.
We saw jack rabbits
for the first time; we
stopped shooting after
sunset and drove five
miles in the light of the
full moon to the
rough but comfortable house
of a settler.
```

*1880-09-17 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
( 1 ~~[illegible] duck~~
1 [illegible] old drake[?]
2 dipper[?]
4 sharptail grouse 3 ducks
~~4 shar~~ 1 grebe 1 coot 1 jack rabbit
E. 5 sharptails 1 jack rabbit
```
> game tally; small cramped interlined words above '3 ducks' partly illegible

### 429_0967_L · September, Saturday 18. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0967/0,0,1047,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We had very much the best
days sport we have had; there
were "innumerable" grouse,
though they were very wild.
But I had to sit up most
of last night with the asthma,
I was so troubled with con-
-tinual attacks of colic that
I could hardly walk, and
it rained most of the day, so
I shot badly; Elliott
however did splendidly.
We have had pretty good
fare and accomadations up
here, so far, and great fun;
but I shall be perfectly
overjoyed to get back to
Alice.
```

*1880-09-18 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
12 sharptail grouse 7 pinnated grouse
E 27 " " " 15 " "
```

### 429_0967_R · September, Sunday 19. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0967/1047,0,940,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started early in the
morning and drove over
thirty ^miles^ in a cold, driving
rain storm; but I feel
much better than yesterday.
We lost our way several
times driving across
the prairie, and finally
had to put up for the
night at the house of a
norwegian settler. A little
before sunset it partially
cleared, and we sallied
out with our guns, returning
with several ducks and grouse.
~~partridges~~. Our new driver
is a stocky little bar-
keeper, a nice little fellow.
```
> 'prairie' spelled with unclear letters ('praiie'[?]); 'partridges' struck through

*1880-09-19 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2 sharptails 2 pinnated 2 duck
E2 " " 4 " 4 "
```
> the duck count digit appears overwritten

### 429_0968_L · September, Monday 20. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0968/0,0,1026,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had a lovely night; I had to
sleep on the floor under a
buffalo robe, the wind howling
round the neat but frail little
house. We started off before sunrise
lying in the cornfields for
ducks, and during the day
tramping a long distance
over the fields after chickens;
we both shot well. All day
long there was a furious,
cold northwesterly gale blowing
with continual rain showers.
We have had very good food
(principally what we have
shot ourselves lately) and
accomodations lately, and
are having the best time
we have yet had on this
trip; now I am feeling
in superb health.
```

*1880-09-20 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8 prairie chicken 4 duck
E10 " " 5 " "
```

### 429_0968_R · September, Tuesday 21. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0968/1026,0,914,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Cold but quite clear. We
started off in good season,
driving straight across
country and nearly
getting mired in a slew.
We shot some grouse in
the stubble fields, the old
pointer working ^well^; we also
~~killed~~ several ducks
from the wagon, and
finally hid behind
some wheat sheafs and
shot two geese out of
a flock which flew
overhead. When night over-
-took us we went into
camp among the timber on
a bend of the buffalo river.
We feasted on roast duck & 
chicken, and then lay down
under our blankets before the
roaring wood fire
```
> 'wheat' appears written over another word; '&' after 'duck' uncertain

*1880-09-21 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 goose 4 ducks 2 sharptail 3 prairie chicken
E 1 " 4 " 2 " 5 "
```

### 429_0969_L · September, Wednesday 22. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0969/0,0,1037,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Started in tolerably good
season, hunting through
the ~~f~~ stubble fields
after chicken and
whitebellies; we both
of us shot pretty
well. At dusk we
again camped in the
woods in a bend of
the Buffalo river; we
have no tent or regular
outfit, and sleep on
the ground; we have'n't
even a frying pan.
```

*1880-09-22 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
4 sharptails 3 chicken 2 plover 2 dove
E3 " " 7 " 2 "
```

### 429_0969_R · September, Thursday 23. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0969/1037,0,957,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Got off pretty early; it was
cloudy all day, raining
sometimes. We had only
fair success in the stubble
fields, but I had capital
fun in a big slew, wading
about after ducks, until
I went head over heels
in a mudhole, gun & all.
Reached Moorehead in the
evening, which we spent
with dear old John
Elliott. We have had great
fun this trip; I have never
had a better hunt; but
I am awfully homesick
for Alice, and shall be too
delighted to see her again.
```

*1880-09-23 · tally · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
3 chicken 4 duck 2 dove 4 plover 2 grebe
7 " 6 " 2 "
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0970/0,0,1055,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day with old Jack. Our
bag is as follows.
```

```
 | T | E | Total
```

### 429_0970_R · September, Saturday 25. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0970/1055,0,942,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Left for St Pauls. The
trip has been great fun;
but how glad I am it
is over and I am to see
Alice!
```

### 429_0971_L · September, Sunday 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0971/0,0,1028,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Chicago
```

### 429_0971_R · September, Monday 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0971/1028,0,943,1542/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Left Chicago
```

### 429_0972_L · September, Tuesday 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0972/0,0,1069,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Stopped at Niagara Falls.
```

### 429_0972_R · September, Wednesday 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0972/1069,0,909,1541/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached New York and in
the evening Chestnut Hill
```

### 429_0973_L · September, Thursday 30. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0973/0,0,1035,1553/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-09-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am so happy that I
hardly know what to do.
My own beautiful queen
is the same as ever and
yet with a certain added
charm that I do not
know how to describe; I
can not take my eyes
off her; she ~~seems~~ is so pure
and holy that it seems almost
profanation to touch her, no
matter how gently and
tenderly; and yet when we
are alone I can not bear
her to be a minute out
of my arms. I have been
gradually getting frightfully
homesick for her; my
happiness now is almost
too great.
```
> struck word before 'is so pure' read as 'seems'[?]

### 429_0973_R · October, Friday 1. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0973/1035,0,962,1553/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day with
my own hearts darling,
my sweetest mistress.
```

### 429_0974_L · October, Saturday 2. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0974/0,0,1045,1593/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am living in a dream
of delight with my
darling, my true-love.
```

### 429_0974_R · October, Sunday 3. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0974/1045,0,927,1593/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening I with
intense regret had to go
back to New York; but
it will be the last
seperation from my darling,
for three weeks from
Wednesday we are married
— it makes me so
happy I am almost
afraid.
```

### 429_0975_L · October, Monday 4. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0975/0,0,1048,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am staying with dear
Aunt Annie & Uncle
Jimmie; in their cosy little
house. I am busy with
wedding presents and getting
all ready — and am
dreadfully homesick for
Alice.
```

### 429_0976_L · October, Wednesday 6. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0976/0,0,1039,1592/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Entered the Columbia
Law School; I shall
be there every day about
six hours, from nine
till half-past three.
Am having a lovely
time at Aunt Annie's
but miss Alice dreadfully.
I am going to give
her a diamond crescent
a ruby bracelet and
a saphire ring — in
all about 2500 dollars!
I have been spending
money like water for
these last two years,
but shall economise
after I am married.
Three weeks from to-
-day we are married!
I hardly dare believe
it; it is too good. Oh
my darling, my darling!
```

### 429_0977_L · October, Friday 8. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0977/0,0,1024,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out to Oyster Bay.
Everything looked very
cosy and homelike
and it was lovely
to see the dear quar-
tette — Elliott, Pussie,
Bysie & Mother. We
have very much to
talk over about the
wedding.
```

### 429_0977_R · October, Saturday 9. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0977/1024,0,957,1552/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day in
the open air, chiefly
playing tennis with
Nell and Fred
Weeks, both of whom
I beat. Emlen, Al-
fred and Griswold
came up to pass
Sunday.
```

### 429_0978_R · October, Monday 11. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0978/1053,0,953,1560/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went back to New
York. I like the law
school very well so far.
The cousins have all
acted like trumps, and
have sent Alice
beautiful presents;
Emlen a pearl ring,
West a bracelet &c &c.
```

### 429_0979_R · October, Wednesday 13. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0979/1030,0,932,1540/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Only two weeks from
today; my happiness
is so great that it
makes me almost
afraid.
Dined at Ediths
with Fanny and
Grace, who are all
going on to the
wedding. So are all
my relatives and
most of my
friends.
```

### 429_0980_R · October, Friday 15. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0980/1062,0,928,1570/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came out to Oyster
Bay.
```
> only these words read normally; everything below is mirrored show-through from the October 16 page

### 429_0981_L · October, Saturday 16. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0981/0,0,1060,1565/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day walk-
ing through the
woods and playing
tennis with Johnny
and Freddy Weeks.
```

### 429_0981_R · October, Sunday 17. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0981/1060,0,913,1565/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
This is the last Sunday
I shall spend here be-
-fore I am married;
when next I come
to Oyster Bay it
will be with my
darling bride. I do
so hope and pray
I can make her
happy; I shall try
very hard to be
as unselfish and
sunny tempered as
she is. It almost
frightens me, in spite
of my ^our^ happiness, to
think that perhaps I
may not make her
happy; but I shall try
so hard; and if ever
man loved woman
I love her.
```
> 'our' interlined above, between 'my' and 'happiness'; 'my' not struck

### 429_0982_L · October, Monday 18. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0982/0,0,1069,1580/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went in to New York;
shall leave for Boston
on Friday. Until then
I shall have my
hands full atten-
ding to various affairs.
Alice bridesmaids are
Corinne, Rosy, Rose and
Fanny Peabody. My
ushers are Emlen,
West, Dick, Frank Lee,
Tom Lee, Cottie Peabody,
~~Cottie[?]~~ ^Frank^ Peabody, Jack
Tel-bots[?], Minot Weld, &
Walter Trimble.
```
> 'Alice' may be 'Alices'; struck word before 'Peabody' with 'Frank' interlined; surname 'Tel-bots' uncertain

### 429_0983_L · October, Friday 22. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0983/0,0,1061,1548/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took night train for
Boston
```

### 429_0983_R · October, Saturday 23. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0983/1061,0,943,1548/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Early in morning went
out to Chestnut Hill
and, oh! how delighted
I was to clasp my
beloved darling again
in my arms! She has
received about 250
presents.
In the afternoon
I went out with
dear old Cotta[?] Peabody
(whom I am very
fond of) to the
Peabodys house at
Kernwood, Salem. It is
a superb place; both
grounds and house
being strikingly handsome.
Besides, I like the whole
household very much;
they are all so well read
and intelligent — and
the boys such stalwart,
manly fellows
```
> name read 'Cotta' (cf. 'Cottie'/'Cotte' elsewhere)

### 429_0984_L · October, Sunday 24. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0984/0,0,1043,1578/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Cottie and I walked into
Salem to church; then
I went in to see sweet,
old "Grandma Lee". We
took tea at ~~[illegible]~~
Fanny Peabodys; she was
just as pretty and
cunning as ever.
Franks fiançée is
also visiting here; she is
Miss Rosamund Laurence,
a strikingly handsome
and exceedingly well
cultivated girl.
```
> struck word at end of line 5 illegible

### 429_0984_R · October, Monday 25. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0984/1043,0,937,1578/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the day making
the final arrange-
ments for the wedding;
in the evening Cottie
& I chopped down
some trees and had
larks generally
```

### 429_0985_L · October, Tuesday 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0985/0,0,1054,1537/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went in to Boston; lunched
with all of both families
out at the Lees; dined &
spent the night at
the Brunswick. Elliott, of
course, is best man.
```

### 429_0985_R · October, Wednesday 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0985/1054,0,938,1537/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 12 o'clock, on my
22d birthday, Alice
and I were married.
She made an ideally
beautiful bride; and
it was a lovely
wedding. We came on
for the night to
Springfield, where I had
taken a suite of rooms
for the night.
Our intense happiness
is too sacred to be
written about
```

### 429_0986_L · October, Thursday 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0986/0,0,1055,1560/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to Oyster Bay
where we are to spend a
fortnight; we are alone
in the house, with two
servants and a groom.
```

### 429_0986_R · October, Friday 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0986/1055,0,925,1560/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice has been resting
from the journey.
```

### 429_0987_L · October, Saturday 30. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0987/0,0,1045,1587/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
My horse is in magnif-
icent condition, and we
have the most beautiful
drives. The tennis is
laid out; and my rowboat
is in the water. We
breakfast at 10; dine at
2; and take tea at 7.
```

### 429_0987_R · October, Sunday 31. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0987/1045,0,947,1587/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-10-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Instead of going to Church
I read my bible aloud,
and we took a beautiful
walk; the weather is
lovely, clear and cool,
and the wooded hills
are red and yellow gold.
Alice has her sewing, and
I read aloud to her,
from Quentin Durward,
Pickwick Papers and the
Newcomes.
```

### 429_0988_L · November, Monday 1. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0988/0,0,1073,1545/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We are all alone in the
comfortable country house,
with it's roaring wood
fires and warm rooms,
and it's beautiful
autumn weather. Mary
Ann & Kate are model
servants; and the old
negro Davis (who al-
ways alludes to Alice
as "the good lady")
takes excellent care of
the horse. The horse, by the
way, and a dear,
clumsy, handsome colly dog
Dare by name, are
really friends, as much
as if they were human;
and besides them we
have an idiotic small
calf and a melan-
-choly ^cat^ calf, ^both of^ which Baby
can never resist teasing.
It is impossible to
```
> "cat" written above "calf" in line 22, which appears blotted/possibly cancelled; "both of" inserted above with caret. Entry continues on next page.

### 429_0988_R · November, Tuesday 2. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0988/1073,0,921,1545/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
describe the lovely, little
teasing ways of my
bright, bewitching dar-
ling; I can imagine no
picture so pretty as
her sweet self, seated
behind the teathings,
in the daintiest little
pink and gray morn-
ing dress, while, in my
silk jacket and slippers,
I sit at the other
end of the table. She
seems in beautiful
health; and she looks
even prettier than she
ever has before; the
little laughing, blushing
darling, with her
sweet, winsome ways;
and oh, she is such
a tender and de-
voted little wife. We
have the loveliest drives
```
> continues from previous page (entry begun under November 1); runs on to next page

### 429_0989_L · November, Wednesday 3. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0989/0,0,1048,1591/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
and walks together; we
play a great deal of
tennis, being about
equally matched; and
wander about the
woods and shores! In
the evenings I read
aloud while she sews;
or she plays on the
piano; or we play
cards together.
On Tuesday th[?] I
drove over to Norwich to
deposit my first vote
for president — for
Garfield.
```
> continues from previous pages (entry begun under November 1); small word after "Tuesday" unclear ("th" or "the")

### 429_0989_R · November, Thursday 4. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0989/1048,0,928,1591/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There is hardly an
hour of the twenty four
that we are not to-
gether; I am living
in dreamland; how
I wish it could last
for ever.
```

### 429_0990_L · November, Friday 5. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0990/0,0,1047,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
spent the usual healthy,
happy day
```

### 429_0990_R · November, Saturday 6. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0990/1047,0,946,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rainy, so we stayed
in the house. I love
to talk over everything
with Baby, from
Politics to Poetry, and
to read aloud to her,
either from a History
or standard novel or
from the daily news-
paper that forms our
only intercourse with
the outside world.
```

### 429_0991_L · November, Sunday 7. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0991/0,0,1053,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning I
read aloud from the
bible; in the afternoon
we took a long walk
through woods and fields,
in the beautiful, clear,
cool Autumn weather. Alice
is a beautiful walker, with
her long, firm step, and
clambers over fences about
as easily as I do.
My old hunter keeps
us pretty well supplied
with woodcock & partridge.
```

### 429_0991_R · November, Monday 8. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0991/1053,0,937,1561/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We took a very long
drive, going way out
beyond Syosset In[?] the
&[?] brush plains beyond
Syosset[?], coming back
through the beautiful
valley of Coldspring, and
through Laurel Hollow
and Oak Openings. In
the afternoon we
played lawn tennis.
```
> Lines 3-4: "In" and "&" read as written but uncertain; second "Syosset" may be spelled differently ("Syoset").

### 429_0992_L · November, Tuesday 9. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0992/0,0,1066,1559/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
A beautiful Indian Summer
day; in the morning we
played tennis and sat
on the piazza while
I read aloud from "the
Betrothed"; in the after-
-noon I rowed my
sweet Baby round Centre
Island, and we saw
a beautiful sunset on the
water.
```

### 429_0992_R · November, Wednesday 10. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0992/1066,0,927,1559/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We took a lovely long
drive towards Desauris[?]
Island, Locust Valley
and Glen Cove.
I doubt if there
was ever a happier
honeymoon than ours
has been.
```
> place name in line 2 uncertain (reads "Desauris" / "Desausus")

### 429_0993_L · November, Thursday 11. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0993/0,0,1052,1558/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rainy, so we stayed in
the house in the morn-
ing, but went to
drive in the afternoon.
```

### 429_0993_R · November, Friday 12. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0993/1052,0,894,1558/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We took a long walk
"across country" almost
to Syosset; Alice is a
fast walker, but our
progress is not very rapid,
as she stops to pet or to
tease every blessed calf, cow
or horse we meet, and runs
away if she sees a bull
or a turkey cock — the
latter — or still more, a
guinea hen — being an object
of intense interest to her.
Dear old Dare, the Colley,
accompanies us on all our
walks, and in fact every
where, and is a great
companion; lying by the log
fire in the cosy little
parlor at night.
This is the last day of our
honeymoon; how sorry I am!
We have had an ideal time; but
what a happy life we look for-
ward to!
```

### 429_0994_L · November, Saturday 13. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0994/0,0,1045,1569/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
With great regret we
left Oyster Bay and
drove in to New
York, where we found,
however, a perfect
ovation awaiting us.
```

### 429_0994_R · November, Sunday 14. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0994/1045,0,922,1569/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice is going to
join the Presbyterian
Church. I went to
the Newsboys Home in
the evening. Our
rooms are too lovely
for anything; we
are going to live
this winter with
mother.
```

### 429_0995_L · November, Monday 15. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0995/0,0,1064,1602/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the afternoon I
took Alice out in the
park in my cart,
which looks as
swell as possible.
In the evening we
went to the opera;
Hilborne gave Alice
his box.
```

### 429_0995_R · November, Tuesday 16. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0995/1064,0,930,1602/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice received in the
afternoon.
```

### 429_0996_L · November, Wednesday 17. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0996/0,0,1068,1567/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I go to the law-school
at 8.30[?] (walking down
& up) & get back at
4.00; then I drive
Alice in the cart.
```
> time in line 2 partly blotted; read as 8.30

### 429_0996_R · November, Thursday 18. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0996/1068,0,921,1567/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to a very jolly
meeting of the St.
Andrews society; had
a long talk with
Whitelaw Reid.
```

### 429_0997_L · November, Friday 19. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0997/0,0,1065,1568/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Alice up the
Riverside Park; Opera
in evening.
```

### 429_0998_L · November, Tuesday 23. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0998/0,0,1046,1564/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening we are
very apt to have a
great many visitors,
even if we don't go
out; but we always
have some lovely home
time. The little Queen
is just too lovely for
anything; I always, if
possible, drive her out
in the ~~evening~~ afternoon,
or, if she can't go, mother
or one of the sisters.
```

### 429_0998_R · November, Wednesday 24. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0998/1046,0,936,1564/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
A good deal of snow;
so Alice and I
had a lovely sleigh
ride. The law work
is very interesting.
```

### 429_0999_L · November, Thursday 25. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0999/0,0,1068,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Emlen and I gave
the newsboys a big
dinner; about a
hundred came;
We had a great
family dinner in
the evening; Alice
looked like a little
Queen.
```

### 429_0999_R · November, Friday 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:0999/1068,0,931,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to a dinner
given to us by Aunt
Annie; I sat by
Annie Murray; met
a bright fellow,
Archie Alexander.
```

### 429_1000_L · November, Saturday 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1000/0,0,1015,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dear Mr. & Mrs. Lee
and Kitty have
come on. I was
perfectly delighted to
see them. They dined
with us.
```

### 429_1000_R · November, Sunday 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1000/1015,0,905,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The Lees and several
other people took
tea with us.
My darling little
wife has decided to
join the church.
Spoke to the
newsboys, as usual.
```

### 429_1001_L · November, Monday 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1001/0,0,1007,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Villamof, one of the
Russian legation dined
here; an exceedingly clever
man.
I took Kitty out
driving.
```

### 429_1001_R · November, Tuesday 30. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1001/1007,0,938,1532/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-11-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
John & Nannie dined
with us.
```

### 429_1002_L · December, Wednesday 1. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1002/0,0,996,1551/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on a long drive
with Baby; in the
evening went to the
Theatre with the Lees.
```

### 429_1003_L · December, Friday 3. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1003/0,0,1037,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We had a very large
dinner party; I sat be-
tween Mrs. Newbold &
Mrs. Astor; & also talked
a good deal to Mrs.
Cutting & Mrs. Potter.
Enjoyed it very much;
I like Astor & Cutting.
```

### 429_1003_R · December, Saturday 4. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1003/1037,0,875,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove Mother up the
Riverside Park.
I like the law
school work very much.
```

### 429_1004_L · December, Tuesday 7. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1004/0,0,1028,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to a rather stupid
dinner at the Millers.
Sat by a Miss Duncan.
```
> "dinner" partly obscured by blot

### 429_1004_R · December, Wednesday 8. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1004/1028,0,892,1573/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Great ball at our
house for Alice & Corrie;
every living individ-
-ual I know came.
```
> "Corrie" reading uncertain ("Conie"/"Corrie")

### 429_1005_R · December, Friday 10. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1005/1001,0,873,1575/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Preparatory service, before
Alice joins[?] the church.
```

### 429_1006_L · December, Saturday 11. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1006/0,0,999,1540/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
There was a grand reception
at our house; I enjoyed
it very much; as I could
move round and talk
to whomever I wanted
to. I like some of the
New York girls (more
especially the married
women, as Mrs. Astor,
Mrs. Cutting & Mrs.
Newbold) very much,
but I am not very
fond of going out.
However, Alice is uni-
versally and greatly
admired; and she seems
to grow more beautiful
day by day. She
couldn't grow any
sweeter, or lovlier.
She seems very happy;
and oh, how happy
she has made me!
```
> "couldn't" underlined in original

### 429_1006_R · December, Sunday 12. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1006/999,0,881,1540/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I love Sunday; we
breakfast late in our
morning dresses and
jackets. We then go to
church; come home to
dinner; in the after-
noon I walk with
Alice, or if it is bad
weather stay in the
house with her; at tea
West, Hillborne, Uncle
Jimmie and Aunt
Annie appear; then I
go down to the News
Boys; and when I
return to the house, spend
the rest of the evening
with the dear family.
My purest saintliest
little darling has joined
the church. Now we are
one in everything. My
cup of happiness is
almost running over.
```
> "everything" underlined in original; "Hillborne" reading medium

### 429_1007_L · December, Monday 13. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1007/0,0,1039,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to a very
jolly little dinner
at the Iselins.
```

### 429_1007_R · December, Tuesday 14. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1007/1039,0,887,1546/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went to the Mendel-
ssohn Concert, which
was very fine.
```
> 'ssohn' on second line partly obscured; spelling of Mendelssohn split uncertain

### 429_1008_L · December, Wednesday 15. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1008/0,0,1017,1575/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The horse is in
superb spirits, and
the weather is beau-
tiful; I enjoy my
~~rides~~ drives with
Alice most intensely;
especially when we
go up the Riverside
Park by the beautiful
Hudson. When my
sweetest little wife can't
go I always take
the dear little mother.
It is lovely to live
as we are now; it
would be hard to
imagine a pleasanter
home. The little wife
is just the ~~[illegible]~~ winningest[?],
brightest, prettiest little
queen imaginable; and
I perfectly worship her.
How I marvel at
my good luck!
```
> struck word before 'drives' read as 'rides'[?]; word after 'the' in 'just the ...' overwritten/struck, reading uncertain

### 429_1009_L · December, Friday 17. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1009/0,0,1036,1581/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to a great afternoon
reception at the Iselins;
and to another at
the Morans. I like
these "teas"; I never
stay more than two
minutes with any
girl and so don't
get talked out.
```

### 429_1009_R · December, Saturday 18. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1009/1036,0,901,1581/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the evening I
gave a whist club
(at Alices instigation
—she is too sweet to
me in everything);
~~to~~ Doug Robinson,
Harry Haddon, Archie
Russell, Ice Iselin,
Bob Sedgwick, Gasper
Griswold, Willys Betts,
West, Em, and my-
self. We had great
fun; I like all the
fellows (especially
Doug — who may
become a connection)
very much.
I also like young
Bigelow, Trimble, and
the Dodges.
```
> 'everything' underlined; 'Ice', 'Gasper', 'Haddon' readings medium

### 429_1010_R · December, Monday 20. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1010/1057,0,901,1571/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to the Patriarch
Ball which was
great fun; Corinne
was a great belle
and my own
sweet little mistress
had a great deal
of attention. I had
some superb waltzes
with Lucy Tuckerman,
Emily Post, & Miss
Livingstone.
```

### 429_1011_R · December, Wednesday 22. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1011/1047,0,898,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
When the ground is
very slippery I use the
buggy instead of the
cart. I love to take
my sweet little wife
up by the Riverside
Park; it is a beautiful
drive now, with the
snowy palisades showing
in fine relief against
the gray winter skies,
and[?] with the dark, waters
~~ice~~[?] of the Hudson, covered
with ice, at their feet.
```
> first word of line 12 unclear ('an'/'and'); first word of line 13 appears struck ('ice'[?]); comma after 'dark' as written

### 429_1012_R · December, Friday 24. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1012/1021,0,867,1549/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice and I went on
to spend Xmas at
Chestnut Hill; it was
perfectly lovely to see
all the dear people
again, and just as
sweet as ever.
```

### 429_1013_L · December, Saturday 25. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1013/0,0,1044,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Xmas. In the morning
I saw all the little
Lees receive their presents;
then we went coasting;
all the Saltonstalls came
over to dinner.
```

### 429_1013_R · December, Sunday 26. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1013/1044,0,901,1557/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to the dear little
Church in the morning;
coasted in the afternoon
```

### 429_1014_L · December, Monday 27. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1014/0,0,1027,1524/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had great fun coasting;
finally smashed up
against a tree. In
the afternoon I took
Rosy out for a long
sleigh ride; the sleighing
is superb.
```

### 429_1014_R · December, Tuesday 28. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1014/1027,0,853,1524/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went in to Boston
for some things; when
we came out found
that Rosy had the
measles so moved
directly over to the
Saltonstalls who at
once took us in most
kindly.
```

### 429_1015_L · December, Wednesday 29. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1015/0,0,1072,1540/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
It is a perfect shame
about the measles; I
am almost afraid to
go home; it is very
hard on Mrs. Lee;
the Saltonstalls are just
as kind as possible.
```

### 429_1016_L · December, Friday 31. 1880.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_429:1016/0,0,1039,1540/full/0/default.jpg

*1880-12-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Darling Wifie and I
decided to go home
to-morrow.
Thus ends by
far the happiest year
I have ever spent,
for in this year I
have won the fairest
and purest and
sweetest of women for
my wife. I never
conceived it possible
that there could be
such a bright, sunny,
unselfish girl. I can
never express how I
love her; and if I
should love her twice as
much and as tenderly
it would not be
nearly as much as
she deserves; I never
can understand how
I won her!
```

## 1881

116 pages with writing · 52 blocks flagged medium/low

### 430_0010_R · January, Saturday 1. 1881., Sunday 2., Monday 3.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0010/715,0,790,1207/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-01-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice and I came on
to New York
```

*1881-01-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to Newsboys Home
in evening as usual
West and the other
cousins at tea as
usual.
```

*1881-01-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Holiday. Took Alice
a 30 mile sleighride
Sleighing beautiful
We dined with Hill[?]
& Frank & went to
theatre.
```

### 430_0011_L · January, Tuesday 4. 1881., Wednesday 5., Thursday 6.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0011/0,0,726,1213/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Began work at Law-School
Took mother on sleigh ride,
Whist Club at Robinsons.
Always walk up and
down to & from Law School
```

*1881-01-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Alice a long
sleighride. Went to
dinner at James
Roosevelts; then ball
at Delmonicos — Mrs
Kings.
```
> Dots after "James" appear to be smudges/show-through.

*1881-01-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dinner at Astors.
Organizing free
trade club. Every
moment of my
time occupied.
```

```
Two Miss Rathbones
arrived.
Very Pleasant little
party at Aunt Annies.
```

*1881-01-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*

```
Took Alice Rathbone
```

### 430_0012_L · January, Monday 10. 1881., Tuesday 11., Wednesday 12.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0012/0,0,734,1186/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-01-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Mother out
sleigh riding. Dinner
at our house; sat
between Grace Rath-
-bone & Lucy Tuckerman
```

*1881-01-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Fanny Smith
out sleighriding;
Dinner at Delanos;
sat between Mrs. Astor
& Mrs. Drayton.
```

*1881-01-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sleighing superb. Drove
Alice beyond Harlem.
Very jolly theatre party
& supper at Tuckermans.
```

### 430_0012_R · January, Thursday 13. 1881., Friday 14., Saturday 15.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0012/734,0,786,1186/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-01-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Great ball at Astors,
```

*1881-01-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
15 of us started in special
car for Niagara; Robinson,
Bells[?], Beekman, Whitmore, Emlen,
Colgate, Weir[?] & myself; Alice,
Corinne, Bamie, the Miss
Parishes, Turnbull & Robinson.
Greatest kind of fun.
```

*1881-01-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Ideal day; perpetual spree.
Saw falls, rapids, &
whirlpool; took long
sleighrides; ended up
with every kind of
dance in evening. Took
hotel by storm. Every body
so jolly & congenial.
```
> Last line at very bottom edge of page.

```
under Falls; grand sight.
Took night train for
```
