```
walk.
```

*1881-04-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to the Porcellian
Club, and to see
Cotty Peabody.
The country and every-
thing is lovely.
```

*1881-04-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Played with the children,
and loafed round all
day.
```

### 430_0027_R · April, Wednesday 13. 1881., Thursday 14., Friday 15.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0027/746,0,794,1200/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came back to N.Y.
succeeded in arresting
a swindler, had quite
a struggle with him,
```

### 430_0028_L · April, Saturday 16. 1881., Sunday 17., Monday 18.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0028/0,0,752,1210/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Alice a long
drive. Dinner party
in the evening.
```

*1881-04-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Meeting of executive
committee of Young
Mens Republican As.
```

### 430_0028_R · April, Tuesday 19. 1881., Wednesday 20., Thursday 21.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0028/752,0,778,1210/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Had the swindler
indicted by the
Grand Jury,
The weather is
superb for driving.
```

### 430_0029_L · April, Friday 22. 1881., Saturday 23., Sunday 24.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0029/0,0,754,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Corinne acted Private
theatricals with Paul
Tuckerman. Excellent;
Dancing afterwards,
```

*1881-04-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Mamie Saltonstall &
Hattie Lee arrived for
a few days visit.
I am working tolerably
hard at my law.
```

*1881-04-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to the News boys
lodging house for
the last time
till October.
Douglas Robinson is
a perfect old
trump.
```

### 430_0029_R · April, Monday 25. 1881., Tuesday 26., Wednesday 27.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0029/754,0,776,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went down to the
"Newsboys dinner"
which of course was
great fun
```

### 430_0030_L · April, Thursday 28. 1881., Friday 29., Saturday 30.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0030/0,0,750,1184/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-04-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The driving is perfectly
delightful, and my
horse never went
better.
```

*1881-04-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I had the swindler
put in the penetentiary
for six months.
```

### 430_0030_R · May, Sunday 1. 1881., Monday 2., Tuesday 3.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0030/750,0,791,1184/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rosy Lee is staying
with us.
```

*1881-05-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Besides working
pretty well at the
law, I spend most of
my spare time in the
Astor Library, on my
"Naval History".
```

### 430_0031_L · May, Wednesday 4. 1881., Thursday 5., Friday 6.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0031/0,0,728,1161/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Every afternoon I take
the little wife out
driving; generally up
the Riverside, by the
beautiful Hudson,
```

*1881-05-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went round to
the "Primary meet-
ing" as usual, rather
a free fight,
```

*1881-05-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dear funny old
Dick Saltonstall
has come to stay
with us.
```

### 430_0031_R · May, Saturday 7. 1881., Sunday 8., Monday 9.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0031/728,0,762,1161/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Little theatre party
for Rosy, Dick &
Fanny Smith &
Romy[?] Colgate.
```
> 'Romy' uncertain

*1881-05-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Excellent sermon at
Dr. Halls.
We have had no
end of bother packing
up.
```

*1881-05-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to, a meeting
of the the Directors
of the Orthopaedic
Dispensary,
```
> 'the' written twice

### 430_0032_L · May, Tuesday 10. 1881., Wednesday 11., Thursday 12.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0032/0,0,759,1174/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The weather is perfectly
beautiful and the
drives lovely,
```

*1881-05-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
My law school work
is over; my will
drawn up; and my
horse going to the
country.
```

*1881-05-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 3. oclock sailed on
the "Celtic" for England,
Lots of people down to
see us off.
Hurrah! for a sum-
-mer abroad with the
darling little wife.
```

### 430_0032_R · May, Friday 13. 1881., Saturday 14., Sunday 15.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0032/759,0,774,1174/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I am perfectly
well; but poor
little Alice is
sicker than the
devil,
```

*1881-05-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice gets worse
```

*1881-05-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Alice sicker than
ever; I am still
in beautiful health,
```

### 430_0033_L · May, Monday 16. 1881., Tuesday 17., Wednesday 18.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0033/0,0,757,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Confound a European
trip, say I. Alice
wretchedly sick; I
haven't been at all sick
but tired out by
taking care of her.
```

### 430_0033_R · May, Thursday 19. 1881., Friday 20., Saturday 21.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0033/757,0,755,1208/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Landed at Queenstown,
Alice revived at once,
very much. Steamed
up the River to Cork;
most beautiful sail.
At Imperial Hotel - good,
```

### 430_0034_L · May, Sunday 22. 1881., Monday 23., Tuesday 24.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0034/0,0,871,1167/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dear little English Church in
morning. In afternoon heard
bells of Shandon, then
drove to Castle Blarney;
crumbling, ivy covered ruin.
Flowers all out; birds
singing; everything lovely.
```

*1881-05-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took R.R. for Killarney.
Excellent hotel, by
beautiful lake. Jaunting
car to ivy-covered Mul-
cross Abbey & Ross Castle; &
Tore Cascade. Boat home.
Scenery lovely. Hurrah!
```

*1881-05-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We drove to Dunloe's Gap;
Alice on pony, I walked
through it; very wild and
grand; then took boat
down through beautiful upper
lake. Most lovely day.
Poor devil fainting from hunger.
Had him fed & sent to Cork.
```
> last two lines cramped at page foot

### 430_0034_R · May, Wednesday 25. 1881., Thursday 26., Friday 27.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0034/871,0,678,1167/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Took train to Dublin.
Alice is best travelling
companion I have ever
known. By Jove, what luck
I have had! This is the
loveliest trip I ever was[?] on.
```

*1881-05-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went out to Bray,
then to Powerscourt,
and through the beauti-
ful glen called the
Dargles. Lovely scenery.
Are leading most luxur-
ious life.
```

*1881-05-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Boat to Holyhead;
cars to Liverpool.
Are staying at
Aunt Hattie Bullochs.
They are all too kind
for any thing
```

### 430_0035_L · May, Saturday 28. 1881., Sunday 29., Monday 30.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0035/0,0,770,1178/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the afternoon
with Uncle Irvine
& Aunt Ella; they
have a most
lovely little house
```
> very heavy, blotted ink

### 430_0035_R · May, Tuesday 31. 1881., June, Wednesday 1., Thursday 2.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0035/770,0,761,1178/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-05-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to London, at
Queens Hotel. Harry
Shaw, Minot Weld &
Harry Chapin came
round to see me.
```

*1881-06-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to National Gallery
Rembrandt, Rubens, (Rey-
nolds, Gainsborough), Murillo,
Velasquez; I am very fond
of Rembrandt & Murillo.
Turner - idiotic. Zoological Gardens
```

*1881-06-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Miss Jenny Tuckerman
to the Tower. Crown
Jewels & armour, very
interesting. Opera in
evening; Haymarket;
Nilsson.
```

### 430_0036_L · June, Friday 3. 1881., Saturday 4., Sunday 5.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0036/0,0,767,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
We drove out on a [illegible]
coach to the Epsom
races, with Shaw, Weld
Chapin & the Lawrences.
Great fun. Theatre in
evening.
```
> word at end of first line overwritten/blotted, possibly cancelled

*1881-06-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
South Kensington
Museum very interesting.
Gilbert & Sullivan's
new Comic Opera in
evening,
```

*1881-06-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
St. Pauls in morning;
lovely music.
Westminster Abbey in
afternoon; excellent
sermon from Dean
Stanley.
```

### 430_0036_R · June, Monday 6. 1881., Tuesday 7., Wednesday 8.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0036/767,0,768,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
~~Shopping; clothes~~.
Lunch with some of
Douglasses relations;
British Museum (beauti-
ful sculptures); dinner
at Kingsfords.
```

*1881-06-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We went out on
coach to Windsor
castle; rainy but
great fun.
Dined with Chapin,
Weld & Shaw.
```

*1881-06-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Grosvenor Gallery;
shopping; Shaw &
Chapin to dinner
```

### 430_0037_L · June, Thursday 9. 1881., Friday 10., Saturday 11.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0037/0,0,756,1200/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Dorée Gallery; very
fine. Bought pres-
ents. Tried on clothes
```

*1881-06-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Shopping; British
museum.
```

*1881-06-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to Paris
Hotel Bellevue; very
good.
Channel very
smooth
```

### 430_0037_R · June, Sunday 12. 1881., Monday 13., Tuesday 14.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0037/756,0,778,1200/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
[illegible] Church.
Walked to place de
la concorde &
champs Elysees.
```
> first word smudged

*1881-06-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Shopping. Bois de
Boulogne. Column
Vendome, Magnif-
icent Arc de
Triumphe.
```

*1881-06-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Louvre. Venus de Milo.
Rubens and Murillo.
superb.
```

### 430_0038_L · June, Wednesday 15. 1881., Thursday 16., Friday 17.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0038/0,0,766,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Left in morning
train for Venice.
```

### 430_0038_R · June, Saturday 18. 1881., Sunday 19., Monday 20.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0038/766,0,752,1165/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
after some slight
misadventures arrived
at Venice, very dirty
& rather tired. But
Venice is loveliest[?] place
we have been to yet; fairy.
```
> 'very dirty' and 'tired' appear underlined; 'loveliest' uncertain

*1881-06-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Hotel Danielli; old
Palace; lovely rooms
facing San Giorgio
Majore & Liddo. In
evening rowed through
the water-streets.
Beautiful,
```

*1881-06-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
In the morning to see
Titians master piece;
then through palace of
doges. In evening
in gondola to the
Liddo. Then to Piazza
San Marco to hear Music.
```

### 430_0039_L · June, Tuesday 21. 1881., Wednesday 22., Thursday 23.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0039/0,0,783,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
San Marco; in
evening again rowed
through Venice.
```

*1881-06-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to
Milan
```

*1881-06-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
~~Left~~ Saw the beauti-
ful Milan Cathedral.
Came on to Lake Como.
Are staying at the
Villa d'Este.
```
> 'Left' appears struck through

### 430_0039_R · June, Friday 24. 1881., Saturday 25., Sunday 26.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0039/783,0,761,1195/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
The Lake is too beautiful
for anything. Nicholas
Fish & his wife are here,
Alice & I drove to Como,
to see the cathedral. Ex-
cellent hotel.
```

### 430_0040_L · June, Monday 27. 1881., Tuesday 28., Wednesday 29.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0040/0,0,795,1193/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We went by steamer
up to Colico; then took
our carraige & three horses,
driven by Lombardino
(good driver & nice fellow) & came
on to Chiavenna. Are going
in ^our^ carraige for a fortnight
```
> last line runs into Tuesday heading area

*1881-06-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went over Splugen Pass.
Took lunch in a vile
inn at Campo Dolcino.
The pass was very grand.
Are spending night in
Splugen; good german-
swiss inn.
```

*1881-06-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In morning drove through
the wildly magnificent
Via Mala; Lunched at
Thusis & walked up to
Rhaetian Castle. Then through
beautiful gorge to Tiefenkastin,
Torrent roaring under our windows.
```
> 'Tiefenkastin' spelling uncertain

### 430_0040_R · June, Thursday 30. 1881., July, Friday 1., Saturday 2.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0040/795,0,761,1193/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-06-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Crossed the barren
Julier Pass into the
Upper Engadine, and
came on to Samaden.
```

*1881-07-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Took Alice out donkey
riding.
```

*1881-07-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Alice on horseback & I
on foot climbed the
Muottas[?] Mountain; lovely
view; excellent exercise.
```

### 430_0041_L · July, Sunday 3. 1881., Monday 4., Tuesday 5.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0041/0,0,778,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Again took to our carraige
& crossed the Bernina;
lunched at the Hospice[?]. the
snow drifts some feet deep.
Then to Le Prese on the lovely
Lago di Poschiavo, on which I
rowed Alice after dinner.
```

*1881-07-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came up through the Valtellina
to the Baths of Bormio.
Very good accomodations.
Just heard of Gar-
fields assassination; fright-
ful calamity for America
```
> initial of 'America' could be read lower-case

### 430_0041_R · July, Wednesday 6. 1881., Thursday 7., Friday 8.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0041/778,0,752,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Later news say Garfield is
not dead. This means
work in the future for
all men who wish
their country well.
```

*1881-07-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Crossed the snow-covered
Stelvio; very grand.
After we had crossed
summit heavy rains
washed away road, hem-
ming us in, and we turned
back & spent night at Trafoi.
```

*1881-07-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Into the Adige valley. Lunched
by the Haider[?] See; most a-
musing inquisitive german
hostess. Are staying at Hoch-
Finstermunz, by superb gorge.
Lombardino knows no german;
so I have to translate for him.
```

### 430_0042_L · July, Saturday 9. 1881., Sunday 10., Monday 11.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0042/0,0,794,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Drove down through
the beautiful Inn-Thal,
with alternate rain and
sunshine. Are staying
at Imst, at a regular
old gutyrollese[?] Gasthaus.
```
> 'gutyrollese' possibly 'gut tyrollese'

*1881-07-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on through the
beautiful valley of the
Inn to Innsbruck.
```

*1881-07-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to Munich,
after having seen the
sights of Innsbruck.
```

### 430_0042_R · July, Tuesday 12. 1881., Wednesday 13., Thursday 14.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0042/794,0,751,1203/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went through the
Picture Gallery &c.
```

*1881-07-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Came on to Lucerne
```

### 430_0043_L · July, Friday 15. 1881., Saturday 16., Sunday 17.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0043/0,0,775,1194/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Walked up Mount
Pilatus from Alpnacht
and came down it
to Hergeswyl. Excellent
walk; rather tired.
```

*1881-07-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Some how or other we
got it into ^our^ heads that
it was Sunday and
only found out
mistake in the afternoon
```

*1881-07-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Poor sermon at the
English Church.
```

### 430_0043_R · July, Monday 18. 1881., Tuesday 19., Wednesday 20.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0043/775,0,738,1194/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Ascended the Rigi
Kulm and saw a
most glorious sunset.
```

*1881-07-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Arose at three to
see a beautiful
sunrise and then
came down to
Lucerne.
```

*1881-07-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We drove over the
Brünig Pass to
Interlaken.
```

### 430_0044_L · July, Thursday 21. 1881., Friday 22., Saturday 23.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0044/0,0,787,1169/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Drove to Grindelwald
in the rain; are
staying at the
Schwartzer Adler.
```

*1881-07-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Alice on horseback and
I on foot went to
the Rigi[?]-Grindelwald
and back,
```
> first element of place name uncertain

### 430_0044_R · July, Sunday 24. 1881., Monday 25., Tuesday 26.
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0044/787,0,730,1169/full/0/default.jpg

*1881-07-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
At 8.15 a.m. left for the
Jungfrau, with the guides
Peter Egger and Hans
Baumann - both excellent;
Reached the Bergli Hütte
at 4.45, and there spent night.
```

*1881-07-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Started off at 3.15; very
windy and somewhat
cloudy. Over the Mönchjoch,
Jungfraufirn and Roththal sattel
to the top at 8.55. Great
fun coming down. Grindel-
wald at 6.p.m. Rather tired
```

26 pages with writing · 6 blocks flagged medium/low

- **430_0107_R** (endpaper) — low: Faint ink on stained back cover/endpaper; first word may be 'See'; ship name uncertain.

### 430_0086_R · JANUARY 1 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0086/929,0,1081,1689/full/0/default.jpg

*1882-01-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
New York. Alice is at
Montreal with the
girls on their great party;
so I am keeping
house with Mother, while
we [illegible] it alone[?].
Aunt Annie and
Uncle Jimmie[?], Aunt
Ella and Uncle
[illegible], Aunt Susie[?]
& Uncle Hill, took
tea with us.
```
> Heavy show-through from Jan 2 overlies lines 2-10; line 6 and names in lines 7 and 9-10 uncertain.

```
to begin my duties
in the legislature.
Attended the

ated for speaker;
```

```
Ballotted for speaker
There are 61 Republi-
cans in the house; there

of these 7 are Tammany
Hall men, who
are "striking" for
their own candidate,
and so a deadlock

```

```
Went down to New
York. Katie Lowell,
and dear little
Rosy are passing a

My book seems
to be getting on

```

### 430_0090_R · JANUARY 9 Monday

```
I very much like my
fellow members as

those from the country;
the city members are

stamp, especially the
democrats, who are
mainly Irishmen
The wrangling goes
on with great
bitterness between

Anti tammany
factions, while we
Republicans can
```

## 1883

71 pages with writing · 61 blocks flagged medium/low

- **430_0109_R** (printed-matter) — low: Pencil marks/boxes around February 5, 12 and 19 (Mondays) and small ticks elsewhere in calendar; no words.
- **430_0126_R** (1883-09-02) — low: Small faint writing left of the printed month.
- **430_0132_R** (1883-09-14) — low: heavily smudged/blotted ink with dense show-through; lower half especially uncertain
- **430_0145_S** (memoranda) — low: column of figures down right edge, partly overwritten
- **430_0146_L** (cash-account) — low: dates written in the left column; ditto marks (") repeat month and year; short remarks in the PAID/RECEIVED area at right. Rows correspond line-for-line with the payee list on the facing page 430_0146_R. Page very dark on film
- **430_0146_R** (cash-account) — low: amounts in RECEIVED column; total below a rule. Numerous figures overlaid by show-through and smudging; J. K. Gracie 4.20 line has faint figures '139' to the left, possibly struck or show-through; total compares with 'Carried Over 7992.41' on 430_0147_R
- **430_0147_L** (cash-account) — low: dates in left columns with ditto marks; rows correspond to payee list on facing page 430_0147_R. Page dark with dense mirrored show-through of the facing account
- **430_0149_S** (cover) — low: very faint column of figures, a rule drawn below the ninth line; a second faint column of figures at upper right is unreadable

### 430_0112_R · JANUARY 1 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0112/949,0,1086,1685/full/0/default.jpg

* · inscription · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Theodore Roosevelt
```

*1883-01-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Albany. I received the
complimentary ^Republican^ nomination for
Speaker of the Assembly. The
Stalwarts made a strong fight
against me; but the Independents
stood firm, and we beat them
two to one. My strength lay among
the country Republicans, all of them
native Americans, and for the
most part farmers or storekeepers
or small lawyers; they are all
shrewd, kindly, honest men,
with whom I get on admirably.
Hunt, a keen, melancholy, humorous
yankee, and O'Neil, a bright,
straightforward backwoodsman,
were my two main backers.
All of the 43 Republicans are
native Americans, with the
exception of two who were born
in Germany. Howe[?], my colleague
from New York is a very good
man; a gentleman of broad culture.
```
> Heavy show-through; colleague surname uncertain.

### 430_0113_L · JANUARY 2 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0113/0,0,1030,1681/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The Legislature organized, and
the Democratic majority elected
as speaker Chapin of Brooklyn,
by all odds the ablest man
on either side of the House;
~~but ah~~ a scholar and a
gentleman, but a thoroughly
cold and selfish man. The
Democrats seem to have
good material in their
ranks, though most of
the city members are
corrupt and ignorant
Irish Catholics. Chapin
appointed a committee
on privilege and elections,
of which I am one, to
hear the cases of the
contestants[?] for seats[?].
The committee went
down to New York.
```
> Very heavy show-through; two faint lines at foot are mirrored show-through of facing page, not transcribed.

### 430_0113_R · JANUARY 3 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0113/1030,0,1018,1681/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Back again in my own
lovely little home, with
the sweetest and prettiest
of all little wives —
my own sunny, darling.
I can imagine nothing
more happy in life than
~~in~~ an evening spent in
my cozy little sitting
room, before a bright
fire of soft coal, my
books all around me,
and playing backgam-
mon with my own
dainty mistress.
```

### 430_0114_L · JANUARY 4 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0114/0,0,1025,1636/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Today we heard the
evidence in the case
of the contested election
between Sprague and Blis[?]
The former was counted
out beyond a shadow
of doubt.
```
> Second name may be cut off at right edge.

### 430_0114_R · JANUARY 5 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0114/1025,0,996,1636/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went to drill my
company (B, of the 8th Regi-
ment) as usual.
```

### 430_0115_L · JANUARY 6 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0115/0,0,989,1607/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
All through to day, as
also yesterday, the com-
mittee, has been wading
through the mass of perjury
involved in the Brooklyn
contested election cases.
```

### 430_0115_R · JANUARY 7 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0115/989,0,1006,1607/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Church in the morning at
Dr. Hall. the family dinner
was at our house; in the
evening we took tea with
Motherling.
```
> Punctuation after "Hall" unclear (period or comma).

### 430_0116_L · JANUARY 8 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0116/0,0,992,1666/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Committee sat all day;
In evening passed my
examination for Captain
in the 8th.
```

### 430_0116_R · JANUARY 9 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0116/992,0,1044,1666/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Went back to Albany.
Chapin announced his
committees, which seem
to very good.
Crane, of my New York
colleagues, is honest but
light weight; Howe is
marvellously conceited
and noisy. I think he
will prove a nuisance.
Old "[illegible]" is "a very
fine fellow, with a
grand head, his hair
and beard both snow
white.
Hunt, O'Neil, Howe and
myself sit together — a
pleasant quartette.
```
> Nickname in quotes reads roughly "Clamfand"/"Cleanfand"; not resolved. Quote marks around "a very" uncertain. "to very good" as written (no "be").

### 430_0117_L · JANUARY 10 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0117/0,0,996,1632/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-01-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Had another fight with
the stalwarts in the caucus
to nominate a regent,
and beat them again;
my nominee, Matthew
Hale, receiving the some-
what empty honor.
On the floor of the house
I opened the session by
a brush with Murphy
& "Tim" Campbell, and
got the better of these
"renowned leaders."
```
> Show-through heavy; closing quote of last line uncertain.

### 430_0118_L · AUGUST 18 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0118/0,0,1035,1669/full/0/default.jpg

* · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
16th
1884
```
> "16th" written over/beside struck printed "18"; "1884" written after "AUGUST".

*1883-08-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Have been spending a couple
of weeks on my ranche, on
the Little Missouri; I now
intend starting out for
a two months trip overland
to the Bighorn Mountains.
My companions are my
foreman, William Merrifield,
who is to hunt with me, and
an old french halfbreed
named Lebo, who is to drive
the team and to cook &c.
I take a light "prairie schooner"
with a canvas cover, drawn by
two stout horses, to carry our
goods, and four riding ponies
for Merrifield and myself to ride.
One of these is Blackie, a stout,
swift rather vicious pony; Whitey[?],
very enduring; Brownie, willing
and swift, but very nervous; and
Roachie, quiet and rather
small. I cary a buckskin suit,
```
> Entry written 16 August 1884 (per TR's heading); continues on next page.

### 430_0118_R · AUGUST 19 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0118/1035,0,986,1669/full/0/default.jpg

* · heading · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Trip from Little Missouri to the
Bighorn Mts.
```

* · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1884
17
```
> "1884" after printed "AUGUST"; "17" below struck printed day number.

*1883-08-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
^sealskin^ ~~leather~~ chaparajos, ^coon^ ~~fur~~ skin overcoat,
otter fur robe to sleep in (buttoning
into a bag form), oil slicker, ^boots^
and moccasins, sombrero, overalls,
jersey, 2 flannel shirts, 3 suits of
light, 3 of heavy underclothing,
~~[illegible]~~ heavy socks, plenty of handkerchiefs,
soap, towels, washing & shaving things
rubber blankets; flour, bacon, beans
and coffee, sugar & salt. A little
brandy and cholera mixture.
My battery consists of a long
.45 Colt revolver, 150 cartridges.
a No. 10 choke bore, 300
cartridges, shotgun; a 45-75
Winchester repeater, with 1000
cartridges; a 40-90 Sharps,
150 cartridges; a 50-150
double barrelled Webley express,
100 cartridges.
```
> Continues list begun on previous page (16 Aug 1884 entry); dated 17 [Aug 1884] by TR. Struck words under insertions read as "leather" and "fur" (uncertain); "40" in "40-90" appears overwritten.

### 430_0119_L · AUGUST 20 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0119/0,0,1075,1680/full/0/default.jpg

* · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
18
```
> Written beside struck printed day number "20"; refers to 18 Aug 1884.

*1883-08-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Made late start.
Left wagon on
prairie & Merrif[ield]
& I rode on
and spent
night at
Langs. Out on
prairie got
caught in
driving hail
storm; sheltered
in a washout.
Wind so strong
could hardly
stand; ponies would
not face it. Our
"slickers" and chaps
kept us perfectly dry,
in spite of the driving
rain. The Winchester
45-75 shoots beautifully.
```
> 1884 entry. Name at right edge cut off by binding/film. Very heavy show-through in lower half.

### 430_0119_R · AUGUST 21 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0119/1075,0,985,1680/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-21 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Heavy rain storm
all day. Left
Langs after break-
fast; stopped to
kill a few mallard
flappers and white
belly grouse down
among ^a^ lot of
slews, ^with rifle^ no lunch;
overtook wagon at
dusk, at Lake Station
on old Keogh Trail.
Heavy rain all
night; no water except
rain water from
rubber blankets. Slept
in wagon. Made
fire of logs of old
station house; good
supper ~~[illegible]~~ fried
duck.
```
> 1884 entry (19 Aug 1884 by sequence; TR wrote no date here). Small dash before printed "21" may be a stroke.

*1883-08-21 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
4 grouse 5 duck
```

### 430_0120_L · AUGUST 22 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0120/0,0,1041,1631/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-22 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Lowering and cloud[y]
very heavy wheeling.
M. & I rode off; skirting
some deep, branching
brush bottomed coolies
we spied a white tail
buck feeding; I shot
him through both
shoulders at a hundred
yards; good horns, but
still in velvet. Also
2 sage hens; all with
Winchester. Camped at
extremely muddy pool in
dried creek, early (at 3 in
afternoon); we needed to dry our
things; first place to water our
horses; only made 12 miles, through
the deep, heavy, water soaked soil.
Went through rolling prairie, [illegible]
and then in the abruptly ^broken^ and fantas-
tically shaped Bad Lands. No lun[ch][?]
```
> 1884 entry (no date written by TR). Words at right edge partly lost in binding. Heavy show-through.

*1883-08-22 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 white tail buck. 2 sage grouse
```

### 430_0120_R · AUGUST 23 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0120/1041,0,982,1631/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Frost in night. Day clear,
cool and windy; wheeling
much better. Early in morning
a great herd of 1500 beef steers
passed us; they had been bedded
near us, and all night we
had heard the cowboys singing
and calling as they circled
slowly round the herd. Our dog,
a greyhound, died from wolf poison.
We made about 25 miles
through broken prairie; are
camped by what I suppose
to be the head waters of O'Fallon
Creek. Saw no living game;
but hundreds of buffalo
skulls and old carcasses.
Lebo is a chatty, tough old plains-
man, full of expedients, and ready
with both wits and hands. Merrifield
is a good looking fellow, who shoots
and rides beautifully; a reckless,
self confident man.
```
> 1884 entry. Show-through heavy in middle lines.

### 430_0121_L · AUGUST 24 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0121/0,0,1061,1632/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In the middle of the night
we had a short but
violent storm of wind
and rain, which, as we
have no tent, wet us pretty
well; quite cold in morning.
Wheeling good; travelled some
20 miles southwest, on a
cattle trail. M. & I rode off
among some foot hills of
a large range of buttes, where
I missed two first class
shots at blacktail bucks
— the only game we saw.
Camped for the night near a
most beautiful natural
park; green sward thickly
dotted over with ~~f~~ small
steep, isolated sand cliffs, of
most fantastic shape, crowned
with pines.
```
> 1884 entry. Stray letter before "small" read as struck "f" (uncertain).

### 430_0121_R · AUGUST 25 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0121/1061,0,957,1632/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Crossed the Little Beaver.
Travelled over great
rolling plains, bedded[?]
with the short, brown
grass; saw several herds
of cattle, which have
now taken the place
of the buffalo, whose
carcasses can be seen
every where; this is already
well occupied as a cattle
country. M. & I. rode off
among some grassy,
pine clad Chalk Buttes;
I missed a long shot
at a blacktail doe;
M. missed two shots.
Knocked off the heads
of two young sage
grouse for supper; good
eating. Camped in Bad Lands
of Powder River. Coyotes howling.
```
> 1884 entry. "bedded" is written over another word; reading uncertain.

*1883-08-25 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 sage grouse.
```

### 430_0123_L · AUGUST 26 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0123/0,0,1009,1651/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Spent the morning travelling
down a creek through the
Powder River Bad Lands;
soon after lunch struck
the Powder, and went on
till dusk through great
sage brush bottoms; made
a dry camp; poor food
for ponies; no sign of
game. There are quite
a number of cattle
ranches along this river.
```
> 1884 entry.

### 430_0123_R · AUGUST 27 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0123/1009,0,1012,1651/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Travelled up the Powder
till we struck the
mouth of the Little
Powder, when we camped,
just after sunset. Took the
shot gun and killed, for
the pot six white bellies,
2 doves and 2 teal; M.
missed a snap shot at
a cougar in a thicket.
He and I made an unsucces-
ful hunt through the
bottoms after white tail
deer; in one dense swamp
or "cane brake" we got very
nearly mired; and in
~~going~~ clambering among
some bad lands, very broken,
M's pony turned two complete
somersaults down a sandy
cliff some thirty feet high.
Saw beaver & whip poor wills.
```
> 1884 entry. Word before "clambering" appears struck/overwritten; reading uncertain.

*1883-08-27 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
6 sharp tail grouse 2 teal 2 dove.
```

### 430_0124_L · AUGUST 28 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0124/0,0,1014,1677/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Made a short days
journey; not over 15 miles
In morning rode off
with M. after chickens;
shot 8. Met a cowboy
with a fine young
horse, which I purchased
for a hundred dollars.
Stopped over night by the
N bar ranche; thirty
men and about three
hundred horses preparing
to start on the beef
round up tomorrow.
Cowboys are a jolly
set; picturesque, with
broad hats, loosely knotted
neckerchiefs, flannel shirts,
leather chaparajos.
```
> 1884 entry.

*1883-08-28 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
8 sharp tail grouse,
```

### 430_0124_R · AUGUST 29 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0124/1014,0,1006,1677/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Country prettier; bottoms
narrower hemmed in
by bold bluffs, with
pines scattered over them.
Shot a dozen sage
hens and prairie
chicken with shot
gun; and late in
evening while riding
through a dense
swamp caught sight
of a yearling white
tail buck, standing
in a marshy opening;
he was fifty yards
off, and did not
see me; kneeling I
shot him through the
heart.
```
> 1884 entry.

*1883-08-29 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 white tail buck. 10 Sage
Grouse. 2 sharp tail grouse
```

### 430_0125_L · AUGUST 30 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0125/0,0,991,1659/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Travelled up the Powder;
crossed the river nine
times; once the wagon
nearly stuck in a quick-
sand; camped a little
before coming to the
mouth of Clear Creek.
Country very pretty.
```
> 1884 entry.

### 430_0125_R · AUGUST 31 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0125/991,0,994,1659/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-08-31 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Travelled a short distance
up Clear Creek. Country
very barren; broken, rolling,
treeless, and covered with
sage brush. Crossings steep
and difficult; road crossed
by deep washouts; three times
the wagon stuck, and we
had to help out the team by
ropes hitched to the saddle trees
of the riding horses. Team nearly
done out.
In afternoon M. & I rode out
through the coolies. Roused three
blacktail bucks; shot three times with
winchester at them running, going close
but a little high, finally stopping
them; two stood side by side; taking
careful aim I broke the backs
of both with one bullet; distance
431 paces (near 400 yards) actual walking ^at^[?]
Best shot I ever made. Kept[?] [illegible].
```
> 1884 entry. "three" before "times" overwritten. Small "at"(?) written below right end of 431-paces line. End of last line faint.

*1883-08-31 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2 blacktail buck.
```

### 430_0126_L · SEPTEMBER 1 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0126/0,0,1005,1678/full/0/default.jpg

* · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Aug 30th
```
> Written after printed "SEPTEMBER", which TR struck through.

*1883-09-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Made some 20 miles up
Clear Creek; travelling
a little better, but country
unchanged; large numbers
of cattle (have crossed off[?] from
Montana into Wyoming);
passed a camp of Cheyennes.
Have had a bath in
the river every day
lately.
```
> Entry for 30 Aug 1884 per TR's heading.

### 430_0126_R · SEPTEMBER 2 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0126/1005,0,980,1678/full/0/default.jpg

```
Aug 31st
```

```
[illegible] post[?]
```

*1883-09-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Kept on up Clear Creek;
camped about six miles
~~before~~ N.W.[?] of the little
town of Buffalo (before reaching
it). Country still treeless,
broken & rolling; many
ranches, some well
built. Have for last
three days been in
sight of the snow streaked
Bighorn Mts. Made a
good shot with the
rifle at a jackrabbit,
cutting him nearly in
two at about 75 yds.
```
> Entry for 31 Aug 1884 per TR's heading. Compass abbreviation after struck "before" unclear; an "E" appears written above it.

*1883-09-02 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
jack rabbit
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0127/0,0,1005,1718/full/0/default.jpg

```
1
```

*1883-09-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Reached Buffalo, Wyoming,
quite a frontier town.
```
> Entry for 1 Sep 1884 per TR's date.

### 430_0127_R · SEPTEMBER 4 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0127/1005,0,998,1718/full/0/default.jpg

* · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2
```
> Written after struck printed day number.

*1883-09-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went on to the sawmill
on Crazy Woman creek,
Here we leave our
wagon and go in on
pack ponies. Have
seven horses, 3 to ride,
3 to pack, 1 spare. Carry
wagon sheet for tent;
300 cartridges for Winchester.
```
> Entry for 2 Sep 1884. Last line in fainter ink.

### 430_0128_L · SEPTEMBER 5 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0128/0,0,1017,1696/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-05 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
The ponies proved fractious
and the trail blind.
so did not get off till
10; marched steadily
till ~~[illegible]~~ 5.30; slow progress
as pack animals and
loose ponies proved hard
to drive. Camped on
willow creek. Already
pretty cold. Shot 2 blue
grouse from a tree with[?]
the rifle, for supper. Still
have a haunch of venison[?]
left from the two bucks.
Ascent to the Bighorn[?]
Plateau was very steep.
Ice formed at night
```

*1883-09-05 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 blue grouse
```

### 430_0128_R · SEPTEMBER 6 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0128/1017,0,1003,1696/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-06 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Only made about six
miles over pretty rough
trail; camped by a
rushing mountain torrent
"Park" country; broken,
steep and rolling; wooded
with open glades; small
lakes & clear streams.
M. & I went out in
afternoon on foot, through
cedar wooded steep
hills. We separated; I
found a[?] fresh tracks
of a band of elk, and
followed them up
till I came on them;
killed an old cow
and a bull calf as
they ran off through
the woods, M after wards[?]
killed one. No bull in
the band.
```
> 'a' in 'found a fresh tracks' has a mark over it, possibly struck

*1883-09-06 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2 elk
```

### 430_0129_L · SEPTEMBER 7 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0129/0,0,1018,1629/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-07 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Ponies nearly done up,
so made a camp to
stretch hides, dry meat
&c.
An old bull elk,
whistling & calling,
almost came into camp
last night; today I
went off alone on
foot to trail him
up; was absent all
day but never saw
him; shot a rabbit
and a grouse on
the way home. Rubber[?]
wagon sheet makes
pretty good tents[?].
```

*1883-09-07 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 red[?] rabbit; 1 blue grouse
```

### 430_0129_R · SEPTEMBER 8 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0129/1018,0,997,1629/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-08 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Stormed all night; when
we waked up snow
covered the ground, bending
down the willow bushes
and loading the tall
pines; rained and
sleeted all day; we
stayed in tent with
roaring log fire in
front of it. In after-
noon took a three hours
brisk ride on California
through the mist and
rain.
Our camp is in a
narrow valley, by a brook
steep hills, some covered
with pine and spruce
others bare[?] with grass
and sagebrush, broken
by ravines[?] and and a
few steep cliffs, come
down round[?] us.
```
> lower lines blotted and overlaid by heavy show-through

### 430_0130_L · SEPTEMBER 9 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0130/0,0,1017,1677/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-09 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
M. & I, on Blackie and
Spleitear[?] (the two best hunt-
ing ponies), started off
early in the morning
and were gone till
dusk. We rode through
awful ground. While
hunting through a great
burnt slash on foot
we struck elk tracks;
following them up we
found three bulls[?]; we
wounded all three (I
two & M. one) and went
after them helter skelter
running, tumbling, jump-
ing, falling and shouting;
one got away; while
following it I ran
across and killed a
black tail doe. One
head had [illegible] good horns[?]
```
> lower lines smudged

*1883-09-09 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2 elk 1 black tail deer
```

### 430_0130_R · SEPTEMBER 10 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0130/1017,0,1002,1677/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-10 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
I rode out alone; saw
a doe and two fawns
which I did not
touch, as we have
more than enough
meat; coming home
struck a covey of grouse
and killed 12, as
they are good eating
for a change.
Meanwhile M.
saw a black bear
up a tree and
shot him; by bad
luck I was not
with him.
```

*1883-09-10 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
12 ^blue^ grouse
```

### 430_0131_L · SEPTEMBER 11 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0131/0,0,1014,1664/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-11 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Broke camp and went
about six miles farther,
to a branch of the
Ten Sleep; better camp.
Snowed last night;
all this afternoon
it snowed at inter-
vals, so that the
country has quite a
wintry look; in the
moonlight at night
the scenery is very
beautiful.
The bear meat
is very good eating; I
have never been in
better health. California
is a superb riding
horse; he is not so
good as[?] a hunting
pony as Blackie or
Spleitear[?].
```
> mark before 'a hunting' may be a struck word

### 430_0131_R · SEPTEMBER 12 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0131/1014,0,1017,1664/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-12 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
M. & I took a long tramp
through the woods
stillhunting, but
without success; we
saw nothing except
two or three deer tracks.
Snow lies on the
ground through the
woods, and the small
pools or tarns are all
frozen over.
These pine and
spruce forests, with
their sights & sounds
or more often, absence
of sound, remind me
of the great woods of
Maine and the Adiron-
dacks; they have a
weird, melancholy
fascination for me.
```

### 430_0132_L · SEPTEMBER 13 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0132/0,0,982,1676/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-13 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
M. & I rode over to find
the Lake; we found it,
and caught about
fifty trout, but only
by ~~after~~ four hours
riding and six
hours as hard walking
as I ever had. Pretty
tired, and glad to
get back to camp
and a first class
trout supper
```
> struck word after 'by' read as 'after', uncertain

### 430_0132_R · SEPTEMBER 14 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0132/982,0,1013,1676/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-14 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · low*
```
No game, so shifted
camp to about six miles
farther down towards
the foot hills, in a
narrow valley with
steep, wooded sides.
M. & I started off on
foot at three p.m.,
and hunted till
nightfall all through
the woods and hills.
I got a shot at a
great bull elk with
magnificent[?] antlers
in the wood[?], facing
me 75 yards (81 paces
off, killing[?] him
very neatly[?]. Knocked
off the heads of
two grouse for supper.
```
> heavily smudged/blotted ink with dense show-through; lower half especially uncertain

*1883-09-14 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 elk 2 grouse.
```

### 430_0133_L · SEPTEMBER 15 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0133/0,0,1012,1640/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-15 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In morning took a
four hours tramp in
moccasins (I wear
them walking because
they make no noise);
shot a spike black
tail buck at forty
yards through the
shoulders.
In afternoon M.
& I went out, on
foot, through the woods;
found a grizzly bear
track in a dense
forest; followed the
trail noiselessly[?]
up, and found him
in his bed; I shot
him through the
brain at 25 feet
distance
```

*1883-09-15 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
1 bear 1 blacktail deer
```

### 430_0133_R · SEPTEMBER 16 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0133/1012,0,1011,1640/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-16 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In morning skinned
the grizzly.
In afternoon M. &
I rode out on horseback.
Down to canyon;
superb scenery. Emptied
the winchester at a
blacktail buck; shot very
badly; killed him at
sixth shot. While skinning
him (near dusk) saw
a female grizzly and her
nearly full grown cub;
we ran after them on
foot about a mile, over
awful ground; I mortally
wounded the old one; she
turned to bay and M,
who was ahead, finished
her; he ^mortally^ wounded the cub;
I cut it off and finished
it, the ball going clean through ^him from end to end^
```
> last line: 'him from end to end' written above 'going clean through' as an interlinear addition

*1883-09-16 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
2 bears 1 blacktail buck.
```

### 430_0134_L · SEPTEMBER 17 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0134/0,0,990,1656/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-17 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
In morning brought in
the bears.
In afternoon M. &
I went out on foot
again with moccasins
as usual. We found
a big grizzly at
the carcass of the
bull elk; M. has not
yet got one solely[?]
to[?] his own gun,
so I made him
shoot it.
Shot four bluegrouse
for supper
```
> 'solely' and following word blotted

*1883-09-17 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
4 blue grouse
```

### 430_0134_R · SEPTEMBER 18 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0134/990,0,1007,1656/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-18 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
We all three started
out on foot to beat
through the woods for
bear; walked all day,
but saw no bear. Lebo,
however, started up a
bull elk, which he
fired at and missed;
it ran across my
way and stood
behind some trees; I
could only see part
of its flank, which
I fired into; away
it started, and, running
at top speed in my
light moccasins, I
got another shot
and broke his
back.
```

*1883-09-18 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1 elk.
```

Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0135/0,0,1016,1647/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-19 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Broke camp and
started off the moun-
tain. Three pack
ponies heavily laden
with hides and
horns; progress [illegible]
Camped on Willow Creek.
```

### 430_0135_R · SEPTEMBER 20 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0135/1016,0,994,1647/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-20 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Reached the saw mill
where we had left
the wagon; California
[illegible] done up, having

```

```
Went (with wagon again)
to Fort McKinney; Dined
at Fort.
```

```
Fair days journey, camped
```


### 430_0137_L · SEPTEMBER 23 Sunday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0137/0,0,1002,1630/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-23 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Long days journey;
Camped where we lunched[?]
on Aug 30th, under the[?]
great Balm tree
```

### 430_0137_R · SEPTEMBER 24 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0137/1002,0,992,1630/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-24 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Went down Clear Creek till
about six miles from
Powder River; then went
into camp under two
huge cottonwood trees, as
our bear skins are
rapidly spoiling and
need to be fleshed and
dried. Horses are not in
good condition.
```

### 430_0138_L · SEPTEMBER 25 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0138/0,0,1039,1667/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-25 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Bear skins ~~[illegible]~~ drying;
so did not break camp;
hunted to north of
~~river~~ ^creek^; no game.
```
> struck word in line 1 unreadable; interlinear 'creek' reading uncertain

### 430_0138_R · SEPTEMBER 26 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0138/1039,0,991,1667/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-26 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Skins not yet dry; hunted
to south side of creek; no
big game; took shot gun
and killed two teal
and five chickens along
(the river) edge.
```
> last line partly obscured by show-through; parentheses uncertain

*1883-09-26 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
2 teal 5 sharptail grouse
```

### 430_0139_L · SEPTEMBER 27 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0139/0,0,1032,1689/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-27 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Made long days journey;
camped under some
large cottonwoods in
a bend of the Powder
Out of fresh meat,
so went out shooting;
shot awfully; missed
three shots at ~~blacktail~~
whitetail; M. also
missed two.
```

### 430_0139_R · SEPTEMBER 28 Friday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0139/1032,0,961,1689/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-28 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Rained all night; drew
wagon sheet over us, but
pretty wet. Made good
days journey; camped
by dead Cottonwood
some four miles above the
N bar ranche. M. shot
a white tail doe.
```

### 430_0140_L · SEPTEMBER 29 Saturday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0140/0,0,998,1630/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-09-29 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Made pasture[?] by SH ranche;
Rained at night.
```

```
28
```

*1883-09-30 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*

```
Went down Powder
camping near "Powderville"
shot two bluewinged
teal with rifle, and
eight teal and two

and left from shot
gun
```

```
12 ducks
```

### 430_0141_L · OCTOBER 1 Monday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0141/0,0,1015,1655/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-10-01 · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Sept, 29th
```
> printed 'OCTOBER' heading struck through in ink and re-dated by TR

*1883-10-01 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Cold ~~[illegible]~~ rain in night,
lasting all day; poor
feed for ponies; walked
till night fall when
we camped near
White Clay Buttes;
water bad, from mud
hole; team nearly
done out.
```

### 430_0141_R · OCTOBER 2 Tuesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0141/1015,0,988,1655/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-10-02 · heading · top · ink · hand TR · high*
```
sept 30th
```
> printed 'OCTOBER 2 Tuesday' struck through in ink; TR's date written to its left

*1883-10-02 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Shot 7 sharptail grouse;
very heavy frost last
night. Hard days journey;
cold and windy; roads
heavy; one of the
team played out and
we had to put
Spleit-ear in the
harness. It was after
dark when we went
into camp among
the fantastic "medicine
buttes"; a most picturesque
camp, blazing pitch
pine fire, in a ~~angle~~
angle of a sandstone
cliff, under pine trees.
```

*1883-10-02 · tally · bottom · ink · hand TR · high*
```
7 sharptail grouse
```

### 430_0142_L · OCTOBER 3 Wednesday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0142/0,0,1035,1650/full/0/default.jpg

*1883-10-03 · heading · over-printed-title · ink · hand TR · high*
```
1
```
> TR's numeral '1' written over the printed day number (3), re-dating the page

*1883-10-03 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · high*
```
Furious hurricane blowing,
with driving rain squalls.
Afraid to venture on prairie
so stayed in camp all
day.
```

### 430_0142_R · OCTOBER 4 Thursday
Scan: https://tile.loc.gov/image-services/iiif/service:mss:mss38299:mss38299_430:0142/1035,0,981,1650/full/0/default.jpg

```
2
```
> TR's numeral '2' written over the printed day number (4), re-dating the page

*1883-10-04 · entry · body · ink · hand TR · medium*
```
Still raining; beds pretty wet;
horses had strayed; ~~[illegible]~~
could not find them
till noon; then one
of the team was in a
mud hole; worked two
hours to get her out;
she could hardly
stand[?]; so had to stay
in camp.
```

```
little rain; camped

chickens.
```

```
Clear and cold; drove
to the Muddy Creek; on

rabbit and two[?] sharptail
```
